  - **Versatility rises over a dynasty and then plateaus; the fresh-world calibration ships.** Rostered share with any second position: fresh 35.4%, then 45.5 / 51.5 / 53.6 / 54.7 / 54.9 / 54.8 at seasons 5/10/15/20/25/30 — flat from season 20, an equilibrium rather than a ratchet. Cause is `growthDamping` compressing a player's strongest skills, which flattens his profile; note a *uniform* rating gain cancels exactly, since every OVR weight row sums to 100. **Not the free-agent tail** — that was the first hypothesis and it is wrong, rostered squads drift the same. Recalibrating on the season-15 plateau was tried and **rejected**: it leaves a new save at 9.3% (3% of wingers), i.e. no utility players in the seasons a new player actually sees.
  - **Candidates are ranked by `ovrAtSlot`, not `p.ovr`, and this is a correctness requirement rather than a refinement.** `selectXI`/`bestFit` break fit-rank ties by rating; using a player's own-position OVR compares a winger's *winger* rating against a striker's *striker* rating and then plays the winner up front. Measured, that cost team `finishing` 0.4736 → 0.4576 and goals/game 2.60 → 2.53, because versatile wingers were taking striker slots on a rating they don't carry into them. Ranking by his rating **at the slot he'd fill** restores it (finishing 0.4652) and is the only comparison that means anything. Same reasoning applies to any future ranking of a player for a slot he doesn't list.
  - **The rate is a difficulty lever, not decoration.** A secondary waives the familiarity penalty, so letting it run high dissolves the positional discipline slot-aware composites exist to enforce. That is the number to watch if these constants are retuned.
- **The XI is chosen on `slotValue`, the same number the sim scores it by (`core/players/positions.ts`, `selectXI`, 2026-08-27).** `slotValue(p, slot)` = `ovrAtSlot(p, slot) − familiarityPenalty(slot, p.pos, secondaryPositions(p))`. **Three places must agree on it** — the composite rollup (how well the XI plays), `benchValueAt`/`worthSub` (whether a sub is worth making) and now `selectXI`/`bestFit`/`chooseBestFormation` (who starts). Only the first two were on one number; the picker ranked **fit tier strictly above rating**, so *any* natural full-back beat *every* non-full-back for a full-back slot however wide the gap. The sim then charged a mere `POSITION_ADJACENT_PENALTY` (6) for the alternative it had refused, i.e. the picker optimized a number it was not scored on. Measured on a season-8 world, tier-1 AI clubs: **16.9% of all starting slots** across **77% of clubs** held a man a bench player was worth more than, mean **15.1 rating points** — a 39-rated full-back starting with a 67-rated midfielder behind him. Fixing the picker on the *same* rosters moved starter mean OVR **W 56.9 → 65.9, CM 64.6 → 70.0, ST 64.5 → 68.1, FB 60.8 → 64.1** (CB flat) and cut starters below OVR 50 from **20.1% → 11.7%**. This is the user-reported "D1 clubs have 40-rated full-backs" bug.
  - **Assignment is over every (slot, player) pair at once, NOT slot by slot, and the distinction is load-bearing.** Slot order is not priority order, so a sequential fill lets an early slot take a player a later one needs far more (in a 4-3-3 the full-back slots precede the striker slot, so a squad's lone good forward gets pulled back to cover). Greedy-best-pair-first cannot do that: a player only loses a slot to someone worth more there.
  - **Greedy alone leaves a residual, so a local-search pass finishes the job.** Two starters can each be worth more in the other's slot, and un-crossing a pair can open a slot for a bench player. Measured before the pass existed: **0** bench improvements but **113 starter pairs across 47% of tier-1 clubs, 705 rating points**. After it, **zero** improving single moves exist anywhere in the world — that is the property `selectXI.test.ts` pins, and it is what a naive picker cannot satisfy. Terminates because `slotValue` is integral, every move gains ≥1, and the total is bounded.
  - **A roster shorter than the formation must leave the TRAILING slots empty.** Callers index the result against `slots` (`xi.forEach((p, i) => … slots[i])`), so a gap in the middle silently shifts every player after it into the wrong job. The assignment is therefore run over `slots.slice(0, min(slots.length, roster.length))`.
  - **A NaN rating turns "strictly better" into an infinite loop, and `>` alone cannot express it.** `heightScore(undefined)` is NaN, so a player missing `heightCm` gets a NaN `computeOvr` at every height-weighted position (GK/CB/ST) — reachable from hand-built fixtures and any malformed save. The improvement pass rejected a move with `if (g <= gain) return`, and **`NaN <= 0` is false**, so a NaN gain was accepted, set `gain = NaN`, and then every later comparison was false too: the loop never exited. It hung `clubSeason.test.ts` indefinitely (62 CPU-minutes before it was traced). The guard is `!(g > gain) || !Number.isFinite(g)`; a non-finite candidate is skipped and left where greedy put him, which is what the old picker effectively did anyway. **Selection has always seen these players** — the old rule read the same ratings and merely sorted them arbitrarily — so the lesson is about the loop, not the data: a sort tolerates NaN, an accumulate-until-no-improvement loop does not. `selectXI.test.ts` pins it, and that test hangs the suite without the guard.
  - **Pure, and no shared-`rng` draw**, so stream order is untouched — but composite values change, hence results change, hence the `touchStats.test.ts` scoreline hash was rebased (intended, not a touch-attribution regression). Two tests were pinning the *old* behaviour and were rewritten, not relaxed: `chooseBestFormation` no longer ties across shapes at exactly eleven players (a squad of three CMs and no DM is a 4-5-1, and 4-3-3 was docking one of them at DM), and a specialist no longer wins his slot unconditionally — he wins it when the stand-in's advantage is smaller than the penalty, which is what a secondary position is worth.
  - **The second half of the same bug lived in the MARKET, and is fixed by the same idea (`posWeakestStarterOvr`, 2026-08-27).** `hasPositionalGap` and buy-side `needMultiplier` both read `posBestOvr`, which assumes one starter per position — true only of the keeper. Formations field *two* CBs/FBs and two-to-three CMs/Ws, so a club with one excellent centre-back and three awful ones registered no need **and** priced a real signing as a downgrade: measured, Regensburg Steinerne field `{73, 32, 29, 29}` at CB with **£125M** in the bank and start a 16-year-old. Both now read `ClubContext.posWeakestStarterOvr` — the weakest man the club's *shape* actually fields there — which is the buy-side counterpart to `posSecondBestOvr`'s keep-side counterfactual: keep asks who'd step up if he left, buy asks who he'd displace. **Fixing the gate alone would not have worked**; the club registers the need and still refuses to pay, because the valuation measured the same 60-rated centre-back against the 73. A position the shape fields none of keeps the old reading, so this only bites where a formation genuinely fields two or more. Tier-1 starters under OVR 50: **12.1% → 5.2%** (20.1% before the XI-picker fix).
    - **Gated on `weakLeaguesAudit.ts`, 4 seeds × 20 seasons, run on BOTH sides — and the headline is that it IMPROVES the ladder rather than merely not breaking it.** 4-seed mean rung gaps, baseline → treatment: BIG4→Turkey **+6.06 → +7.74**, France→Turkey **+2.82 → +4.98**, France→Portugal +2.76 → +3.33, Belgium→Turkey +0.34 → +1.52, Portugal→Belgium **−0.27 → +0.13** (flips from inverted to positive), BIG4→France 3.25 → 2.77 (the only rung that narrows). That direction is the documented mechanism working: the weak leagues run on transfer receipts and the upward drain of talent is what counteracts `growthDamping` compressing the gaps — measured, deals roughly doubled (366 → 757 in one window) and receipts into every weak league roughly doubled with them.
    - **Solvency is a genuine trade, not a clean win, and neither side is clean.** Deficits over 20 seasons: baseline **2/4 seeds** (s2 −£7.3M, s3 −£1.8M), treatment **1/4** (s4 −£4.0M). It fixes two and introduces one, so fewer failures and a smaller worst case — but not zero. **Pure `origin/main` is not zero either** (s2, −£4.7M, Portugal), which is the control that matters: 20-season deficits predate both this change and the XI-picker fix, and this reduces without curing them. Anyone re-tuning here should measure main first rather than assume a green baseline.
    - **Per-seed runs MISUSE this gate.** The script applies its *mean* inversion test to whatever seeds it is handed and warns it is "under-powered below 3", and Portugal→Belgium is the documented coin-flip pair — baseline reads −1.92 on seed 3 alone and −0.27 pooled. Run `SEEDS=1,2,3,4` in one invocation, or pool the per-seed numbers by hand; never read one seed's "BROKEN" as a verdict.
    - Keep side deliberately untouched: `AI_RENEWAL_MARGIN` is calibrated against buy-side numbers (see `ValuationSide`), and the keep-side counterfactual already asks its own question correctly.
- **A player's listed position can CHANGE over his career (`changedPosition` in `core/players/positions.ts`, `RatingsSnapshot.pos`, 2026-08-13).** Decided once per offseason inside `progressPlayer`, after the rating step (he trains as what he is) and before ovr/potential are computed (so both are recomputed at the new position). Pure, **no shared-`rng` draw**, and the draw *count* is unchanged either way — `stepRatings` and `estimatePotential` both draw independently of position — so stream order is untouched; a test pins it. Conversions only fire in the offseason, so the single-season `touchStats` hash is **not** rebased this time. `RatingsSnapshot.pos` is a new persisted field; `migrate.ts` backfills it to the player's current position, which is **exact rather than a guess** — nothing could change a position before this existed, so every historical snapshot really was taken at the one he holds today.
  - **The SUSTAINED window is the load-bearing gate, not the margin.** Measured, **5.9%→8.0% of rostered players change which position they rate highest at every single season** (`scripts/positionChangeProbe.ts`), so an instantaneous "play your best position" rule would relabel ~600 players a year and the badge would mean nothing. `POSITION_CHANGE_SEASONS` (3) demands the same winner N seasons running. It is also the *inflation* control, for the same reason: best-of-N is a maximum over noisy quantities and so sits systematically above any fixed pick, and requiring persistence strips out the noise half of that bias.
  - **The window counts only snapshots stamped at his CURRENT position, which buys the cooldown for free.** Right after a conversion his history at the new position is empty, so he cannot move again until he has served a fresh window. This is what stops a borderline player oscillating between two positions and minting OVR on every leg — measured, **3 round trips against 497 changes over 20 seasons**. Don't "simplify" this to the last N entries regardless of position; that reopens the pump.
  - **A fresh world is already correctly listed, and the backlog is built by development rather than aging.** At generation, mean best-position gain is 0.15 OVR and *zero* players clear a 6-point margin — generation picks a position then rolls ratings for it. Over a dynasty the gap accumulates, and by age band it runs 0.37 / 0.70 / **0.74** / 0.60 / 0.51 across ≤20 / 21-24 / 25-28 / 29-32 / 33+: it peaks in the mid-20s and *falls* for the oldest players. So the intuitive story ("physicals decline first, so the winger drops to full-back") is **measurably wrong** — a uniform rating loss cancels exactly, since every OVR weight row sums to 100. The cause is `growthDamping` compressing a player's strongest skills during growth years, the same mechanism that raises versatility over a dynasty.
  - **Measured against `origin/main`, 20 seasons × 3 seeds** (`scripts/positionChangeAudit.ts`, run on both sides — main reads 0 conversions and is a valid control): rostered mean ovr **51.66 → 51.94 (+0.28)**, tier-1 **53.80 → 54.10 (+0.30)**. Per seed the tier-1 delta is +0.06 / −0.09 / +0.94, straddling zero on two of three, so it is **small and partly seed noise rather than zero** — worth stating honestly, since league mean ovr already drifts ~+4 over 25 seasons on main for unrelated reasons and this adds a few percent on top. Rate settles at **0.6-0.9% of rostered players per season**, ~5% of the pool ever converting over 20 seasons, and **zero in season 1** (nobody has the history yet).
  - **Wages are the cost, and the finance column is where this would fail first.** Wages are cubic in ovr, so conversions are money: mean wage bill **17.2M → 18.2M (+5.8%)**. Minimum AI budget is higher on two seeds and lower on one (9.8 vs 5.7, 14.2 vs 8.2, 6.7 vs 12.5) with **no deficits on either side** — that spread is seed noise, not an effect. **Don't read a single seed here as an improvement; an earlier draft of this entry did exactly that from seed 1 and a third seed contradicted it.**
  - **The position-mix risk is real in principle and absorbed by roster management in practice.** `COVERABLE` is asymmetric and the flows are lopsided — W→AM and CB→DM dominate, and releasing the raw backlog at a 3-point margin would swell AM from 8.0% to 10.5% of the pool while draining CM and FB. It doesn't happen because `trimRosterSurplus` and composition-weighted youth intake hold squads *at* `ROSTER_COMPOSITION`: measured, the rostered mix is exactly on target after 20 seasons on **both** branches. A conversion therefore creates churn (a surplus at one position, a hole at another) rather than drift. That the mix holds is also proof the free-agent pool can still supply every position.
  - **News volume is gated (`NEWS_POSITION_CHANGE_OVR`, 72).** The user's own players are always reported; elsewhere only established players are, for the same reason AI free-agent churn is kept out of the aggregate feeds.
- **Composite rollup is position-weighted + star-concentrated (`rollupComposites`, `COMPOSITE_STAR_CONCENTRATION`, 2026-07-21).** `attack`/`defense`/`control` no longer flat-average their position group: each is a position-weighted mean (who drives the phase counts most — ST/W for attack, CM/DM for control, CB for defense) *blended toward the group's single best player* by `COMPOSITE_STAR_CONCENTRATION` (0.3), so an elite individual resists being averaged down by weak teammates ("stars carry their phase"; a balanced XI beats a lopsided higher-OVR one). `finishing` (own shot-share weighting) and `keeping` are deliberately left alone. **Pure function of attributes — no shared-`rng` draw**, so RNG stream order is untouched; it only changes composite *values*, hence match results, hence the `touchStats.test.ts` scoreline hash was rebased a second time (intended, not a touch-attribution regression). Dynasty-audit champion churn before raising the concentration (interacts with league z-norm spread).
- **Shot conversion blends team + individual (`SHOOTER_FINISH_WEIGHT`, 2026-07-20, `simMatchDetailed` only).** `resolveShot` takes a `finishAdj` = the finisher's own skill *relative to his team's average* (shooting for shots, heading for corner headers), added to the team finishing composite for `onTargetP`/`saveP` only — **never xG** (xG stays the team-blind "average attacker" baseline, so goals-vs-xG reveals finishing). Centered on the team average, so it *redistributes* a team's goals toward its best finishers without inflating league scoring; a lone elite finisher on a weak side now genuinely stuffs the sheet (the fast composite-only `simMatch`/montecarlo path is untouched → benchmark gates unaffected). Side effect: the season top scorer rises into the mid-30s (m3-top-scorer gate widened 32→36). Rebased the `touchStats.test.ts` scoreline hash once (intended outcome change, not a touch-attribution regression).
- **Every league match runs on its own seeded stream (`leagueMatchSeed`, `simThrough.ts`, 2026-08-18).** Matches used to share the matchday's `rng` in fixture order, so a match's result was a function of every match simmed before it that day and **no single match could be re-run in isolation** — re-simming one desynced every later fixture. Now seeded off `hashInts(lid, season, matchday, home, away, LEAGUE_MATCH_STREAM=41)`, which buys two things: a result no longer depends on fixture order, and one match is independently reproducible. **The second is the load-bearing one** — it is what lets the live match viewer re-run a single match (with, later, a substitution the user made while watching) without touching the rest of the matchday. `leagueMatchSeed` is **exported so callers derive the seed rather than duplicating the recipe**. Cost, paid once: pulling matches out of the shared stream shifts every draw after them, so results differ from the pre-change baseline going forward (saves aren't damaged — their history is data). **The `touchStats.test.ts` scoreline hash did NOT need rebasing**, for the same reason the suspensions entry below documents: that baseline runs `simSeason` (`core/season.ts`), a test-only harness on a separate code path that was deliberately left alone so it keeps guarding `simMatchDetailed`. The gates that *do* cover this path — `simThrough.test.ts`, `m3-top-scorer`, `m4-multiseason`, `benchmarks` — are distributional, and all passed unchanged, which is the expected result: probabilities didn't move, only which draw landed where.
- **Determinism boundaries:** cup rounds and decorative touch-attribution (`passes`/`crosses`) each run on their own seeded streams so they can't perturb league match results — league scorelines stay bit-identical to a fixed baseline hash (`touchStats.test.ts`; the baseline was rebased once for the finishing change above).
- **UI conventions:** no emoji in UI (real icons = hand-written inline SVG, see `AwardIcons.tsx`); player-facing prose is casual/second-person, sentence-cased, no em-dashes.
- **Remapping `--bs-primary` does NOT reach form controls, and every checkbox in the game was Bootstrap blue for it (2026-09-01).** `styles.css` maps `--bs-primary` to the pitch green up top, which covers buttons and links — but Bootstrap compiles the checked colour into `.form-check-input:checked` as a **literal `#0d6efd`** rather than reading a variable, so all 12 checkboxes and switches rendered in stock blue on a green-tinted dark surface. Themed explicitly in the `.form-check-input` block beside the other form overrides; **a new control needs no work, but if one ever comes out blue this is why.** Two related gotchas: the tick and the switch knob *are* variables in Bootstrap 5.3 (`--bs-form-check-bg-image`, `--bs-form-switch-bg`), so they are themed without restating the selectors' rules; and the **unchecked** switch knob ships at `rgba(255,255,255,.25)`, drawn for a light page, which on this one reads as an empty pill rather than an off switch — hence the lift to 0.55. **`form-switch` is the house control for a setting that takes effect the moment you flip it** (Manager's "Never sack me", the depth-chart toggles, the live viewer's "Every chance", the Transfers for-sale filter, a league's "link money", New League's three). A bare `form-check` is for the other two shapes: **picking items out of a list** (World setup's per-country include — a switch per row in a twelve-row list is heavier than the choice deserves) and **staging something that happens on submit** (God Mode's clear-injury / clear-suspension, which apply on Save).
- **Two more Bootstrap colours were never remapped, and a 2026-09-03 UI audit found both (2026-09-03).** Exactly the trap the entry above documents, one selector along each time. **(a) `.nav-pills .nav-link.active`** compiles against `--bs-nav-pills-link-active-bg`, which resolves to a literal `#0d6efd` rather than to `--bs-primary` — so the Value/OVR and League/Cup toggles on Player Profile and Players/Clubs on Frivolities rendered in stock blue *inches from a pitch-green `btn-primary` doing the same job*. Themed on `.nav-pills` itself, so a new pill strip is green without anyone knowing this. **(b) `--bs-info` was the one semantic colour left unmapped** (`--bs-success`/`danger`/`warning` are all pointed at tokens), so every `.bg-info` / `.text-bg-info` / `.border-info` was stock cyan `#0dcaf0`: the board-confidence meter's "settled" step, the club-season "In progress" badge, the Schedule's current-matchday marker. Now `--bs-info: var(--sg-cup)` — the cup teal is already this system's cool, *non-result* accent, which is exactly what an "info" colour needs. **The Schedule marker had a second fault worth knowing**: `border-info` sets the border *colour on all four sides*, so it repainted that row's existing cell borders and drew a box around the row rather than an edge on it. It uses `.row-current` (an inset box-shadow left edge, combined explicitly with the win/loss washes, since a later box-shadow rule replaces an earlier one rather than stacking) — the same technique `.team-highlight` and `.row-through` already use. **The general rule: if a control comes out blue or cyan, a Bootstrap variable is unmapped; grep `styles.css`'s `--bs-` block before styling the call site.**
- **Empty states are a shared component (`ui/components/EmptyState.tsx`), because nine pages rendered as a bare sentence on the page background and read as broken (2026-09-03).** Every one of them was a `<p className="text-muted">` with no container, which made them the only content in the app not sitting on a surface — and a page-by-page audit graded all nine of them worst, above every genuine defect, for exactly that reason. They were *correct*: those competitions really hadn't started. The copy was rarely the problem; the missing container was. `EmptyState` takes a `headline` (what this page is), `children` (**when it fills up and what it will hold** — the pages that already said this graded a full grade higher than the ones that only said "nothing yet") and an optional `action` (somewhere to go now, since an empty page that is also a dead end is the worst case). Applied to Continental Cup/Shield, Domestic Cup, Champions Cups, Promotion Playoffs, Academy, Watchlist and the National Teams shell. **Deliberately capped at 62ch and NOT stretched to fill the viewport** — the void is the problem, so a wider box makes it worse. Two follow-ups the audit named and this did not take: Promotion Playoffs and Continental Shield can both derive a *provisional field* from the live tables and show it instead of prose, which is the real fix for those two.
- **`NationalTeamsLayout` set its group name larger than its page name, on all eleven pages (2026-09-03).** `<h4>National Teams</h4>` above `<h5>{title}</h5>`, so the loudest thing on every screen in the section was the one word identical across the section. Now a `.page-eyebrow` (the sidebar's own `nav-section` treatment) above an `h4` page title. Worth knowing as a shape: any grouped section that adds a shell should put the *variable* at the top of the type scale, not the constant.
- **Seasons display as years, and the start year is per-save (`meta.startYear`, 2026-08-23).** A season is a 1-based counter everywhere in the sim; `ui/format.ts`'s `seasonYear` is the only thing that turns it into a year, and which year season 1 lands on is now the player's choice on the New League screen. **Cosmetic only** — nothing in core, the engine or persistence reads it, and the field is optional with absent meaning the 2026 old saves have always shown (so no `migrate.ts` change). The offset lives as **module state in `format.ts`**, set by `LeagueContext`'s `commitLeague` — the single funnel every league (loaded, created, imported, switched away) passes through — rather than threaded to the ~90 `seasonYear` call sites, half of which are render helpers with no league in scope. The consequence to know: **anything rendering a season for a save that is not the active one must use `seasonYearFrom(startYear, season)`**, not `seasonYear` (`/leagues` sidesteps this by listing raw season numbers).
- **Crash visibility (2026-07-25):** `ui/components/ErrorBoundary.tsx` wraps the `<Outlet />` in `Layout.tsx` **keyed by `location.key`** (a page crash keeps TopBar/Sidebar mounted so the user can navigate away, and moving route remounts a clean boundary) plus an outer one in `App.tsx` for the routes outside `Layout` (`/leagues`, `/new-league`). `posthog.init` sets **`capture_exceptions: true`** (`main.tsx`) and the boundary also calls `captureException` itself, because React swallows the error on its way to unmounting. Before this, *any* throw blanked the whole app with the reason only in the console, and error tracking had **zero** issues recorded ever — a crash report could never be more specific than "it crashed". Gotcha for testing: **server rendering does not run error boundaries** (React re-throws to the caller), and there's no DOM test env in this repo, so `test/ui/errorBoundary.test.tsx` pins our half of the contract (`getDerivedStateFromError` + the fallback render) rather than a live catch. `test/ui/transfersRender.test.tsx` is the render harness pattern for a page (mock `useLeague`, wrap in `MemoryRouter`, `renderToStaticMarkup`) — a throw there surfaces as a test failure.

## Milestone status (per `SOCCER_GM_SPEC.md` §7)

All done and merged:
- **M0** — engine port.
- **M1** — players → composites: generation, per-position OVR, composite rollup + league normalization.
- **M2** — season loop, IndexedDB persistence, web worker, scheduling, `simThrough`, minimal UI.
- **M3** — box scores: per-player attribution, `simMatchDetailed`, `SeasonStats`, box-score/leaders pages.
- **M4** — dynasty mechanics: progression (`players/progression.ts`), aging, retirement, youth intake, free agency, contracts, offseason orchestrator (`offseason.ts`).
- **M5** — match texture: cards, fatigue + AI subs, corners, penalties, in-match injuries (multi-matchday recovery), stoppage time, composite re-rolls after subs/reds/injuries. Sub selection blends fatigue with a live `computeMatchRating` estimate (`subPriority`) to pick who comes *off*; a **bench-quality gate** (`worthSub`/`tiredValue`/`benchValue` in `matchSim.ts`, `SUB_FRESHNESS_BONUS`/`SUB_QUALITY_MARGIN`/`SUB_FATIGUE_RELIEF`) decides whether the swap is worth it at all — a fresh replacement may fall short of the tired starter's ovr by an allowance that grows with the starter's energy deficit, so weak benches hold their tireds on (holds back ~1 sub in 10; rebased the `touchStats.test.ts` scoreline hash, intended). **`SUB_QUALITY_MARGIN` was retuned 1→2.5 (2026-07-23)** — at 1 the gate held back ~1 sub in 5, twice the intended rate, which cost the champion enough late-game quality to push the M1 standings gate (`simThrough.test.ts`, champion 78-94 pts) below its floor on main. Measured over 15 seeded seasons: margin 1 → 78.7 champion pts, 2.5 → 80.8, always-sub → 79.8. Note the gate's own 5-season sample is noisy (single seasons swing 70-87), so it sits near its floor by construction — **re-measure over ≥15 seasons before reading a small move in it as a regression.** The gate is also **form-aware** (`SUB_GATE_RATING_INFLUENCE`, `liveRatingFor`): the tired starter's live `computeMatchRating` (vs the 6.0 baseline) shifts `tiredValue` — a starter playing well is harder to justify hooking, one playing poorly easier — reusing the same signal `subPriority` uses for the who-comes-*off* pick. The form term is mean-zero around baseline, so it redistributes *which* subs are held without moving the ~1-in-10 aggregate; rebased the scoreline hash a second time (intended). `MatchPlayer` now carries `ovr` for this. See **Roster & tactics** for the user "more minutes" flag.
- **M6** — depth + finance slice: budgets, scouting, hype, contracts, transfer valuation, two transfer windows, negotiation, extensions/FA signings. (Design detail beyond this summary lives in a Google Doc the user maintains + `docs/finance-design.md`.)

## Feature ledger (post-M6, all shipped)

Each entry: what it is / where it lives / gotchas.

**Manager career (the user's club can change mid-save)**
- **`meta.userTid` is no longer fixed for the life of a save (2026-08-23).** `core/manager/` adds board confidence, job offers and sackings, all resolved at the offseason boundary. The reassignment itself was always trivial — every one of the ~198 `userTid` consumers reads it live, so the AI starts managing the club you left and stops managing the one you joined on the next read. **The load-bearing part is `switchClub`'s handover**, because several `StoredTeam` fields are *user-only by convention* and an AI club that inherits one populated is in a state nothing unwinds.
  - **The academy is the one that silently breaks things.** `academyRoster` is a user-only holding pool: AI clubs never promote from it, never field those players and never release them — yet the offseason's season-start charge sums `[...roster, ...academyRoster]` and `freeAgentCull` counts them as rostered. Left behind they are permanent zombies: paid, uncullable, unable to ever play again. `switchClub` graduates them onto the senior roster on ordinary `contractTerms` (rng-free), letting the next `trimRosterSurplus` sort out the surplus.
  - Also cleared on the way out: `starters`→null, `transferListed`/`moreMinutes`/`scoutingObserved`→empty, `nextScoutingSpend`=`scoutingSpend`, plus **only that club's** formation re-picked via `chooseBestFormation`. Re-running `assignAIFormations` over the world instead would re-pick 300-odd clubs' shapes at a moment nothing else in the sim does, changing their match results as a side effect of the user changing jobs. `ensureUserRosterSafety` runs once on the departing club, since it only ever protects `userTid`. League-level `negotiations`/`inboundOffers`/`loanListings`/`loanRejections` are dropped (all implicitly "the user's"); `activeLoans` deliberately survive (club-owned contracts).
  - **Arriving fogs the new squad** (`reconcileScoutingObserved({}, roster, season)`) — falls out of the existing scouting model rather than being special-cased, and reads exactly right: you know their ratings, not their ceilings.
  - **Runs at the offseason transition in `simThrough`, beside `initInternationalCampaign`, not inside `simOffseason`.** It judges the season just finished (wants that final table and nothing after it), and it must be answerable *before* the offseason runs so a manager who moves manages the **new** club's summer. Pure state, **no shared-`rng` draw** — offers use their own `hashInts` stream (`MANAGER_OFFER_STREAM` 970), so the `touchStats` scoreline hash and the M1 standings gate are untouched (verified).
  - **Confidence is a running 0-100 balance, not a "last N seasons" rule** — legible to the user, and it lets goodwill be *banked*, so an overachiever survives one bad year and relegation is never an automatic sacking. Judged on **finish vs the club's standing**, never vs its current squad — see the next bullet. Deliberately **not** built on `deriveLeagueContexts`'s `stature`: that is the transfer AI's view, and coupling to it would let a market retune silently change who gets sacked.
  - **Expectations must not be settable by the manager, and the first model got this wrong (user catch, 2026-08-23).** `deriveExpectations` originally ranked clubs by `computeTeamRating`. Squad quality is the one variable the manager fully controls, so tearing the team down lowered the bar with it: sell everyone, finish next-to-last with the worst squad in the league, and the board reads it as *beating expectations*. It rewarded sabotage. **Standing is now built only from what a transfer window cannot touch** — recent finishes (`MANAGER_EXPECTATION_W_HISTORY` 0.7, dominant because they are the most direct statement of what a club is) and hype (0.3), each normalized within the competition. **There is deliberately no money term, and two attempts at one were removed** (code review, 2026-08-23): bank balance alone is *backwards*, since spending the kitty is the normal thing a manager does and emptying it moved a mid-table club's bar six places easier while it fielded a stronger squad; balance-plus-wage-bill fixes buying and selling (each just moves value between the halves) but not **releasing**, where wages fall with no fee arriving, so a teardown still dragged the bar down — caught by the teardown test itself. Self-harming is not the same as impossible, and the model exists so that *no* sequence of transfer decisions moves the bar. Cost: a suddenly-rich club is not expected to improve until it actually does, which is the right way round for a board that judges results. Squad rating survives only as the season-1 bootstrap, blended out by `historyCoverage` as real results accumulate — and that is the squad the manager was *handed*. Two traps: **(a)** a past finish is meaningless without its division, so `seasonStanding` converts it to a percentile and lays the divisions on **one continuous scale meeting at seams of `MANAGER_EXPECTATION_TIER_SEAM` (0.35)** — tier 1 takes [seam, 1] and each tier below takes a band of width `seam` under it, so tier 2 is [0, seam] and tier 3 is **[-seam, 0]** — or a promoted second-division champion carries a "1st" into the top flight and is expected to win it. **Going NEGATIVE is deliberate and is what let a third division be seated without retuning the two above it**: the sole consumer feeds this through `normalizeWithinComp`, which min-max rescales each competition against its own observed range, so nothing reads the absolute value and the scale was never bounded to [0, 1]. Tiers 1 and 2 are byte-identical as a function to the two-tier version. **The bug this replaces was `tier === 2 ? low : high`, which sent every OTHER tier into the top-flight band** — measured, a third-division title scored **1.000, identical to winning the top flight**, and finishing bottom of the third division outscored mid-table in the second, so a third-division manager was judged on top-flight standards. It needs no dynasty audit for the same reason the difficulty levers don't: `deriveExpectations` is read only for `expectations.get(userTid)`, so nothing reaches the AI or the rng stream. `scripts/managerTenureProbe.ts` is still the calibration tool if the bands are ever moved; **(b)** `reviewSeason` must pass `league.seasonHistory` *excluding the season being judged* — it gets this for free since a season is appended during the following offseason, but any new caller must preserve it, or the bar moves to match the result it is judging. `test/core/manager.test.ts` pins the teardown case directly.
  - **Harshness has two axes.** In-world `demand` blends club-rank-within-league with league-strength-within-world (asymmetric: a demanding board damps rewards and amplifies penalties). Globally, **`DifficultyProfile.boardPatience`** (easy 1.7 / normal 1 / hard 0.75 / brutal 0.55) scales the whole verdict — the one difficulty knob with **no world effect**, so it needs no `difficultyProbe` recalibration.
  - **`MANAGER_CONFIDENCE_SWING` was sized against measured variance, and the first guess was 1.9x too harsh.** `scripts/managerTenureProbe.ts` judges **every AI club as if it were the user** over N seasons and reports sackings per 100 club-seasons per difficulty. Judging the *actual* user club is useless here: it is unmanaged in a headless run, so it rots and would report "the board fires everyone" against any tuning whatsoever. Measured over 2552 club-seasons: an ordinary season lands within **±0.158 of a division** (p25-p75, ~±3 places in a 20-club league), a bad one about **-0.368** (p10). At the first-guess swing of 130 a single p10 season cost ~68 against a starting 65 — one bottom-decile year all but ended a career — and normal difficulty sacked a manager every 8 seasons (12.5 per 100 club-seasons, median tenure 3, only 119/320 surviving 8 seasons). Now 70. Re-measured after expectations moved onto club standing (which is noisier than squad rating, since results vary more than ratings do — p10 -0.42 vs -0.37): normal lands at **7.6 per 100 club-seasons — one sacking per ~13 seasons**, median tenure 5, 163/320 surviving 8 seasons; easy 2.3, hard 11.5, brutal 15.0. (Dropping the money term moved these by under 0.2 — it was contributing almost nothing to the distribution while offering an exploit channel, so removing it was free.) Re-run the probe after touching either constant or any `boardPatience`; note the figures describe **competently-managed AI clubs**, so they are the baseline a human over- or under-performs against, not a prediction of one player's tenure.
  - **`MANAGER_CONFIDENCE_RECOVERY` (0.12) exists because the probe exposed a dead end, not to soften the numbers.** Confidence only ever moved on over/underperformance, so a manager at 20 who then finished *exactly* to expectation every season stayed at 20 forever, permanently one bad year from the sack with no route back. Each season now drifts 12% of the way back toward `MANAGER_START_CONFIDENCE` **before** the verdict, which cuts both ways on purpose: a long-banked 100 also decays, so a title six years ago stops being a permanent shield.
  - **The offer pool was the intersection of an ABSOLUTE band and a RELATIVE filter, and at the top of either scale they stopped overlapping (2026-09-02, user report).** `generateJobOffers` centred a `MANAGER_OFFER_BAND`-wide window on `reputation/100` — an absolute position on the world prestige scale — and then intersected it with `prestige >= currentPrestige`, which is relative to the job you hold. The pool is empty whenever `currentPrestige > reputation/100 + MANAGER_OFFER_BAND`, and that is reachable two ways which look unrelated and are the same arithmetic: a **decorated** manager who climbed to the summit (reputation caps at 100, so the band caps at 1.2, while `prestige` is min-max normalized and hands exactly one club 1.000 — no club can ever satisfy the step-up filter) and an **unproven** one handed a big club (base reputation 30 puts the band's top at 0.5, below any serious club). Measured on a fresh 626-club world at reputation 100, mean offers per season by the user club's world rank: **rank 1 `0.00`, rank 3 `0.75`, rank 10 `2.83`, rank 25 `3.33`** — i.e. the better you did, the fewer offers you got, until they stopped. Fixed by centring on `max(reputation/100, currentPrestige)` and allowing a `MANAGER_OFFER_LATERAL_BAND` (0.05) step down, so **your current club sets the floor and reputation sets how far above it you can reach**. Reputation still does the only job it had — it is what lets a small club's manager be offered the biggest jobs in the world — it simply no longer *caps* how big a job may approach you.
    - **`generateNationOffers` had it word for word** and took the identical fix (`NATIONAL_OFFER_LATERAL_BAND` 0.08, wider because national prestige is read off *rank* over ~44 nations, so one place is ~0.023 and 0.05 would reach barely two). The unemployed path is **not** affected by this fix and needed its own — `currentPrestige` is 0 with no job, so the `max` is inert there; see `NATIONAL_OFFER_CLUB_REP_WEIGHT` immediately below.
    - **A club career was INVISIBLE to federations, which made the unemployed path a dead end (2026-09-02, user question, `NATIONAL_OFFER_CLUB_REP_WEIGHT` 0.65).** A separate bug from the band/filter one above and found by asking about the case that fix does *not* touch: with no country, `currentPrestige` is 0 so the `max` is inert. `nationalReputation` is derived from `NationalStint[]` alone, and the only way to open a stint is to be offered a job, so a club manager who has never held one is pinned at `NATIONAL_REP_BASE` (30) **forever**. Measured on the real 70-nation world: 4 offers every offseason (so the path was never *dead*), but always from **ranks #45-62**, and the best country the band could reach at all was **#33** — identical at club reputation 30 and 100. A decorated club career bought literally nothing, which is not how the crossover works in real football. Federations now see a discounted slice of `managerReputation`, folded into the same offer target.
      - **It feeds the OFFER TARGET ONLY, never `nationalReputation` itself.** That number is displayed to the player as their international standing and must keep meaning what it says; it also drives nothing else, so confidence and sackings stay strictly on the national record and a club-side retune still cannot change who a federation dismisses — the property `NATIONAL_*` exists as a second constant block to protect. `clubReputation` is a **required** field on `NationOfferInputs` rather than an optional one, so a new caller breaks at compile time instead of silently under-offering.
      - **Swept against what is ACTUALLY OFFERED, not against the band arithmetic, and the two disagree by ten places.** The band's ceiling at target `t` is `t + NATIONAL_OFFER_BAND`, but `pool.slice(0, NATIONAL_MAX_OFFERS * 4)` then keeps only the 16 nations *closest to the target*, truncating the top of the band away. At weight 0.5 the band reaches rank 18 and the best country ever offered over 20 seasons is **rank 28**. The first draft of this entry quoted the reachable number as though it were the offered one. Measured best-offered at club reputation 100, by weight: 0.50 → #28, 0.55 → #25, 0.60 → #21, **0.65 → #18 (Wales)**, 0.70 → #14, 0.75 → #11 (Brazil). **Quote the offered rank; the reachable one is the optimistic lie.**
      - **The ladder is preserved deliberately and is pinned by a test.** The top five nations stay out of reach on a club career alone; Spain still needs a national reputation near 75, earned in the job. The weight also only bites above club reputation **47** (below that it lands under `NATIONAL_REP_BASE` and the `max` ignores it), so an unproven manager's offers are byte-identical to before and roughly one league title is what starts opening it.
      - The probe's national grid is a **synthetic 44-nation field** and so reports a *different* rank for the same weight (#9 rather than #18). It is built rather than simmed because the arithmetic is what is being probed, but it means **the shipped calibration numbers came from a real world and must be re-measured there**, not read off the probe.
    - **The sacked path took the same centring, and that IS a behaviour change** — `MANAGER_SACKED_PRESTIGE_PENALTY` means "a rung down", which only means something relative to where you were; measuring the drop from *reputation* was the same category error. A manager sacked by a giant is no longer offered the bottom of the pyramid. It was never *broken* there (the path has a never-empty fallback), which is exactly why it would have been left carrying the bug.
    - **Count alone cannot detect this and cannot verify the fix, which is why `scripts/managerOfferProbe.ts` prints two grids.** Offer count saturates near `MANAGER_MAX_OFFERS` once the pool is comfortably large, so after the fix every cell reads ~3.33 and reputation looks inert. It isn't: the second grid is the mean prestige of the clubs offered, where a prestige-0.349 club's manager goes **0.346 at reputation 30 to 0.960 at 100**. Read them together.
    - **Nothing here touches the sim.** Both lists draw on their own seeded streams (`MANAGER_OFFER_STREAM` 970 / `NATIONAL_OFFER_STREAM` 971) and nothing else reads an offer, so no scoreline, rating or dynasty baseline moves and no audit was needed — only the number of draws *within* those two streams changes.
    - **The generalizable lesson, and it is the fifth instance of this shape in this file: a filter expressed in absolute units and a filter expressed relative to the user are not composable, and their intersection fails silently at the extremes.** It presents as a dead feature rather than an error — no throw, no empty-state copy, just a list that is always empty for the players most likely to have earned it. The tell is that the failure is *monotonic in the user's own progress*: when a mechanic gets rarer the better the user does, suspect two scales before suspecting a tuning constant.
  - **There is no unemployed state, by design** — every page assumes a user club, so a sacked manager's offer list is guaranteed non-empty (`generateJobOffers` drops the band filter rather than return nothing) and he must accept one. `ManagerState.sackingEnabled` is the user-facing "never sack me" switch (user call, 2026-08-23); offers still arrive so moving clubs stays available.
  - Reputation is **derived** from `ManagerStint[]` (`managerReputation`), never stored, so the formula can be retuned without rewriting careers. The stints themselves store raw results because the board judges at the end of a season *before* it is appended to `seasonHistory` — deriving would need a special case for exactly the moment it matters. Migration backfills **confidence 100** (the board never watched those seasons; an upgrading dynasty must not be one bad year from a sacking) and reconstructs titles/trophies from `seasonHistory`/`cupHistory`/`shieldHistory`/`domesticCupHistory` so an established save arrives with the reputation it earned; `overperformance` stays 0, being unreconstructable without past squad ratings.

**National team management (`core/nationalManager/`, `LeagueStore.nationalManager`, 2026-08-29)**
- **The user can manage a country alongside their club.** Chosen on `/new-league` (a nullable 6th param to `createLeagueState`) or accepted later from a federation's offer. Three pages: `/national-teams/player-pool` (who is in the 23 — a search across every eligible player in the country), `/national-teams/my-squad` (the team sheet — shape and XI, on the club Roster page's own `PitchField`, whose nine club-only props were made optional for it) and `/national-teams/federation` (the portal — confidence, verdict, offers, career). Naming the squad and picking the eleven are split because they are different jobs on different data, and together on one screen the several-hundred-row search drowned a team sheet of 23. Both squad pages mark each player's club with `DivisionBadge` (country flag + D1/D2, extracted from Power Rankings on its second use rather than copied — its two halves have to agree about which competition they describe) through a shared `useClubIndex`/`ClubCell` in `nationalTeams/shared.tsx`, because the two pages list the same players and would read as a bug the moment they described one differently. **The badge earns its room here in a way it would not on a club roster**: a national squad is drawn from the whole world, so without it an English international at an Italian second-division club is indistinguishable from one at a big-four side. The flag is the *league's* country, never the player's. Deliberately shaped as a mirror of `core/manager`, because it is the same relationship from a different chair.
- **`nation: null` is an ordinary, permanent, playable state, and that is the one structural difference from the club career.** A sacked club manager must take another job because every page assumes a club; a manager with no country is simply a save that plays the way every save did before this shipped. So nothing here ever blocks the offseason, `generateNationOffers` may legitimately return an empty list (where `generateJobOffers` drops its filters rather than do so), and `migrate.ts` backfills `emptyNationalManagerState()` with **nothing reconstructed** — those campaigns were played with nobody in charge.
- **The whole match-side mechanism is one field and one line.** `NationSquad.starters` (optional, so no migration) plus `nationMatchData` passing `squad.starters ?? null` instead of the hardcoded `null` it had. Everything below that is the club lineup path unchanged — `leagueMatchData` → `resolveXI` → `isValidStarters` — so a stale national XI degrades to the auto-pick exactly as a club's does.
  - **`resolveXI` is all-or-nothing, and that bites harder here than it does for a club.** `isValidStarters` fails if *any* named pid doesn't resolve, so one unavailable man discards the whole eleven and `selectXI` re-picks all eleven — it does not keep the other ten and fill the gap. A club lineup is re-examined every week; a **qualifying campaign spans three offseasons**, so a national XI genuinely outlives its players and this fires in ordinary play. It is the behaviour we want (never field ten) but it is *not* what "the eleven fills in around the gap" would mean, and the Manual, the changelog entry and the page copy were all written wrong on this once and corrected — say "the game picks that whole eleven for you", not "he's skipped".
- **`editableSquad` (`international/userSquad.ts`) is the single answer to "which squad does an edit apply to".** A campaign's squads live in three places (qualifying, the World Cup, an array of confederation cups) and which one you are picking for follows the **pending stage**, not "the most recent campaign" — editing only means anything while a stage is left to play. `displaySquad` is the read-only fallback so the page has something to show between campaigns. The action layer, the page and the Dashboard nudge all go through them, so they cannot disagree.
- **Expectation is read off `IntlPowerSnapshot`, never off the named squad, and that is the un-gameable half.** `core/manager/expectation.ts` goes to great lengths to build a club's bar out of things a transfer window can't touch, because squad quality is what a club manager controls. **A national manager controls none of it** — he cannot sign, sell, release or develop anyone — so player strength is a safe bar, and the file says so. The one lever he *does* have is naming a weak squad, which is why the rank comes from the snapshot (built by `buildSquads`/`selectSquad` from each nation's **best available** eleven) rather than `squad.rating`. Do not "simplify" that to the stored rating; it reopens the teardown exploit the club side exists to prevent. `fieldExpectation` then ranks within the campaign's own field, since a confederation cup is not the world.
- **Judged per campaign, not per season, and therefore inside `simOffseason` rather than at the `simThrough` boundary where `reviewSeason` sits.** Forced by the calendar: a campaign is *drawn* at the boundary but *played* during the advance, so at the boundary there is nothing to judge. `reviewNationalCampaign` runs immediately after `simThroughInternational`, which also means it sees a finished campaign even when the user skipped every stage. `concludedCampaigns` detects what finished from the campaign objects (`season`, `championNid`, `qualified.length`, and `qualifying.season + INTL_QUAL_LEGS - 1`) rather than from `season % INTL_CYCLE_YEARS`, so a save that joined the cycle late or a world too small for confederation cups can't leave a campaign silently unjudged. In the shipped cadence exactly one concludes per offseason, in three offseasons of four.
- **Qualifying needs its own placement function, and the clamp is the reason.** `tournamentPlacement` bands a finish by the round the nation went out in (champion 1, runner-up 2, beaten semi-finalists 3.5, quarters 6.5, R16 12.5, group exit the midpoint of what's left), derived from the bracket's own depth so a 16-nation confederation cup reads correctly. Qualifying has **no granularity** — you are in the finals or you are not — so an unclamped midpoint reads a favourite's routine qualification as a collapse (expected 3rd, "finished" 16th). `qualifyingPlacement` therefore clamps *toward* expectation, which makes all four cases come out with the right sign: qualifying only helps if you weren't expected to, missing out only hurts if you were.
- **Offers draw on `NATIONAL_OFFER_STREAM` (971), distinct from the club side's 970**, and nothing here touches the shared `rng`. Generated every offseason regardless of whether a campaign concluded, because that is the only route back in for an unemployed manager — hence `NATIONAL_OFFER_UNEMPLOYED_CHANCE` (0.55) against the employed 0.14, and the step-up filter lifting when you hold no job.
- **`NATIONAL_*` is a second constant block on purpose**, not a reuse of `MANAGER_*`: the two jobs are judged on different things at different cadences, and folding them together would let a club-side retune silently change who gets sacked by their country. Same argument as `WORLD_AWARD_OVR_WEIGHT` beside `AWARD_OVR_WEIGHT`. `NATIONAL_CONFIDENCE_SWING` is 46 against the club's 70 because a 32-nation field spreads far wider than a league finish does. `confidenceMood`/`confidenceLabel` *are* shared — both are pure display of a 0-100 number.
- **Sits out a multi-season jump**, guarded on `meta.userTid === AUTOPILOT_TID`. Same reasoning the club board's guard documents (you weren't picking the team), plus it stops a jump ending with a country lost over campaigns the user never saw.
- **`leaveNationalJob` clears `starters` on the departed nation's live squads but deliberately keeps the named 23.** A national team owns nothing — no budget, no contracts, no roster of its own — so unlike `switchClub` there is no handover to unwind; the manual eleven is the only user-only state, and a successor inherits a squad and picks a team from it. `takeNationalJob` is therefore safe at any point in the season, unlike a club switch.
- **Gated by a determinism test** (`nationalManager.test.ts`): one offseason run with and without a managed nation must produce identical `players`/`teams`/`transfers`/`seasonHistory`/`international`. If that ever fails, every dynasty audit in `scripts/` is measuring two different games depending on whether the user had a national job.

**Roster & tactics**
- **Starting XI** — user drag-drops between XI and bench *and* between two XI slots, persisted as `StoredTeam.starters` (11 pids, `null`=auto). `resolveXI` (`core/lineup/resolveXI.ts`) falls back to `selectXI` auto-pick when invalid. Only the user's club sets `starters`; AI always auto-picks. **The swap rule is `core/lineup/swapStarters.ts`, not the page** — `Roster.tsx` used to reject any drag where both ends were on the same side with the comment "nothing to swap", which was true only while composites bucketed by natural position; since they bucket by **slot** (2026-08-12), a starter-to-starter drag is a genuine tactical move, so that guard was lifted (2026-08-14) and only the bench-to-bench case is a no-op. The keeper rule is **not** re-derived there: `swapStarters` builds the candidate array and asks `isValidStarters`, so there is one definition of "an outfielder may not stand in the GK slot" and a rejected swap returns `null` (leave the lineup alone) rather than writing an invalid array that `resolveXI` would silently replace with an auto-pick.
- **Formations** — 16 shapes in `FORMATIONS` (`core/lineup/formations.ts`), `StoredTeam.formation` (default `4-3-3`). A real tactical lever: the match sim rolls up composites from the XI each club's formation fields (`teamFormation`/`teamSlots`). AI clubs auto-pick their strongest shape (`chooseBestFormation`, refreshed end of each transfer window via `assignAIFormations`; never the user's). The user can opt into the same pick via a **"Best XI"** button on Roster (`autoPickBestXIAction` in `LeagueContext`, runs `chooseBestFormation` on the user roster + clears `starters`). `PitchField.tsx` + `pitchLayout.ts` render per-formation layouts; every UI surface derives from `FORMATION_IDS`, so adding a shape needs only the slot array + its layout.
  - **A shape must be a distinct slot multiset, and the obvious additions aren't (2026-08-15, when 7 shapes were added).** Composites bucket by slot and nothing in the sim knows where a slot is *drawn*, so two shapes with the same slots are the same team: **4-1-4-1 is exactly 4-3-3** (GK/CB2/FB2/DM/CM2/W2/ST) and **4-2-4 is exactly 4-4-2** — adding them would only add ties for `chooseBestFormation` to break. A test pins distinctness. The corollary is that back-3 vs back-5 can't be carried by wing-back height (no such axis); it's carried by the midfield band instead — 3-4-3 fields CM2 where 5-2-3 fields DM2, 3-5-2 fields CM2+AM where 5-2-1-2 fields DM2+AM.
  - **Order is load-bearing**: `chooseBestFormation` breaks ties by `FORMATION_IDS` order, so 4-3-3 stays first (it's the default, and the "11-man roster ties to 4-3-3" test depends on it) and new shapes append.
  - **Measured before shipping** (`scripts/formationSpreadProbe.ts`, **4 seeds** × season 3, 319 AI clubs each): the risk is a new shape whose slots happen to match `ROSTER_COMPOSITION` winning everywhere and making every club play alike. It went the other way on every seed — all 16 shapes get used, and the most common shape's share *fell* (25.7→19.4, 20.7→16.6, 20.4→16.0, 18.5→13.5), with 29.8–33.9% of clubs switching shape for +0.145 to +0.203 raw ovr per starter. **The baseline itself moves ~7 points across seeds, so quote the range, not one draw** — seed 1 happens to be the highest of the four, and an earlier version of this entry generalised from it alone. The probe also reports a **slot-adjusted** figure because `chooseBestFormation` ranks on **raw `p.ovr`, not `ovrAtSlot`** (pre-existing), so a shape can in principle win the pick by fielding a strong man in a slot foreign to him and then play worse; measured, the two track on all four seeds (adjusted +0.134 to +0.193, never more than 0.011 below raw) and 1.9–2.8% of clubs end up marginally worse than their legacy pick. Re-run the probe before adding more shapes — a gap opening between those two numbers is the signal that the raw-ovr ranking needs to become slot-aware.
  - **Dynasty-audited** (`scripts/slotChurnAudit.ts`, 15 seasons × **4 seeds**, run on both this branch and its merge-base — the script is byte-identical on both sides). The risk was the same one the slot-aware composites change was audited for: a shape lever that sharpens the strong/weak gap into a procession. It doesn't. Title churn is **flat-to-better and straddles zero per seed** (distinct champions per league 8.13 → 8.54 on the mean, per seed +1.37/0.00/−0.62/+0.88; dominant club's title share 31.5% → 28.8%, per seed −6.7/+0.8/+1.7/−6.7) — so "no procession" is what the data supports, **not** "title races opened up". Anti-inflation holds (mean ovr drift over 15 seasons 0.00 → 0.15, against the ~+4 over 25 seasons main already carries for unrelated reasons; two seeds sit at +0.3-0.4 where the baseline was ~0, so it is small rather than literally zero). D2 ceiling unchanged (45 → 44 over-threshold AI player-seasons across the four seeds).
  - **The one systematic effect is champion points, down on ALL FOUR seeds** (77.82 → 77.34, per seed −0.65/−0.53/−0.29/−0.43). That direction is the mechanism working as designed: every club gets a marginally better XI, not just the strong ones, so the champion's margin over the field narrows. It is worth knowing because the **M1 standings gate (`simThrough.test.ts`, champion 78-94 pts) already sits near its floor by construction** and is noisy over its 5-season sample — the gate passes here, but roughly half a point of its headroom is now spent. **Re-measure that gate over ≥15 seasons, not its own sample, before adding more shapes**; a further shape batch pushing champion points down again is the thing that would fail it.
- **"Extend all"** (`core/contractRenewal.ts`, `ui/components/ExtendAllButton.tsx`, 2026-08-21) — one click re-signs every one of the user's players whose deal is in its final year. `renewalsDue(league, group)` answers *who and what it costs*, `extendAllContracts` re-signs exactly that set; the button label is built from the former so it can't promise something the latter doesn't do. Four things worth knowing: **(a) scoped per surface** (`"senior"` Roster / `"academy"` Academy / `"loanedOut"` Loans) rather than one global button, because a loan moves the pid onto the loanee's roster — a loaned-out player is on **no** list but the Loans page, so a single Roster button would either miss him or silently reach players that page doesn't show. **(b)** Terms are each player's **age-based default** (`contractTerms` with no override), so anyone wanting a hand-picked length must be extended individually first; the Division 2 holdouts `wouldRefuseExtension` names are excluded and surfaced as a separate count in the banner, since they wear the same "Final year" badge and nothing re-signs them. **(c)** One `mutate`, not a loop over `extendContractAction` — every mutate writes the whole save, so N calls would be N IndexedDB writes; `extendContracts`/`extendAcademyContracts` (`contracts.ts`) do it in **one pass that keeps untouched players by reference**, which the split-storage dirty-set diff depends on. **(d)** `renewalsDue` walks the player pool, so all three pages hold it behind a `useMemo` keyed on `league` — on Roster it lives inside the existing `derived` memo, because that render body runs **once per dragover event** (see the comment there). No persisted field, no migration, no rng draw.
- **The user's youth intake is a TRIAL GROUP he chooses from (`StoredTeam.youthTrialists`, `/youth-intake`, 2026-08-31).** Ten to twelve 16-year-olds arrive unsigned each offseason; `YOUTH_TRIAL_SIGN_LIMIT` (5) get academy contracts and the rest are free agents. Replaces the Incoming Talent page, which listed every unsigned under-22 in the world — a screen that only had anything worth reading because AI clubs binned ~85% of their youth (see `AI_PROSPECT_SLOTS`); with that fixed, the leftovers are leftovers and moved to `/free-agents`, which no longer filters by age. `/incoming-talent` redirects rather than 404ing a bookmark.
  - **Sized against the decision, not against realism.** An ordinary intake is 3-5 against an `ACADEMY_ROSTER_CAP` of 10, so "choose from your intake" over that group is a confirmation dialog: you keep nearly all of them nearly every year. The fog on potential is what makes 5-of-12 a real call.
  - **Generation is unperturbed, and it is verified rather than argued — but read the claim precisely.** What is bit-identical is *the world the offseason generates*: no other club's rng draws, pid assignment or players move. It is **not** a claim that a dynasty is unchanged, and it can't be: the user's squad genuinely differs from the first intake on, his results move the table, and the table feeds `freeAgencySigningOrder` and every club's academy form. That divergence is the feature working, the same as any gameplay change; what must not happen is one club's academy silently re-rolling all 420 clubs' youth. Three things are each required to prevent that: **(a)** the top-up runs *after* the intake loop, so every other club keeps the pids it would have had — pids are not labels, `developmentBias` and `isGenerational` are hashed off them, so shifting assignment changes who is a wonderkid world-wide; **(b)** extras are drawn on `YOUTH_TRIAL_STREAM`, never the shared `rng`, so the shared draw count is identical; **(c)** the user's *ordinary* intake is still drawn inside the loop exactly as before. Verified by fingerprinting every non-user rostered player after one offseason with and without the feature: **identical on seeds 4 and 11** (10,375 and 10,326 players, name/pos/ovr/pot/born). `youthIntake.test.ts` pins (a) structurally so a regression surfaces without a two-branch run.
  - **Trialists count as rostered in `freeAgentPids`**, read off the team so all 18 callers get it with no signature change — otherwise an AI club signs one out from under the decision, or the cull deletes him. They carry no contract, so the season-start charge (which sums roster + academy) ignores them.
  - **There is deliberately NO "release a trialist" action, and one existed briefly and was the hole.** Declining used to make him a free agent on the spot, and `/free-agents` no longer filters by age — so the route round `YOUTH_TRIAL_SIGN_LIMIT` was: sign five to the academy, decline the other seven, sign all seven from Free Agents the same day (only `ROSTER_CAP` and a 16-year-old's negligible wage apply). The decision the feature exists for evaporated. Undecided trialists now simply stay held until the rollover, which needs no extra machinery because a held trialist is already out of `freeAgentPids`.
  - **Three places strand a trial group if they forget it, and all three are user-only state handovers.** `handToAI` (`manager/switchClub.ts`) and `beginAutopilot` clear it alongside `starters`/`transferListed`/`moreMinutes`; without that the group sits on a club the AI now runs, where the offseason's reset — which keys off the *current* `userTid` — never reaches it. Since `freeAgentPids` counts trialists as rostered, that group is then invisible to every signing path for the life of the save, never plays again, and (being high-potential) escapes `cullFreeAgentPool` too. Exactly the `academyRoster` zombie `dissolveAcademy` sits three lines above to prevent. **Dropped rather than graduated**, unlike the academy: a trialist holds no contract and sits on no roster, so releasing the pid is all it takes.
  - **`signTrialist` charges a mid-season stipend and checks the budget**, as `signToAcademy` does for the same player by the other route. The group is normally resolved in the offseason, where the season-start charge picks him up — but the page is reachable all season, since the trial list survives until the next rollover, so a March signing would otherwise be free.
  - **The group is REPLACED, not appended, each offseason**, which is what stops it becoming the `academyRoster`-style zombie CLAUDE.md warns about for AI clubs: an undecided group resolves itself at the next rollover, and the unsigned simply stop being held (no contract, no roster, so nothing has to release them). The academy stamp on `hist` is applied at *signing*, not generation — a trialist who was never signed was never an academy player and his OVR chart must not claim he was.
  - **Dynasty-audited anyway, despite being user-club-only, and it was worth it.** `weakLeaguesAudit`, 20 seasons × 4 seeds: **every rung mean passes** (worst −0.09 against a −1 gate; BIG4→Serbia **+7.14** ≥ 4, France→Serbia **+4.32** ≥ 1) and **3 of 4 seeds are solvent**, the exception a −£0.2M dip (Greece, season 21) against a merge base that is solvent on only **1 of 4** with deficits to −£6.7M. The plausible mechanism for that one dip is the audit's own artifact rather than the feature — the user's club is unmanaged in a headless run, so it now *never* signs a trialist, is weaker than it used to be, and its results perturb the table that feeds academy form and signing order. **Stated as a hypothesis, not a finding: it was not isolated.** The reason to audit a user-only change at all is precisely that the user's club is in the league table, which is the one channel through which "user-only" leaks.
    - **Read solvency per seed and inversions only pooled.** A single-seed run applies the script's *mean* inversion gate to one sample and prints a confident "BROKEN" it disclaims in the next line ("under-powered below 3"); seed 4 alone reports two inversions that vanish pooled. Solvency is genuinely per-seed and is the column to read that way — the same split the audit's own header describes.
    - **Chunk long audits by seed when running them as background jobs.** A 4-seed run was killed twice mid-flight here; per-seed runs lose at most one seed's ~12 minutes, and the means can be pooled by hand afterwards.
  - **`ensureUserRosterSafety` had to grow two new sources, and the dynasty audit is what caught it.** It kept the user fieldable by promoting from `academyRoster` — which was *guaranteed* non-empty only because youth intake used to sign itself in. Once the user chooses, an ignored Youth Intake screen means an empty academy forever, the roster starves on retirements and expiries, `selectXI` leaves slots empty and `pickInterceptor` dereferences an undefined tackler: **a hard crash of the whole sim, not a soft degradation.** It now calls up from academy → this year's trial group → **free agents**, which also makes `ROSTER_SAFETY_FLOOR` a real guarantee rather than best-effort. The market fallback existed already for the GK branch and was simply missing from the general floor, where an empty academy quietly did nothing — reachable before this change too, by releasing your whole academy.
    - **A market call-up is a real arrival and is logged like one.** `ensureUserRosterSafety` returns `marketSignings` and its three callers (the offseason, `endAutopilot`, `switchClub`) write a fee-0 `FREE_AGENT_TID` transfer for each, plus `faSignedSeason` so the one-season hold applies. Club-by-season history is rebuilt from `league.transfers` alone (`teamForSeason`, the OVR chart's club colours), so an unrecorded free arrival keeps displaying whichever club last had a record for him — the precise bug the sentinel record exists to prevent. A promotion from the academy or the trial list is deliberately *not* reported: same club, so the owner such a record would establish is already correct.
    - **The fix's own first version had a duplicate-pid bug worth remembering.** `freeAgentPids` reads the *caller's* `teams`, which the promotion loop does not update, so a player called up on one pass still looked unsigned on the next and was promoted again. The roster then held one pid twice, `selectXI` could not fill a slot (a player may hold only one), and `improve` dereferenced the resulting null. **The general shape: any loop that promotes out of a derived pool must exclude what it has already taken, because the derivation is computed from state it isn't writing back.**
  - **Scout directions (`StoredTeam.scoutingRegions`/`scoutingPositions`, `scouting/scoutDirections.ts`, 2026-09-01).** Two things the user tells his youth scouts: where to look, and which positions to look for. **They do not reach equally far, and the asymmetry is forced rather than chosen** — a country is a post-hoc relabel of the whole trial group, while a position decides how a player is *generated* and so can only touch the extras. Set on one panel, saved by one action (`setScoutDirectionsAction`), read at one point in the offseason, and sanitized through `scoutDirectionsOf` on both the way in and the way out.
    - **Where they look (`scoutingRegions`).** Up to `SCOUTING_REGION_MAX` (3) countries supply `SCOUTING_REGION_SHARE` (0.6) of the user's trial group between them, the league's own mix supplying the rest. **A blend, never an override**, so the academy still produces local players and no setting can manufacture a single-nationality squad.
    - **Applied POST-HOC — the nationality and name are re-drawn on the trial stream after generation — and that is a requirement, not a style choice.** The user's *ordinary* intake is generated inside the main loop on the **shared** rng, and `drawFrom` spends one draw normally but **two** when the roll lands in the rest-of-world bucket; handing generation a different table would therefore change the shared draw count and shift every club generated after it. Re-drawing afterwards on `YOUTH_TRIAL_STREAM` cannot. It covers the whole group, ordinary intake included, because the setting should describe everyone the scouts turned up.
    - **Sound only because nationality is rating-neutral**, which CLAUDE.md already verifies elsewhere (regenerating a world after changing the tables leaves every country's starter mean OVR identical to the decimal): it feeds `generateName` and international eligibility and nothing else, so a re-nationalised player is the same footballer with a different passport. `scoutingRegions.test.ts` pins both halves directly — the group's OVRs are *identical* with and without scouting, and every player outside the group fingerprints identically. **If nationality ever gains a rating effect, this becomes a balance lever and needs an audit.**
    - The eligibility side is the actual hook, and it is why this is worth having rather than decoration: a player's nationality decides who can cap him, so scouting a country deepens that nation's pool — which matters now that a national team can be managed. The UI says outright that the scouts find *different* players and not better ones, because a control sitting beside a quality bonus invites the opposite reading.
    - `sanitizeScoutingRegions` drops any nation with no name pool, for the same reason `sanitizeNationalityWeights` does: `generateName` synthesizes nonsense words for an unknown nationality. The picker reuses `PICKABLE_NATIONALITIES`, so only nations with names (and mostly flags) are offered.
    - **Which positions (`scoutingPositions`, `SCOUT_POSITION_SHARE` 0.75), and this one reaches the SCOUTED EXTRAS ONLY — a hard constraint, not a design preference.** A position decides which `GEN_OFFSETS` tier row the ratings are rolled from, and `rollRating` spends **one** draw for an `ABS` tier against **two** for every other — so a keeper costs **23 rating draws and every outfield position 27** (measured; GK marks 5 skills ABS, everyone else 1). The user's *ordinary* intake is generated inside the world loop on the **shared** `rng`, so re-weighting that draw would shift the stream by four and re-roll every club generated after his. The extras are drawn on the trial stream, where it is free. **The share is 0.75 against the country blend's 0.6 precisely BECAUSE it reaches less of the group** — extras are ~5-9 of a 10-12 group, so a higher within-extras share lands the skew the user actually sees in the same place a country's does. Still a skew and not a filter, for the reason the country blend is one. `targetedPositionCdf` keeps it to exactly one draw, same as the plain `ROSTER_COMPOSITION` table.
    - **Measured, seed 4 (`scripts/scoutDirectionsProbe.ts`, the tool to run before touching either constant).** Targeting the three rarest positions (GK/DM/AM, two roster slots each) takes them from **30% of the group to 60%** — a doubling, and the number to size `SCOUT_POSITION_SHARE` against, since what the user sees is the *group* share rather than the within-extras one it is applied at. The probe also prints the group's OVRs with and without the skew, because steering positions must change which positions the group fills and not how good it is — a keeper and a striker are generated off the same academy base, so a systematic move there would mean the skew had become a balance lever rather than a preference.
    - **Containment is pinned directly.** `scoutDirections.test.ts` fingerprints every non-trial player in the world with both directions set against a run with none, and they are identical — that is the test that fails if positions ever stop being confined to the trial stream. `scoutingPositions` is `migrate.ts`-backfilled (`[]`, exact rather than a guess) and, like `scoutingRegions`, deliberately **not** cleared by `handToAI`/`beginAutopilot`: nothing reads them but the intake pass and only for the club matching `userTid`, so on an AI-run club they are inert rather than zombies.
  - **`academyFacilitiesBonus` (`players/academyFacilities.ts`) is the scouting/hype lever, user's club only.** `scoutingSpend` (the *locked* current-season figure, so the peek-then-lower dodge stays closed) and `hype`, each normalized to 0..1 and paying half of `YOUTH_TRIAL_SCOUTING_SWING + YOUTH_TRIAL_HYPE_SWING` (6 ovr at both maxed). **A bonus only, never a penalty**: the anchor already carries standing and `ACADEMY_FORM_SWING` already cuts both ways, so a third downward lever would stack three penalties on a struggling club's cheapest route back. Applied as an intake-time modifier beside `academyFormModifiers` and **never written into `academyBase`** — same reason the difficulty academy lever isn't (promotion convergence and roster-import realignment both read that field). Being one-sided and reaching one club of 420, it cannot move a league's mean, which is what lets it ship without a dynasty audit.
- **ROSTER_CAP** (30, `core/constants.ts`) blocks user FA signings/buys when full; youth intake/AI FA unaffected (AI trimmed to 25 each offseason).
- **"More minutes" flag** — user flags bench players via `StoredTeam.moreMinutes` (pids; user-only, `setMoreMinutes` in `core/lineup/moreMinutes.ts`, Roster bench-row toggle). Carried through `simThrough`→`leagueMatchData` onto each bench `MatchPlayer.minutesBoost`; in-match, a flagged player gets a `SUB_MINUTES_BOOST` ovr credit in the sub decision (helps him win the "who comes on" pick and clear the bench-quality gate) plus a dedicated boost-driven sub path in `attemptSub` that subs him for the weakest on-pitch player at his position. A nudge, not a force — the gate still blocks a clearly-worse flagged player. Migrated (backfills `[]`).

**Jump forward N seasons, club on autopilot** (`core/autopilot.ts`, `LeagueStore.aiManagedSeasons`, 2026-08-19)
- **The whole feature is one substitution: `meta.userTid` is swapped for `AUTOPILOT_TID` (-2) on a working copy.** Every AI system is already written as "each club except `userTid`" — market, free agency, renewals, `trimRosterSurplus`, `assignAIFormations`, `enforceDivisionCeilings`, `protectedStarPids`, `financeScaleFor`, the difficulty levers — so pointing `userTid` at nothing drops all of them onto the user's club, which is exactly what "the AI takes over" means. **No branch was added to `simThrough`/`simOffseason`, so RNG stream order for ordinary play is untouched** and a jumped season is what that world would have produced with the club under AI control all along. Any future carve-out that reads something *other* than `meta.userTid` to identify the user's club silently exempts it from the jump; `autopilot.test.ts` catches that by asserting the AI actually traded on the club's behalf.
- **The sentinel must never reach disk** (a save carrying it has an owner who manages no club), so `jumpSeasons` restores the real tid before returning and the whole jump commits in **one** write at the end. **-2, not -1**: -1 is `FREE_AGENT_TID`, a live value in `CompletedTransfer.fromTid`/`toTid`.
- `beginAutopilot` clears the user's `starters`/`transferListed`/`moreMinutes` and the league-level `negotiations`/`inboundOffers`/`loanListings`/`loanRejections`. **Clearing `starters` is the load-bearing one** — `resolveXI` keeps a stored array as long as it still *resolves*, so leaving it has the "AI" fielding the user's eleven for a decade, minus whoever got sold.
- `endAutopilot` repairs the two things that are user-club-only and so froze during the jump: `reconcileScoutingObserved` (kept first-seen for players who were already there, stamped at the return season for arrivals → new signings come back fogged, which is the honest reading) and `ensureUserRosterSafety` (a no-op in practice — the AI leaves a trimmed 25 — but it is the guarantee the club can field a keeper).
- **The control is a TopBar menu, not a Dashboard card (2026-09-01, user call)** — reachable from any page.
