  - **The cost is a second copy of the prize rules, and two things hold it to the sim.** Every *amount* comes from the sim's own helpers (`cupFormat().prizes`, `koPrizeByRound`, `domesticPrizeForRound`, `domesticCupScaleFor`) rather than being re-read from constants, so no retune of a prize can pass it by. The *rules* — which events pay at all — are pinned by `test/core/prizeIncome.test.ts`, which plays a whole English domestic cup through `playDomesticRound`, accumulates the `prizes` maps the sim actually returns, and asserts the derivation matches **per club and in aggregate**. Without that test the failure is silent and reaches a player as "my budget went up by more than the page says".
  - **Two edges worth knowing.** Participation is credited when league-phase round 0 is *played*, not at the draw, so a club that has qualified and not yet kicked off has banked nothing and the card must say so (pinned). And the domestic side must apply `domesticCupScaleFor`, not `financeScaleFor` — country-scaled, tier-flat — or a lower-division club's card under-reports the cheque it actually received; the test passes the real scale rather than a flat 1 for exactly that reason.

**Difficulty** (`DIFFICULTIES` in `constants.ts`, `LeagueStore.difficulty`, 2026-08-17)
- Four levels (easy/normal/hard/brutal) chosen on `/new-league`, **fixed for the save's lifetime**, migrated to `"normal"` for old saves. `normal`'s profile is every lever's identity value or the shipped constant, so an existing dynasty is provably unchanged (pinned by `difficulty.test.ts`).
- **Every lever is user-club-only, and that is a hard constraint rather than a convenience.** AI↔AI trading, the country strength ladder and the anti-inflation equilibrium are one tuned system. The specific trap: widening the protected-star gate *world-wide* on the hard levels is the obvious implementation and would cut AI↔AI deal volume, which lands straight on the documented tripwire (the weak leagues run on transfer receipts; the last change that reduced deal flow put 2 of 4 seeds into deficit with every strength check green). Because nothing reaches the AI, **no difficulty change needs a dynasty audit** — that property is worth preserving.
- Five levers and their seams: **money** — `financeScaleFor` (`finance/budget.ts`) is the *one* seam, wrapping `financeScale` and multiplying for `userTid` only; it must scale income **and** the savings ceiling, since `clampBudget` would otherwise destroy an easy level's bonus. **Academy** — an intake-time addend beside `academyFormModifiers` in `offseason.ts`, **never** written into `StoredTeam.academyBase` (promotion convergence would drag it back toward the competition centre and roster-import realignment would permute it onto another club). **Buy price** — `reservationPrice`'s new `priceScale`, whose single call site is `makeTransferOffer`, plus `scoutedValue` on the two buy-side display surfaces; both still clamp at the unscaled `MAX_TRANSFER_VALUE` so no level quotes a fantasy fee. **Market access** — `ProtectedStarBar` on `isProtectedStar`/`protectedStarPids`, defaulting to the shipped constants; only the four user-facing call sites pass `userProtectedStarBar(difficulty)`. **Fog** — a `fogScale` on `potentialFog` (half-width *and* clearing time), purely informational, no rng draw.
- **Opening budget scales the post-wage SURPLUS, every later season scales income.** Deliberately asymmetric: scaling the base allocation at creation starts a Brutal save at an expensive club already in the red before the user has made a decision, whereas a post-wage figure is positive by construction. From season 2 on `chargeSeasonStart` takes the scale normally and going broke becomes a consequence of how the club is run.
- **Going broke is reachable by choice, not by construction, and that's the calibration.** Measured (`scripts/difficultyProbe.ts` + the wage curve): a mid-table generated squad costs ~£18M/season against £88M base income, so nobody starts in trouble; but a 30-man squad averaging ovr 75 costs **£88.6M**, i.e. break-even on normal and a **~£18M/season deficit on hard** (£70.4M income), ~£36M on brutal. Beware measuring this in a headless audit — the unmanaged user club rots to academy stipends and understates its wage bill ~5×, which made the squeeze look far too weak on the first pass.
- Bankruptcy is survivable and already half-implemented by existing code: `makeTransferOffer` gates on budget so you simply can't buy, and `clampScoutingSpend`'s `Math.max(budget, 0)` switches scouting off, which widens the fog. Both are surfaced (Finance in-the-red alert, Dashboard warning); there is still **no debt mechanic** and open decision #2 is unchanged.
- **Difficulty diverges the rng stream** (it changes what the user can afford), so two levels on one seed are different worlds and cross-world counts are not comparable. `difficultyProbe.ts` therefore applies all four bars to a *single* simmed world; a probe that sims each level separately will report a moving AI-side count and look like a leak that isn't one.
- Copy gotcha: on hard levels a player can be unbuyable by the user while still moving between AI clubs, so the not-for-sale reason switches to **"Not available to you"** rather than claiming his club won't sell (`protectedStarReason` in `recommendations.ts`).

**World / leagues** (competitions-as-data)
- `core/competitions.ts` — `Competition{id,country,tier,name}`, persisted `LeagueStore.competitions`. Teams carry `compId` (not a raw division number). Every "div 0 vs 1" pair is a loop over competitions. Finance fns take a `scale`, looked up via `financeScale`.
- **Per-league tuning lives ON the `Competition`, and the player can add leagues (2026-08-21).** `Competition` gained four **optional** fields — `strengthOffset`, `academyOffset`, `budgetScale`, `continentalSlots` — resolved through accessors (`competitionStrengthOffset`/`competitionAcademyOffset`/`competitionBudgetScale`/`isWeakLeague`/`academyBaseCenterOf`) that fall back to the `COUNTRY_*` tables. **Absent ≠ defaulted**: every shipped competition leaves them unset, so the default world and every existing save read exactly as before and there is **no migration**. The record was the right home precisely because `LeagueStore.competitions` is already per-save persisted state — no new settings store, nothing threaded through `src/core`. `generateWorld`/`createLeagueState` now take the table (defaulting to `worldCompetitions()`); `buildCompetitions(LeagueSpec[])`/`worldLeagueSpecs()` assemble one, and a test pins that rebuilding the shipped specs returns `worldCompetitions()` byte-for-byte (the "leave an untouched knob absent" rule is what makes that hold). UI is `ui/components/WorldSetup.tsx` on `/new-league`, **in every mode, roster import included (2026-08-28)** — see the roster-mode entry below for why it used to be hidden there and what had to change first. `worldTuningWarnings` is advisory-only and flags the weaker-but-richer inversion, plus (since names became editable) two divisions sharing one name, which would make a roster file aimed at that name fill only one of them.
  - **Division names are player-set (`LeagueSpec.d1Name`/`d2Name`, exposed 2026-08-28).** The fields already existed and `worldLeagueSpecs()` already filled them from the shipped table; what was missing was any control, so a division was always "`<country>` Division N". That is not cosmetic: `resolveRosterSlots` matched a world-wide roster file's competitions **by name only**, so a file written for "Eredivisie" could never apply to any world the game could build. Renaming is offered on shipped rows behind a per-row toggle (`renaming`, a `Set` of entry ids) and inline on added ones. `buildCompetitions` treats an all-whitespace name as absent (`?.trim() ||`), so emptying the box reverts to following the country rather than producing a blank league name.
    - **The other half of that coin was a trap, and matching now falls back to COUNTRY + TIER (2026-08-31, user ask).** A name is the one identifier the player can invalidate, and renaming was the *documented, encouraged* thing to do — so renaming England's top flight to "Premier League" silently cost you every file written for "English Division 1", which is **every file the EA FC converter has ever produced**, i.e. the Download Real Rosters file itself. The user reported it as "I want to rename the leagues and still import the real players file". `resolveRosterSlots` now tries three routes per file competition, most authoritative first: an explicit `country`/`tier` on the entry (new optional fields, parsed strictly, emitted by `buildRosterFile` and the converter), then the literal name, then **the country and tier the name DESCRIBES** (`competitionRefFromName`). A country is not renameable on a shipped league, so the third route is what carries an untouched file across any rename.
    - **`competitionRefFromName` reads names back through `worldCompetitions()`, NOT a hand-written demonym table.** Shipped names are adjectival ("Dutch Division 1" is the Netherlands'), so the mapping is real data rather than derivable from the country string — but it is data the game already ships, so building the lookup from the competition table means adding a country puts its name in for free and the two cannot drift. The second form, `"<country> Division <n>"`, is what an *added* league is called by default and is handled by regex. Anything else ("Eredivisie") returns null and matches by name alone, which is correct: nothing in that string says which country it is, and that is precisely what the new `country` field is for.
    - **Two consequences worth knowing.** (a) `RosterSlotWorld` now needs `country`/`tier` on its competitions — satisfied structurally by both `LeagueStore` and `worldTeamSlots`' caller, so no call site changed, but a hand-built fixture must carry them. (b) A competition can be **claimed once**: two entries reaching the same division (now possible under two different names) would both write onto the same slots with the later silently winning, so the first claim holds and the collision is warned. `retargetRosterFile` **strips** any stated country/tier, since leaving "England, tier 1" on an entry now aimed at Neverland would send it straight back to England.
  - **`academyOffset` is split from `strengthOffset` in the engine but DELIBERATELY NOT EXPOSED in the UI (user call, 2026-08-21).** `generateDivisionTeams` computed a single `base` and used it *both* to generate players and as the club's permanent `academyBase`, so "how good is this league now" and "what does it keep producing" were literally the same number. They're now separate params; the strength spread is still drawn and shuffled **once** and both bases derive from it, so rng consumption is unchanged and — since `competitionAcademyOffset` falls back to `strengthOffset` — leaving it unset reproduces the old behaviour exactly. It works: measured on `scripts/customWorldProbe.ts`, a league generated 1.87 OVR *below* its neighbour finished 1.44 *above* it after 3 seasons on academies alone, and it is safe to set because `academyBase` is fixed-at-generation by design (it's the anti-inflation anchor, so setting it picks an equilibrium rather than opening a ratchet). **The control was cut because a second 0-20 slider beside Strength made the player do arithmetic to work out which way their league was pointing** — the mechanic was fine, the affordance wasn't. If it comes back, make it a *direction* ("rising / steady / fading") and derive the offset, rather than a second absolute scale. The field, the accessor, the generation split and their tests all stay; `newLeagueEntry` simply leaves it unset.
  - **Continental slot counts are per league, which forced the Shield's offset to be derived.** `CupFormat.strongOffset`/`weakOffset` were constants duplicating the Cup's slot counts ("change one and you must change the other"). That breaks the moment a league sets its own count, so `cupOffsetForCompetition` now sums the slots of every competition above it in `CONTINENTAL_ORDER` — fields butt up exactly, can't overlap or gap, and inserting a third competition shifts everything below it automatically.
  - **`isValidCupFieldSize`/`largestValidCupField` (`constants.ts`) — a real crash this exposed.** The league-phase draw needs the field to split into 2 pots that are *each even* (intra-pot rounds are perfect matchings within a pot) and big enough for `CUP_LEAGUE_PHASE_GAMES/POTS`, plus ≥ `CUP_MIN_FIELD` (12 — held where it has always been, so which worlds get a competition at all is unchanged; the draw and `cupKnockoutPlan` would both cope with 8). `drawLeaguePhase`'s own guard **omitted the even-pot condition**, and `cupPlan` only checked the minimum — unreachable while every field was a shipped size, but a custom world producing 23 qualifiers threw inside `simOffseason`. `cupPlan` now trims to the largest buildable size and `qualifyCupTeams` slices **after seeding** (drops the world's weakest qualifiers, not an arbitrary league's). Shipped world still lands on 24/16 exactly, so nothing there changed.
  - **Per-league roster import (`retargetRosterFile`, `leagueRosterFiles`).** An added league's divisions did not exist when any roster file was authored, so nothing in a file can name them and `resolveRosterSlots` would skip every competition as unrecognised. `retargetRosterFile` rewrites a file's `match` values onto one league's division names **positionally** (first competition → tier 1), the same rule clubs already follow onto slots; `WorldEntry.rosterSources` holds the files and `leagueRosterFiles` retargets them at build time, **not** at pick time, because the division names follow the country name and the player can rename the league after loading. The result is folded through `combineRosterFiles` → `applyRosterFileToNewLeague` like any other import, so slot resolution, the picker preview and crest suppression are shared. Retargeting is non-mutating and drops (with a warning) any competition past the league's division count.
  - **The world editor is available in roster mode, and the fix that allowed it was to stop storing the resolution (2026-08-28).** It was hidden on `?roster=1` for a real reason: `NewLeague` held the loaded file **already resolved onto slots** (`useState<LoadedRoster>`), so reshaping the world underneath it left `byTid` pointing at clubs that had moved. That is the wrong half to fix by hiding a panel — **the world editor is exactly what a player needs when a file doesn't fit**, since `resolveRosterSlots` skips any competition the world hasn't got by that name, and adding or renaming a league is the only way to make such a file apply at all. The page now holds **only `NamedRosterFile[]`** and derives every resolution (`useMemo`) against the world as it currently stands, so a reshape re-aims the file instead of misaiming it. Two consequences: the two roster sources are no longer mutually exclusive, so `activeRoster` **combines** them (world-wide first, per-league last, because a league carrying its own file is the more specific claim on the competition it names — `combineRosterFiles` gives the later file the win); and a world change that alters the **slot layout** clears `selectedTid` (`changeWorld` compares the tid→compId layout before and after), because otherwise the club you picked in the list is not the club the save starts you at. A rename or a strength/money retune leaves the layout alone and keeps the selection. World-wide roster files can also be loaded from the plain `/new-league` screen now, so the two entry points differ only in which screen they open on.
  - **The world editor CARD now collapses, and it must default open in roster mode (`WorldSetup`'s `defaultOpen`, 2026-08-30).** Twelve shipped countries is the tallest block on a page whose job is picking a club, and most saves take the world as it ships, so it is shut by default and the header carries a summary of the world it would build (`N countries, N divisions, N clubs`) — which is the whole reason collapsing is safe, since you can see what you are getting without opening it. **Two things would otherwise go silently missing.** (a) `worldTuningWarnings` renders *inside* the body, so a shut card would swallow advice the editor raised; the header counts them as a badge. (b) A roster file naming a league this world hasn't got is skipped entirely and the editor is the only cure, per the entry above — so `NewLeague` passes `defaultOpen={!!worldRoster}` and an import opens it. Collapsing it there would hide the fix behind a card the warning merely points at. Gotcha for tests: `worldSetupRender.test.tsx`'s harness passes `defaultOpen: true`, because every case in it is about what the editor *contains* and a shut card renders only its header; the shut state has its own block at the bottom of that file.
  - **The club picker shows ONE division at a time (2026-08-30).** Forty club rows for a big country buried everything under the picker, and the two divisions are alternatives rather than one list to read end to end. Tabs carry the real division names, so a renamed or added league labels its own. Three things worth knowing: a one-division country renders no tabs and the shown tier **falls back** rather than showing an empty list (same shape as `activeCountry` falling back when a country is switched off underneath the picker); switching division deliberately does **not** clear `selectedTid` the way switching *country* must, so a line reports which division the pick is in, because an invisible selection reads as no selection with Start live at the bottom; and the tier is a **view**, never save state — which division a club plays in is still read off its tid via `tierForTid`.
  - **Slider anchors are measured, not extrapolated (`scripts/strengthAnchorProbe.ts`).** Generates every strength rung 0-20 in one world so they're directly comparable, and reports division mean OVR, best/worst club rating and best player per rung. Confirms ~0.94 OVR per point and gives the cross-league anchors the Manual and the settings guide quote (e.g. strength 9 → best club ≈ 67, about a mid-table big-four side; strength 20 → best club 54, below *every* big-four club).
  - **Division count (1 to 3) and size (even, `MIN_DIVISION_TEAMS` 8 to `MAX_DIVISION_TEAMS` 20) are per league (2026-08-22, deepened 2026-08-30).** `Competition.teamCount` + `competitionTeamCount`; `LeagueSpec.divisions`/`d1Teams`/`d2Teams`/`d3Teams`. A country's divisions are a **chain**, not a pair — see the N-deep pyramid entry under Promotion/relegation. `worldTeamSlots`/`countryClubRanges` **no longer take team counts** (they derive them per competition), which is why the club-identity lookup had to be country-anchored first: a country with a different number of divisions shifts every later country's tids by a whole division.
    - **A smaller division SPREADS across the fixed 38-matchday grid rather than finishing early** (`spreadOverSeason` in `leagueState.ts`): round r of R maps to `round(r * SEASON_MATCHDAYS / R)`. At R=38 that is the identity, so **the shipped world is untouched** (pinned by the `touchStats` hash and a test asserting matchdays 1..38 exactly). Spreading is what keeps the rest of the calendar meaningful for a short season — the winter window still opens mid-season, deadline day still has games left, the run-in still lines up with the continental finals. `MAX_DIVISION_TEAMS` is 20 because 2(n-1) ≤ 38 and `matchdayToMonth` **throws** outside the grid; going past it means moving the cup matchday tables too.
    - **Blank matchdays broke the injury/ban tick, exactly as CLAUDE.md warned they would.** Both count in MATCHES and both decremented once per matchday for *everyone*, which is exact only while every club plays every matchday. `applyInjuries`/`applySuspensions` now take a `played(pid)` predicate; `simThrough` builds it as **"who did NOT play"** so free agents (on no roster, no fixtures to attach to) keep ticking on the global clock as before, and the shipped world's set is empty. Both default to "everyone played", so no other caller changed.
    - `updateHype` takes the club's own division size — finishing 8th of 8 has to score like bottom, not like 8th of 20. Defaults to `NUM_TEAMS`.
  - **Club identities are anchored to a country, never to a tid.** `CLUBS` is indexed by tid, so switching a country off (or adding one ahead of another) shifts every later country's tids and a tid lookup hands Spain's clubs England's names. `clubIdentitiesFor(country, count)` (shipped block, else `generateClubIdentities` from `teams/clubNames.ts`) is shared by `assignIdentities` **and** the new-league picker — they must derive names the same way or the save renames every club on open. The generator is deterministic from `(country, count)` on its **own** seeded stream, which is what lets the picker preview an added league before a world exists.
- `worldCompetitions()` = **36 competitions / 626 clubs**: England, Spain, Italy, Germany (equal siblings) + **France, Netherlands, Portugal, Belgium, Turkey, Greece, Scotland & Serbia (deliberately weaker + poorer)**, **three divisions each, every country** via `COUNTRY_STRENGTH_OFFSET`/`COUNTRY_BUDGET_SCALE` (`constants.ts`). **The table's order is generation order, not the ladder** — appending a COUNTRY is what keeps existing countries' players byte-identical (verified: adding four countries left all eight prior leagues' generated OVR identical), so the Netherlands is appended after Turkey while sitting above Portugal on the ladder. **Adding a DIVISION is not the same thing** and does shift tids: see the third-division entry below.
  - **Division sizes are the REAL ones, not a uniform 20 (2026-08-28).** England/Spain/Italy 20, Germany/France/Portugal/Turkey/Netherlands 18, Belgium/Serbia 16, Greece 14, Scotland 12, with second tiers to match (down to Scotland's 10) — set per competition via `Competition.teamCount`, so the world is **626 clubs / 15,650 players / 10,538 fixtures** (420 / 10,500 / 7,092 before third divisions). Four things worth knowing. **(a) `MAX_DIVISION_TEAMS` is 20 and that is a calendar fact, not a preference**: a double round robin of n clubs needs 2(n-1) matchdays against a fixed 38-matchday grid, which is why England's real 24-club second tier is 20 here. Sizes must also be **even** (the circle-method fixture generator leaves an odd field unpaired). **(b) A smaller division SPREADS across the same grid** rather than finishing early (`spreadOverSeason`), so the winter window, both continental competitions and the run-in still line up — and the blank matchdays that creates are exactly why `applyInjuries`/`applySuspensions` take a `played(pid)` predicate. **(c) `worldLeagueSpecs()` is DERIVED from `worldCompetitions()` and must carry `d1Teams`/`d2Teams`/`d3Teams`/`divisions`/`promotionSpots` back**, or `buildCompetitions(worldLeagueSpecs())` stops reproducing the shipped table, which a test pins. Fields equal to the default stay absent, which is what makes that round trip exact. **(d) `CLUBS` must be trimmed to match**, because `SHIPPED_RANGES = countryClubRanges(worldCompetitions())` slices that array by the derived ranges — leaving the surplus entries in place would hand every country after the first shrunken one its predecessor's tail. Each country's block is its divisions' slots in tier order, so a shrink drops the tail of *each division*, not the tail of the block.
  - **EVERY country runs THREE divisions (2026-08-30), and the symmetry is the load-bearing part.** Sizes: the big four 20 in all three (`NUM_TEAMS_D3` by omission — the real size of a German 3. Liga even though the two above it hold 18, and the calendar cap for the other three); France/Portugal/Turkey/Netherlands 18, Belgium/Serbia 16, Greece 12, Scotland 10. Everything mechanical falls out of the N-deep pyramid work (see Promotion/relegation); what is specific to *this* world change is below.
    - **Giving a third tier to only SOME countries makes those countries stronger, and it broke the ladder.** It shipped that way first (big four only) and the 20-season 4-seed audit caught it. Measured against a control that varies ONLY the world (same N-deep code, 420 clubs): **BIG4 D1 at s21 57.8 → 60.3, BIG4→France +2.74 → +7.35, BIG4→Serbia +7.06 → +11.60**, against a documented design target of **~+3.4** for BIG4→France. Generation is identical on both (BIG4 64.5) — the gap opens over the dynasty. A three-division country has 50% more clubs producing and developing players and `enforceDivisionCeilings` pumps the best of them upward, so it drains 60 clubs where a two-tier one drains 40; and having more talent at home it buys less from the weak leagues, whose economies run on those receipts. **Symmetry removes the cause rather than compensating for it: with every country three deep, BIG4→France comes back to ~+3.4 on all four seeds.** The general lesson is that *world composition is a strength lever* — adding clubs to a country is not a neutral depth change.
    - **Solvency is a genuine, accepted cost, and three hypotheses about it were wrong before the right one.** Deficit rate went **0/42 sampled points on the 420-club control → 4-6/42 on the 626-club world, worst 2 of 625 clubs (0.32%) at once**, in the TOP FLIGHTS of the poorest countries, seasons 15-21. Ruled out by measurement, in order: **(a) tier-3 poverty** — the broke club is tier 1, not tier 3; **(b) re-roll noise** — the control is cleanly 0/42 on both seeds, not a coin flip; **(c) a size-dependent estimator** — the *rate* rose too, not just the min. What it actually is: the pre-existing weak-league wage ratchet, modestly amplified. `scripts/sweepCostProbe.ts` measures it — over 15 seasons the poorest top flights' wage-bill-to-base-income ratio runs **0.183 → 0.496 on the control and 0.189 → 0.587 with third divisions (+18%)**, with **+24% more forced upward moves** (520 → 645 cumulative). So the ceiling sweep does contribute (it is a FORCED buy: the fee is capped at what the club can pay, the arriving player's WAGES are not), but the ratchet is overwhelmingly pre-existing and nearly triples on its own. **Accepted (user call): 1-2 clubs of 626 in the red late in a long save, on a mechanism that predates this.** Fixing it properly means attacking the wage ratchet itself, which is its own project.
    - **`financeScale` COMPOUNDS country scale × tier scale, and that is a real latent problem even though it is not what caused the deficits above.** Wages are country-independent while income is scaled twice, so six leagues now sit below anything the wage curve was ever validated against: **Serbian D3 0.126 / Scottish D3 0.133 / Greek D3 0.140 / Turkish D3 0.144 / Belgian D3 0.162 / Portuguese D3 0.180, against a previously-tuned floor of 0.210 (Serbian D2) — the poorest is 1.67x poorer than anything tuned.** A floor on the combined scale is the obvious fix and it is untaken; the deficits are in tier 1, so it would not have fixed them.
    - **Ids are renumbered INLINE, not appended, and that is forced by two things.** `CompetitionSelect` groups by country and filters the table in **array order**, so a division listed out of place shows out of order in every competition dropdown in the game; and `buildCompetitions(worldLeagueSpecs())` hands out ids sequentially, so an out-of-place id breaks the round-trip test outright. England still holds ids 0 and 1. A test pins that each country's block is contiguous and in tier order.
    - **`CLUBS` grew by 206, INSERTED into each country block rather than appended.** `SHIPPED_RANGES = countryClubRanges(worldCompetitions())` slices that array by derived ranges, so appending would hand Spain England's names. Two collision classes, both measured rather than assumed: **14 of the big four's first 80 abbreviations collided** with the shipped set, and the weak countries' first pass **reused ~60 towns** their existing clubs already hold, because the smaller countries had already spent their obvious ones. Check the TOWN, not just the full name, and resolve abbreviations against the taken set programmatically.
    - **The domestic cup is now at its ceiling.** The largest field is **60** (England/Spain/Italy) needing **6** rounds against `DOMESTIC_CUP_MATCHDAYS`' **6** slots. A country past 64 clubs overflows it, and `buildDomesticCup` answers that by returning **null** — no cup at all for that country, silently. Check this before deepening any pyramid further.
    - **Perf was measured, not assumed** (`scripts/marketTiming.ts`, season 2, best of 5, same probe on both tables): **420 clubs 1745ms / 866 deals → 500 clubs 2281ms / 983 deals**, i.e. 1.19x the clubs for 1.31x the time — an exponent near **1.5**, not the ~5 the old 240→320 figure in this file implies. **Re-measure rather than extrapolating from either figure.**
    - **Two UI sites assumed exactly two tiers and one of them was silently wrong**: Frivolities rendered `tier === 2 ? "D2" : "D1"`, labelling a third-tier season **"D1"**; `DivisionBadge` collapsed everything below the top flight into one colour. Both read the tier now. Grep for `tier === 2` before adding a fourth.
    - **A third tier lands on a band the game already ships.** Rostered OVR min/p10/mean/max on a fresh world: English **D1 50/57/64.5/81**, **D2 36/42/50.5/67**, **D3 21/28/36.2/52** — and Serbian **D2 is 21/27/35.5/51**, i.e. an English third division is almost exactly the existing bottom of the world. The accepted cost is the academies: tier-3 `youthGenerationBase` is **1.00-1.50**, fully saturated on the softplus floor, so every third-tier club produces near-identical youth. A third-division save is a transfer-market game, not an academy game.
    - **D2/D3 converging is ACCEPTED (user call), and the audit encodes that.** At 30 seasons D3's strongest team reached 52.1 against D2's average 51.0, from a correct 45.4 vs 50.5 at generation — deeper tiers erode faster for the same reason the bottom of the COUNTRY ladder does (`growthDamping` throttles growth above an absolute ovr, so a tier further below it grows undamped). `divisionAudit` therefore fails only at the **D1/D2** boundary and gates deeper ones on **mean ordering** instead, which still has real headroom (D1 56.5 / D2 50.9 / D3 44.1). Note `divisionRefusalOvr(3)` = 62 barely bites anyway, since D3's best player is 61.
  - **Real division sizes exposed a pre-existing bug that uniform 20-club divisions had hidden: the free-agency queue was ordered by RAW POINTS across the whole world (`freeAgencySigningOrder`, 2026-08-29).** The offseason's step 4 is "worst club picks first" over one world-wide queue, and it sorted by `points`. A 12-club league plays 22 games, a 20-club one 38, so at equal quality the small league's clubs carried ~58% of the points and sat at the front of the queue whatever they were worth — and the pool drains by quality, so picking first is the entire prize. **Symptom:** on the coefficient audit, Scotland (12 clubs, generated second-weakest in the world) finished 20 seasons with the **highest tier-1 mean OVR of any country**, above Germany, taking Cup places off England, on both seeds. **The first hypothesis was wrong and is worth recording:** prize money (`successPayout`'s fixed rank cutoffs paid a 10-club division's *every* club top-10 money). That was real and was fixed, but moved the symptom **not at all** — 59.3/60.9 → 59.2/59.3 — because money was never the channel. **What found it was a controlled probe, `scripts/divisionSizeProbe.ts`:** six countries identical in strength, budget, promotion and nationality, differing only in club count, in twin pairs so every reading has a check. Whole-roster drift over 15 seasons ran **−1.2 / −5.3 / −8.6** at 12 / 16 / 20 clubs, monotonic, twins agreeing to within a point — and attributing it (OVR moves only in progression, so a league's *retained* players' mean change is pure progression and the remainder is churn) put **all** of it in churn, with progression running the *other* way. Churn was free agency: the same ~4.4 arrivals per club everywhere, averaging **39 OVR at 12 clubs against 26 at 20**, tracking the raw-points sort key exactly. That gap is also where the sub-40 squad filler came from (5 per club at 12 against 10 at 20) — big leagues signing junk into their holes, not small leagues developing talent. **Fix is points per game.** Within one division every club has played the same games, so ppg order *is* raw-points order and **a uniform-size world is byte-identical**; only mixed-size worlds move. With it, the probe's spread collapses to **2.8 / 1.4** on the two seeds with sizes fully interleaved, FA arrivals flat at 29–31 everywhere, and the 20-club leagues *improve* while the 12-club ones fall to meet them — the pool's quality is conserved and shared fairly, which is what a queue fix should look like. **It was the only cross-competition raw-points sort in the sim**, verified by grep: hype normalizes by `row.played`, academy form by `rows.length`, the market and loans by surplus, the D2 sweep is per-country, and the display layer already used ppg. **General lesson, fourth time in this file: a quantity that is comparable within a division is not comparable across divisions of different lengths.** Points, appearances (`FULL_SEASON_APPEARANCES` is 30 against a 22-round season — progression's `minutesFactor` is the next thing this will bite, currently masked because it pushes the other way), goals, anything counted per season. Any new cross-competition ranking must normalize by games first. **Verified on the original symptom** (`scripts/coefficientAudit.ts`, 20 seasons × 2 seeds, same seeds as the failing run): Scotland's D1 mean OVR **59.3 / 60.9 → 51.7 / 50.4**, holding 2 Cup places on both seeds (was 4), no deficit, audit OK where it had FAILed on both — and the whole ladder reads as designed, big four 56–60 > France 55 > Netherlands 52–54 > Portugal/Belgium 51–53 > the bottom block 50–52. The Netherlands earning a 3rd/4th place on both seeds is the coefficient doing what it is for: it is the genuine 5th-strongest league.
  - **Variable division sizes broke three tests, and only one was fixture drift.** Worth knowing because each is a different class. **(a)** `worldIntegration` asserted "every competition still has exactly 20 teams after the swap" — now `competitionTeamCount(comp)`, which is the stronger claim anyway (promotion must preserve *each* division's own size). **(b)** `domesticCupIntegration` assumed every country's cup starts on `DOMESTIC_CUP_MATCHDAYS[0]` and reaches a Round of 32. It doesn't, and that is correct behaviour: rounds needed is `ceil(log2(field))` and a shorter cup takes the **last** n matchdays so every final still lands together, so Belgium and Serbia (32 clubs — an exact power of two, hence *no* preliminary round) don't kick off until matchday 9. **(c)** The M4 points gate pooled the whole world into one table and read the best and worst club in it. Points are a function of games played, so the world's worst club became whoever sits bottom of the *shortest* season (Scotland's 10-club D2, 18 games) and the gate read **8.8 against a floor of 10 while every league was healthy**. Now measured **per competition, scaled to a 38-game season, then averaged**: 78.9 / 27.3 against the untouched 70-100 and 10-38 bands. Fourth instance of the same lesson as the M3 top-scorer and M1 benchmark gates — **fix the statistic, don't widen the band** — and the first where *division length*, not sample size or sampling position, was what made the estimator invalid.
  - **Promotion counts scale with the divisions they connect** (`Competition.promotionSpots`): 3 for England/Spain/Italy/Germany/France/Turkey, 2 for Portugal/Belgium/Netherlands/Greece/Serbia, 1 for Scotland. Three up out of a 10-club second tier would churn a third of it a season and leave `enforceDivisionCeilings` far more relegated squads to sweep.
  - **The country count MUST stay even, and this is a hard structural constraint, not a preference (2026-08-28).** Both continental fields derive from it — Continental Cup = `2C + 8`, Continental Shield = `2C` — and a field the league-phase draw can build must be a **multiple of 4** (two pots, each even, each perfectly matched). An odd `C` puts *both* on `4n+2`, so both get trimmed and clubs that qualified on league position are cut: measured at C=11, the Cup wants 30 and gets 28, the Shield wants 22 and gets 20. There is no uniform slot rule that rescues an odd count — the arithmetic forces weak-league slots to a multiple of 4, i.e. a 44-club Cup. **Add countries in pairs.** This is why Greece went in alongside Serbia rather than Serbia alone. Weakness is invisible in a country's own matches (per-competition z-norm) — it bites only in finance (selling leagues) and the Cup (shared baseline).
- **Strength ladder is compressed, not literal (2026-08-08; Netherlands/Greece/Scotland/Serbia added 2026-08-28).** Offsets France 5 / Netherlands 8 / Portugal 10 / Belgium 11 / Turkey 12 / Greece 13 / Scotland 14 / Serbia 15 follow the real UEFA five-year coefficient *ordering*; the real gaps below France are only a few coefficient points, so mapping them literally would put Belgium ~17 and Turkey ~30 and generate unplayable squads. Each offset point ≈ **0.94 OVR** off a league's starters (measured). **`COUNTRY_BUDGET_SCALE` MUST stay monotonic with `COUNTRY_STRENGTH_OFFSET`** (France .7 > Netherlands .6 > Portugal .5 > Belgium .45 > Turkey .4 > Greece .38 > Scotland .35 > Serbia .32) — **a richer league climbs the ladder over a dynasty, so a weaker-but-richer league overtakes a stronger-but-poorer one.** Measured, not theoretical: Belgium/Turkey generate within 0.9 OVR of each other (a near-matched pair that isolates budget from everything else), and at Belgium .35 / Turkey .5 the ladder **inverted by 2.23 OVR over 20 seasons** — Turkey, generated weakest, finished *above* Belgium. Real squad values do invert here (Belgian Pro League €0.98B < Süper Lig €1.27B while Belgium outranks Turkey on the pitch); that is deliberately **not** modelled — the engine can't hold "weak but rich", and the ladder is load-bearing while the flavour isn't. **Gotcha when measuring this:** comparing leagues with very different starting OVR (England vs Portugal) hides the budget signal entirely behind progression's mean-reversion — an earlier attribution run did exactly that and wrongly concluded money was a weak lever. Compare near-equal leagues.
- **Weak-league solvency holds, and an AI wage-affordability gate was built for it and then removed as provably inert (2026-08-11).** History worth keeping, because the same fix will look attractive again. A deficit was reported, a `staysSolvent` gate was added to every AI buy path (transfer/loan/free agency) to project the next season-start charge with the incoming salary on the books, and it was measured "near-neutral, deficits 0 → 0". **All three of those measurements were taken on a market that could not sell a club's best player** (the `AI_MARKET_AVAILABILITY` bug — see the AI↔AI market entry). Once that was fixed, re-measurement showed: (a) *with* the gate, on the pre-fix market, **4 AI clubs still went negative on seed 1** (worst −£5.96M, first at **season 12**) — the gate never prevented the thing it was for, and the "0 → 0" claim survived only because it had been re-validated with a **5-season** spot check after the fee rescale; (b) on the fixed market, deleting the gate changes **nothing that matters**: 0 deficits across 4 seeds either way, and on 3 of those 4 seeds the entire 20-season dynasty comes out **byte-identical**. Identical output is a *proof* of zero firings on those seeds, not a coincidence — RNG stream order is load-bearing and a declined signing skips a contract-length draw, so one firing moves every downstream number. **It is therefore inconsequential rather than literally inert, and the distinction was nearly missed:** seed 3 *does* differ (France→Portugal +0.24 → +0.31), i.e. the gate fired there at least once and still prevented no deficit. An earlier version of this entry generalised "byte-identical" from the two seeds it had been measured on into a claim that the gate never fires at all; a fourth seed contradicted it. **Measure the seed you are about to generalise from.** **The real mechanism was never wages:** with the market shut, a club could not sell its best (and highest-paid) player, so wage bills ratcheted with no relief valve — which surfaced as insolvency in the finance column and a collapsing ladder in the ratings column, one cause with two symptoms. **Before rebuilding this gate, first check whether the market can still shed wages.**
  - **Margins, measured.** Opening budgets have real headroom: poorest club £15.8M (Belgium, seed 1) / £12.6M (Turkey, seed 2), via `scripts/genBudgetProbe.ts`. The thin point is *after season 1 is played* — £0.01M / £0.06M (Turkey) — driven by in-season spending, and it is the AI market's cash reserve that holds it above zero.
  - **The structural asymmetry is real even without a gate**: `financeScale` multiplies *income* (`BASE_SEASON_BUDGET`, prize payouts, hype revenue) while `seasonSalaryForOvr` is country-independent. Weak leagues stay solvent because `COUNTRY_STRENGTH_OFFSET` hands them cheaper squads *and* the market keeps draining their expensive players upward — nothing links those knobs, so re-check solvency after any change to either.
  - **Two audit methodology bugs found on the way, both fixed and both worth remembering:** (a) `weakLeaguesAudit.ts` sampled the budget only at generation and at the end while reporting "min AI budget over dynasty", so a mid-dynasty dip read as solvent — it now samples both points every season; (b) sampling *only* after `simOffseason` misses the true minimum, which lands **post-season** (after the winter window, before season-end settlement), not post-offseason.
- **Only rungs ≥2 offset points apart get a PER-SEED order gate; the one-point block is gated on the cross-seed mean (2026-08-28).** With eight weak rungs the bottom six sit one point apart — below the resolution the ladder has after a dynasty — so gating every adjacent pair per seed started failing on seed noise alone on a world whose every *mean* was healthy (Turkey→Greece ran +1.31/+1.69/**−2.27**/+0.25, mean +0.25). `scripts/weakLeaguesAudit.ts` now applies the per-seed gross-inversion gate only where the design claims separation (`RESOLVABLE_STEP` = 2: BIG4→France, France→Netherlands, Netherlands→Portugal) and gates the converged block on mean order plus block spreads. **`SEED_INVERSION_TOLERANCE` was NOT loosened** — untouched, and still fires on any rung meant to hold apart; `isConvergedRung` reads the offsets, so widening a rung back to a 2-point step re-enables its per-seed gate automatically instead of needing a hand-maintained list.
- **Adjacent weak rungs are NOT separately resolvable across a dynasty.** Portugal/Belgium/Turkey are one offset point apart (≈0.9 OVR) and ~40% of that erodes, so they end within a few tenths (measured seed 1: 49.6 / 49.6 / 49.3) and their mutual order coin-flips on seed noise. The audit therefore gates *ordering* plus block-level spreads, **not** per-rung magnitudes — asserting the latter would be asserting precision the design doesn't have. If rungs ever need to be individually resolvable, widen to 2-point offset steps rather than tightening the audit.
  - **A 2-point step does NOT buy a surviving gap at the BOTTOM of the ladder — erosion scales with depth, and this revises the advice above (measured 2026-08-28, 4 seeds × 20 seasons).** Scotland went in at offset 14 against Turkey's 12, a deliberate 2-point step taken precisely because the line above says to widen when a rung needs to be resolvable. It generates exactly as intended — and then converges away: **Turkey→Scotland ends `+0.02 / +0.37 / −1.26 / +1.94`, mean +0.27, straddling zero**, i.e. exactly the coin-flip the one-point rungs already have.
    - **The same 2-point step higher up survives on every seed**, which is what identifies the mechanism as ladder *position* rather than step size: **Netherlands→Portugal ends `+1.21 / +2.98 / +1.22 / +1.97`, mean +1.85, positive on all four.** Mean erosion of the gap to the big four runs Portugal 37% → Turkey 41% → **Scotland 48%**, so the deeper a rung sits the faster it climbs back — `growthDamping` throttles growth above an *absolute* ovr, so a league further under that line is less damped. (Directional, not clean: seed 4 has Scotland eroding *less* than Turkey. The gap outcome is the reliable signal, not the per-seed erosion rate.)
    - **So: widening the step is not a general fix.** It works in the middle of the weak block and is progressively eaten toward the bottom. Resolving a bottom rung would need a step scaling with depth (~16 for Scotland), which runs into the unplayable-squad floor. The honest position — and what the audit gates — is that the bottom of the ladder is **ordered at generation and converged by season 20**. Don't chase it with bigger offsets. Greece (13) and Serbia (15) were placed knowing this: they are correct at generation and expected to bunch with Turkey/Scotland by season 20.
    - Generation on seed 1, tier-1 starter means across all twelve: England 67.30 / Spain 67.50 / Italy 67.32 / Germany 67.39 / France 62.27 / **Netherlands 59.32** / Portugal 57.20 / Belgium 56.08 / Turkey 55.14 / **Greece 54.35** / **Scotland 53.32** / **Serbia 52.65**. Strictly ordered, and every pre-existing country is byte-identical to before the additions.
    - **No AI club is ever insolvent on any audited seed** — the min-budget point is season 1 and lands on a different country each seed, i.e. the knife-edge is the pre-existing season-1 one, not something the new countries introduced. Pre-existing Portugal→Belgium is still a coin-flip.
    - **Adding countries thins every poor league's transfer receipts, and that shows up as insolvency (measured 2026-08-28).** Going from 6 selling leagues to 8 splits the same big-four demand more ways. The 10-country audit had **0 deficits across 4 seeds**; the 12-country one had **2 of 4** (seed 3 Scotland −£5.2M s20, seed 2 Serbia −£0.2M). Raising the bottom block's `COUNTRY_BUDGET_SCALE` (Greece .38→.39, Scotland .35→.37, Serbia .32→.35, still strictly monotonic) took that to **1 of 4**, and the free-agency-queue fix below took it to **0 of 4** — see the resolution in the next bullet, and note the budget scales were *not* what fixed it. This is the tripwire `docs/transfer-mobility.md` describes, reached from a new direction: **anything that adds selling leagues lands on it, not just anything that reduces market volume.** Budget-check the poor end after every country addition.
  - **The residual deficit is a wage ratchet, NOT a scaling error, and the distinction was measured rather than assumed.** At generation the new leagues have the *safest* wage-bill-to-income ratios in the world — Serbia 0.153 / Scotland 0.163 / Greece 0.177 against Turkey 0.209, Portugal 0.215 and the big four's ~0.36 (worst single club: Serbia 0.36 / Scotland 0.40 / Greece 0.49 vs England 0.77). So the leagues are not underfunded; a single club is failing to shed a high earner over 20 seasons, the mechanism the deleted `staysSolvent` gate entry already documents ("first check whether the market can still shed wages"). **It is a tail that grows with world size** — more clubs, more chances to hit it — and it moved country when budgets changed (seed 3: Scotland −£5.2M → Greece −£5.1M, same magnitude), which is the signature of a per-club effect rather than a per-league one.
    - **RESOLVED, and not by the budget scales (2026-08-29).** The shipped tree is **SOLVENT on 4 of 4 seeds** (`SEASONS=20 SEEDS=1,2,3,4`, `RESULT: all checks passed`), min AI budget £0.0–0.1M and always at **season 1-2**, i.e. the documented pre-existing knife-edge rather than a dynasty-long ratchet. What fixed it was the free-agency queue ordering above, not money: a weak league's clubs were being handed the *first* pick of the free-agent pool by the raw-points bug and spending real wages on it, and a per-club wage ratchet is exactly the shape that produces. **The lesson is the one the deleted `staysSolvent` gate already recorded and this is now the second instance: when a weak league goes insolvent, look at what its clubs are being made to buy before reaching for `COUNTRY_BUDGET_SCALE`.** The bottom block's scales were raised (Greece .38→.39, Scotland .35→.37, Serbia .32→.35) *before* the cause was known and are left in place — they are small, monotonic, and re-lowering them would be a second unmeasured change on top of a fixed system.
    - Final 4-seed rung means on the shipped tree: BIG4→France **+3.40**, France→Netherlands **+1.94**, Netherlands→Portugal **+0.93**, Portugal→Belgium **+1.25**, Belgium→Turkey **−0.84**, Turkey→Greece **−0.69**, Greece→Scotland **+1.83**, Scotland→Serbia **+0.42**, BIG4→Serbia **+8.24**, France→Serbia **+4.83**. The two negative rungs are inside the converged bottom block and inside the gate's own ±1 mean tolerance — exactly the "ordered at generation, converged by season 20" property documented above, not a regression.
  - **Youth underflow does not get worse at the bottom.** `YOUTH_BASE_FLOOR`'s softplus has already saturated by Turkey's weakest academy, so Scotland's (16.8) and Serbia's (15.8) map to the *identical* `youthGenerationBase` of 1.00 — same youth, ~5.3 pinned ratings per prospect, OVR 12-14, the healthy band that constant documents. The only cost is that the flattened floor gets more crowded, where `ACADEMY_FORM_SWING` has less bite.

- **Country strength gaps erode ~43% over a 20-season dynasty, and it's `growthDamping`, not the market (2026-08-08).** Measured by attributing drift to its only two sources (ovr changes *only* in progression, so retained players' mean change is pure progression and the remainder is pure churn): progression gave England +4.06 vs Portugal +7.53 — a **3.47 OVR relative lift for the weaker league** — against churn's 0.55. Cause: `growthDamping` throttles positive growth above a **global, absolute** `GROWTH_DAMPING_START` (65), so a league sitting under that line grows undamped while the big four are throttled. **Do not "fix" this by making the threshold league-relative** — that hands every weak league its own elites, which is the inflation the equilibrium exists to prevent; `GROWTH_DAMPING_END`/`FLOOR` were swept against a 30-season × 3-seed audit to land D1's 80+ population at 8-16 and re-verified over 100 seasons. `scripts/weakLeaguesAudit.ts` now **asserts a minimum surviving gap per rung** (and exits non-zero) rather than mere rank order — the old ordering-only check passed cleanly while the England→Portugal gap fell 9.6 → 5.5.
- `generateWorld()` — one rng pass; England block is byte-identical to the old England-only generation, and each country is generated in table order so appending one never perturbs an existing country's players (verified: adding Belgium/Turkey left all six prior leagues' generated OVR identical). `createLeagueState` uses it for all new saves; old saves stay at whatever country set they were created with (never re-run generation). `CLUBS` = 320 fictional identities (no real clubs — trademark caution). New-save UI has a country picker (`NewLeague.tsx`); Customize Teams editor is competition-scoped.
- **Per-league nationality tables must exist for every home country** (`LEAGUE_NATIONALITY_WEIGHTS`) — a missing country silently falls back to *England's* table. Source percentages routinely over-sum (the supplied Belgian list totalled 116.5%, the Turkish 130.7%); since the engine normalizes to the table total, feeding them raw understates the domestic share badly (Turkey would land 36.7% domestic instead of 48%). **Scale the foreign block so the domestic share lands on its stated value.**
  - **A truncated top-N list is the mirror image of that trap and the leftover MUST NOT go into `REST` wholesale (2026-08-29).** Published breakdowns usually list only the top ~20 nationalities, so they *under*-sum — Scotland's covers 84% of the league, Greece's 79%, Serbia's 92%. Putting the remainder in the rest-of-world bucket looks like the honest reading and isn't, because **`TAIL_BASE` is ~40% English** (it is built from `NATIONALITIES`' weights, i.e. the Premier League's own foreign makeup — see `REST_OF_WORLD` below). Measured: at Serbia's real 8% leftover, England came out the league's **second-largest nationality at 3.7%** against a source list that contains no English players at all, and Scotland's England would have gone 15.1% → ~21%. Name the tail explicitly instead and keep `REST` at 1.6-2.3%; the shipped tables run it at 6.5-9.7%, which is only safe because none of them is a league where England is already a top import or a plausible absurdity. **`scripts/natShareProbe.ts` samples the real draw with the bucket expanded** — the only way to see this, since the raw weights look right.
  - **All four of the Scottish, Greek, Serbian and Dutch tables are now from real published breakdowns and land on them to ±0.1pp.** The three sourced ones also corrected first-draft guesses that were badly wrong in the same way each time — the *domestic* share and the *neighbours* were plausible, the actual biggest import was not: Greece's is **Spain** (6.3%, not Brazil) and Serbia's is **Ghana** (4.2%, not a Yugoslav neighbour), while Scotland was written at 55% domestic against a real 35.8%. **The Dutch table was the fourth and worst of the guesses, and it is the one that shows what the failure mode actually is.** It was written as a development market: Japan and South Korea, a broad West African intake, and a comment explicitly denying a colonial pipeline. The real list has **no Ghana or Nigeria in it at all**, one Japanese player for every two Moroccans, and **three former colonies in the top thirteen** — Curacao, Suriname and Indonesia at 1.4% each, plus Morocco at 3.1%. The Scandinavian half of the guess was right. So the pattern across all four is not that the guesses were random: the *domestic share* was in the right area three times out of four and the *neighbours* were usually right, while **the distinctive foreign block — the one thing that makes a league read as itself — was wrong every single time**. Do not hand-author one of these; get the breakdown. **Curacao and Suriname needed name pools** (`UNLISTED_NATIONALITIES`, 56x56, ASCII "Curacao" to match every other key, both CONCACAF as in real football, neither with flag art so both take the neutral swatch). Verified the documented way: 360,000 draws across the other 11 league tables and the no-country pool are **byte-identical** to before, since `TAIL_BASE` reads `NATIONALITIES` + `OTHER_NATIONS` only.
  - **Changing a table is rating-neutral, and this is verifiable rather than assumed.** `pickNationality` runs on the player's own identity sub-stream, and although it consumes **one** rng value normally and **two** when the draw lands in `REST` — so resizing that bucket really does change the draw count — the sub-stream is per-player, so nothing leaks. Verified by regenerating the seed-1 world before and after: every one of the twelve countries' tier-1 starter mean OVR was identical to the decimal, the three changed tables included. Only names and nationalities move.

  New nationalities named only by a new league's table go in **`UNLISTED_NATIONALITIES`**, never `OTHER_NATIONS` — `TAIL_BASE` is built from `NATIONALITIES` + `OTHER_NATIONS` only, so the latter would re-weight the "Rest of the World" tail draw for every league in every existing save (Belgium needed none; Turkey added Bosnia-Herzegovina/Gambia/Albania).
- **Per-league nationalities** — `LEAGUE_NATIONALITY_WEIGHTS` (`core/players/nationalities.ts`), real top-flight breakdowns; drawn on each player's isolated identity sub-stream (RNG-safe). Youth intake is country-aware.
- **A league the player ADDS carries its own table (`Competition.nationalities`, `LeagueSpec.nationalities`, 2026-08-26).** Before this, an added league had an invented country name that matched no shipped table, so `pickNationality`'s England fallback caught it — a league you named "Netherlands" generated **38% English** players *with English names*, and kept doing it forever, because youth intake redraws from the same table every offseason. Resolved through `competitionNationalities` (the same absent-means-shipped-default pattern as `strengthOffset`/`budgetScale`), so **no migration** and the shipped world stays byte-identical. Threaded through all four generation paths — world gen, **youth intake** (the load-bearing one; miss it and the league drifts English one intake at a time), and roster import's filler + un-nationalitied players. **Shipped leagues are deliberately not editable** (user call): they keep their calibrated breakdowns. Three things worth knowing: **(a)** the draw takes 1 rng value (2 via the REST bucket) either way, on the player's own identity sub-stream, so a custom table cannot shift the shared stream; **(b)** `sanitizeNationalityWeights` drops any nation with no name pool, because `generateName` falls back to synthesized nonsense words for an unknown nationality — the UI only offers the 78 that have both names and flag art; **(c)** the editor shows each row's *realized* share, which is not cosmetic — it is the on-screen defence against the over-summing trap in the bullet above, which cost Turkey 48% → 36.7% domestic when it was fed raw.
- **`REST_OF_WORLD` is NOT a neutral global prior, and an added league can't use it as one.** `TAIL_BASE` is built from `NATIONALITIES`' weights, which are *the Premier League's* nationality frequencies, so a pure rest-of-world draw is **England 39.6%**, France 6.4%, Brazil 6.4%. That is correct as an English league's foreign tail (what it was built for) and wrong as filler anywhere else: a hand-authored Dutch league with a 30% REST row still comes out ~12% English (measured). `restOfWorldPreview` surfaces this in the editor rather than re-weighting it, since re-weighting would change every existing league's tail. A newly added league is seeded with `{__REST__: 100}` **explicitly** rather than left absent — absent would fall back to England's *full* table while the editor displayed the REST bucket, and a preview that disagrees with the result is worse than none.
- **30 nations added to `UNLISTED_NATIONALITIES` (2026-08-26), taking the pickable set 78 -> 108.** Ten in Asia (Saudi Arabia, Qatar, UAE, Iraq, Uzbekistan, Jordan, Thailand, Vietnam, Indonesia, Malaysia), eight in Europe (Hungary, Bulgaria, Russia, Georgia, North Macedonia, Montenegro, Northern Ireland, Belarus), seven in Africa (Ethiopia, Uganda, Zimbabwe, Sudan, Libya, Togo, Benin), three in North America (Guatemala, El Salvador, Trinidad and Tobago) and two in Oceania (Fiji, Papua New Guinea) — the last of which gives that confederation a second nation, so an OFC Nations Cup becomes reachable if a world ever fields enough. **`UNLISTED`, never `OTHER_NATIONS`, and this is the whole safety argument**: `TAIL_BASE` is built from `NATIONALITIES` + `OTHER_NATIONS`, so adding thirty nations there would re-weight the "Rest of the World" tail of every league in every existing save. **Verified rather than argued — 270,000 draws across all 8 league tables plus the no-country pool are byte-identical to `origin/main`.** Each carries 24 first + 24 last ASCII names (the test floor is 20) drawn from common civilian names rather than national-team squads, per the file's own rule that generated players must never read as real pros — the first draft of this leaned on squad lists and had to be redone. Every one has a confederation (`international.test.ts` enforces it) and a name pool. **15 have no flag art and render `<Flag>`'s neutral swatch** (Saudi Arabia, Iraq, Uzbekistan, Jordan, Malaysia, Montenegro, Northern Ireland, Belarus, Uganda, Zimbabwe, Libya, Guatemala, El Salvador, Fiji, Papua New Guinea): their flags need Arabic calligraphy or a coat of arms, and the shipped flags are hand-written SVGs, so a wrong flag was judged worse than a blank. The other 15 got real art.
- **The pickable set is now ALL 211 FIFA members (`src/core/players/worldNationalities.ts`, 2026-09-01, user call).** The 108 above were never a designed list: they were whichever nations some shipped league's real breakdown happened to name, which is the right rule for a *tail* and the wrong one for a *catalogue* — so "add a Taiwanese league" was not a thing the game could do, and a roster file naming Taiwan had the row silently dropped by `sanitizeNationalityWeights`. `WORLD_NATIONALITIES` holds the remaining 101 and is spread into `UNLISTED_NATIONALITIES` (first, so a hand-curated entry wins a collision); it imports only the `NationalityDef` **type** back, so there is no runtime import cycle. **Same safety argument as the 30 above, and it is the reason this is cheap: `TAIL_BASE` is `NATIONALITIES` + `OTHER_NATIONS` only, so a nation here is reachable ONLY from a table that names it.** Verified rather than argued — **390,000 draws across all 12 league tables plus the no-country pool are byte-identical to the pre-change build, and so are the name pools they resolve to**. Moving any of these to `OTHER_NATIONS` would re-weight every existing save's rest-of-world roll and put a Bhutanese full-back in the Bundesliga; `customNationalities.test.ts`'s existing tail test covers all 136 unlisted nations automatically. Pools are **56x56** (the documented floor for a nation that can now be a league's *home*), ASCII, dupe-free, civilian names. **The completeness gate is the per-confederation counts, not the total** (`nationalities.test.ts`): 55/54/46/35/11/10 **is** FIFA's real membership, so it catches the likely error — a nation filed under the continent it sits on rather than the confederation it plays in. Guyana (CONCACAF) and Guam (AFC) both look wrong and are right; a typo in either moves two counts at once. **25 got flag art** (`ee lv lt lu am td ye sl mu bw mg cg ne bd la mm ps sy kw so dj ss kp lr tw`), all exactly reproducible as bands, a disc, a triangle or a star, and eyeballed rasterised before shipping; ~118 of the 211 still render the neutral swatch on purpose, for the reason the bullet above gives. **One real knock-on: a country with generated players becomes internationally eligible**, so a custom Taiwanese league puts Taiwan into AFC qualifying and makes it a manageable national side. Nothing changes for a default world (none of the 101 generates a player there), so no dynasty audit was needed.
- **Every name pool was expanded and the test floor raised 20 -> 50 (2026-08-26).** A name is one `first` x one `last` draw, so pool size squared is the whole namespace, and the old 20x20 floor was sized for a nation that supplies a handful of players through some *other* league's rest-of-world tail. Once a player-built league can name its own nationalities, **any** nation can be a home nation supplying ~1000 players. Measured at 24x24 (576 names) over 1000 draws: **82% of players shared a name with someone and one name landed six times**; England, at 96x112, reads 8%. Pools now start at **56x56 (3136)**, 103 of 108 clear 3600, and the file carries **~16,000 names**; worst case is 24% sharing with no name appearing more than three times. **Two invariants were verified rather than assumed:** the nationality *distribution* is byte-identical to `origin/main` (270,000 draws across all 8 league tables plus the no-country pool, 0 differences — pool size feeds `generateName`, never `pickNationality`, and `TAIL_BASE` weights are `def.weight`, not pool length), and `generateName` still costs **exactly 2 rng draws** whatever the pool size, so stream order is untouched. The one real consequence: a *newly generated* player draws a different name than he would have before, so a fresh world with a fixed seed is name-different (ratings, positions and nationalities identical). Existing saves are unaffected — their names are already stored. `scripts/namePoolProbe.ts` measures the rate.
- **Roster files can declare the mix** (top-level `nationalities` block, `rosterFile.ts`). Top level, not per competition: `competitions` entries are a league's individual *divisions*, and a country's two tiers can't meaningfully differ. Parsing is **strict on shape, lenient on content** — a wrong type throws (an authoring mistake worth reporting) but an unknown nation is dropped, because these files are usually AI-written and one hallucinated country shouldn't cost the author the other forty. A loaded file **pre-fills the editor** rather than overriding it invisibly, so there is one visible source of truth and no precedence rule; the AI prompt (`rosterAiPrompt.ts`) documents the field.

**Promotion/relegation & the lower divisions**
- Every country generates a full pyramid of divisions; an N-up/N-down swap between each adjacent pair each offseason, the last place up decided by a playoff (below) (`core/promotion.ts`, `computeCountrySwaps`/`applyCompetitionSwaps`, per LINK). A swapped club's `academyBase` converges gradually (`stepAcademyBaseConvergence`); finance scale switches immediately.
- **N is per COUNTRY, not per save (`Competition.promotionSpots`, 2026-08-25)**, set on a league the player *adds* in World setup and resolved through `competitionPromotionSpots`; absent → `PROMOTION_RELEGATION_COUNT` (3), which is what every shipped country plays and what every existing save keeps. **Optional field on `Competition`, so there is no `LeagueStore` field and no migration** — the same fallback pattern as `strengthOffset`/`teamCount`, and the reason it is modelled this way: World setup only exposes per-league knobs for *added* leagues, so a world-wide setting would have been the wrong shape for the one place it can be edited (the first cut of this shipped as a save-wide picker and was reverted, user call). Written onto **both** divisions of a country by `buildCompetitions` (built from one spec together, so they can't drift). Two gotchas: **(a) 0 is a legitimate value and `slice(-0)` is `slice(0)`**, i.e. the *whole* table, so a closed pyramid would relegate every club in the division — `computeCountrySwaps` returns early at `n <= 0` rather than trusting the slice, and the accessor maps non-finite values to 0 for the same reason; **(b)** the accessor clamps to both divisions' `teamCount` and `effectivePromotionSpots` clamps again to the actual table lengths (and to half a MIDDLE division, below), since a division can be as small as `MIN_DIVISION_TEAMS` while the picker offers up to `MAX_PROMOTION_SPOTS` (6). Nothing else in the sim reads it — the swap is the whole mechanic — so no audit or equilibrium is involved, but a big count churns tier 1 harder and leaves `enforceDivisionCeilings` more relegated squads to sweep.
- **A country's divisions are a CHAIN, not a pair — the pyramid is N deep (2026-08-30).** `LeagueSpec.divisions` is 1-3; `Competition.tier` is a plain `number`. The shipped world is still every country at two divisions and is **byte-identical** across this change (verified: `generateWorld`'s England block still matches `generateTwoDivisionLeague`, the CLUBS/tid-layout guard, `fixtureFidelity` and the `touchStats` scoreline hash all unchanged), so there is no migration and no save moves.
  - **`partnerOrNull` is GONE, and its removal is the point.** It asked for "the other competition in this country" and answered by taking the first same-country competition that wasn't this one — correct by luck at two divisions, silently wrong at three, where it would hand a tier-3 club the tier-1 competition about half the time. Replaced by `divisionsOf` (the chain, top flight first), `divisionAbove`/`divisionBelow` (adjacent **by tier**), `countryDivisions` (every country's chain) and `promotionLinks` (every adjacent pair, top-down). `tier1Pairs`/`Tier1Pair` went with it. Ask for the neighbour you actually mean; there is no longer a "the other one".
  - **The three tier-keyed numbers are FORMULAS over the tier, not two-entry tables**, each reproducing its shipped value exactly so no existing world moves by a float: strength `divisionStrengthOffset(tier) = (tier-1) * DIVISION_2_OFFSET`, money `tierScale(tier) = DIVISION_2_BUDGET_SCALE ** (tier-1)`, ceiling `divisionRefusalOvr(tier)`. The last returns **`Infinity` at tier 1**, which is what lets every call site be a bare `ovr >= divisionRefusalOvr(tier)` with no tier test of its own (`wouldRefuseExtension`, the `inboundOffers` buy gate, the `runAILoanMarket` gate).
  - **The sweep runs one pass per tier, DEEPEST FIRST, and the order is load-bearing.** Each pass reads the rosters the previous one left, so a third-tier player over both bars is swept to tier 2 and then to tier 1 within one call. Shallowest-first would strand him a division short for a whole season — exactly the drift the ceiling exists to prevent. A two-division world runs one pass and is unchanged.
  - **A middle division can be promoted out of AND relegated out of in the same season, which two divisions could never do.** The swap is therefore clamped to half a middle division's size, or an over-large `promotionSpots` puts the same club in both slices and `applyCompetitionSwaps` silently keeps whichever it wrote last. Scoped to middles deliberately: at two divisions a top-flight club only goes down and a second-tier club only goes up, so clamping there would change a world with nothing wrong with it. **The clamp lives in `effectivePromotionSpots`, NOT in `computeCountrySwaps` where it was first written** — that function is the single source of truth the promotion playoff also reads, and a clamp only the swap could see would be exactly the bracket-vs-slice disagreement it exists to prevent (it cannot bite in the shipped world, whose smallest middle division is 10 clubs against at most 3 places, but a hand-built one reaches it at `MIN_DIVISION_TEAMS` 8 and `MAX_PROMOTION_SPOTS` 6). Note the links read the **final tables**, not each other's output, so a club still moves at most one division a season — do not "fix" that by chaining them.
  - **Lower-division cup seeds take one band per tier** (`lowerDivisionSeed`, `LOWER_DIVISION_SEED_BASE * (tier-1) + rank`). One shared base would put a third-division cup winner in the same range as a second-division one and rank them on league position alone, reading 3rd in the third tier as better than 8th in the second.
  - **Tier-3 constants: refusal OVR 62, money scale 0.36 (`DIVISION_2_BUDGET_SCALE ** 2`).** Audited once every country gained a third division — see the world entry above for what that measured and what it cost. The known cost at tier 3 is that its academy anchor lands in the saturated part of `YOUTH_BASE_FLOOR`'s softplus, so third-tier academies all produce near-identical youth and `ACADEMY_FORM_SWING` has little bite there.
- **The last promotion place is a PLAYOFF, and WHICH KIND is per country (`core/promotionPlayoff.ts`, `Competition.playoffFormat`, 2026-08-30).** Three values, resolved through `competitionPlayoffFormat` (never the field): `english`, `german`, `none`. Optional, so **absent means the country's own system** — `COUNTRY_PLAYOFF_FORMAT` for a shipped country, `DEFAULT_PLAYOFF_FORMAT` otherwise — and **no save carries the field or needed migrating for it**, the same pattern as `promotionSpots`/`strengthOffset`. Shipped defaults are the real ones: **Germany `german`**, Scotland `none` (it promotes one club), every other country `english`. Editable on **every** league in World setup, shipped rows included, because the panel there is already shared between shipped and added leagues and already shows resolved values while writing only on change — so a shipped country picks up an explicit field only when the player actually moves the control, which is what keeps `buildCompetitions(worldLeagueSpecs())` equal to `worldCompetitions()`.
  - **The two formats reach the table differently, which is why `computeCountrySwaps` takes a `PlayoffOutcome` rather than a winner.** English sends one extra club up and leaves relegation alone (`autoPromoted` N-1, `autoRelegated` N). German cuts **both** automatic counts to N-1 and then either swaps a pair or moves nobody. **Both come out with `promoted.length === relegated.length` whichever way a tie goes**, which is the property the whole feature rests on: division sizes are fixed, so one extra club up would mean one extra down. A German tie the incumbent wins is the case worth holding in mind — that season the country promotes and relegates one fewer club than its `promotionSpots` says, and that is correct rather than a leak.
  - **`slice(-0)` is `slice(0)`, and the German format is what makes that reachable again.** A country promoting one club under `german` automates nothing, so `autoRelegated` is 0 and an unguarded `d1Table.slice(-0)` would relegate the entire division. Guarded in `computeCountrySwaps`. Worth knowing that the *test* written to check this fell into the same trap computing its own expectation, which is how sharp the edge is.
  - **A German tie pools BOTH divisions for composites; an English bracket pools tier 2 alone.** Same lesson as `cupMatchData` and the domestic cups: composites z-normalize within the pool they are built from, so a tier-2 club measured against its own division reads as an average side and would meet a top-flight club as an equal — which would erase exactly the incumbent's advantage the format exists to test. An English bracket needs no pooling because all four entrants come from the one division, so measuring them against it *is* measuring them against each other.
  - **Feasibility is per format, and `english` is the fussy one.** It sits *below* the automatic places, so it needs `promotionSpots >= 2` (at one place the only bracket is positions 1-4, which takes promotion off the champion) plus four clubs below those places; too short a division holds no playoff rather than a smaller bracket, so the shape is identical everywhere it runs. `german` works from one place upward, needing only one club either side of the line. The World setup control disables `english` below two places rather than letting a player pick a format that silently produces nothing.
  - **The German entrants can never overlap the automatic slices.** The tier-1 side is at index `length - spots`, which sits exactly above the bottom `spots - 1`, and the tier-2 side at index `spots - 1`, exactly below the top `spots - 1`. Pinned across every spot count by a test, because getting it wrong promotes or relegates a club twice rather than throwing.
  - Measured over 3 seasons of a real world: 10 English brackets and Germany's one German tie a season, with the German tie going to the top-flight club 2 of 3 times — the direction to expect, since it is the better side and the pooled baseline says so.
  - **English format detail.** A country giving out **two or more** places sends only its top `N-1` up automatically; the four clubs at tier-2 positions `N..N+3` contest two-legged semi-finals (best v worst, 2nd v 3rd) and a single-leg final for the last place. The winner is provably not already in the `N-1` slice (he came from below it), so `promoted` cannot hold a club twice.
  - **Why `english` needs two places, stated once more because it reads like a size guard and isn't.** With one place the only English-style bracket is positions 1-4, which takes promotion *off the champion* and hands it to a lottery. Scotland (1 place) therefore keeps its straight single promotion. A division too short to seat all four entrants likewise holds none rather than a smaller bracket, so the shape is identical everywhere it runs.
  - **`effectivePromotionSpots` (`competitions.ts`) exists so the bracket and the swap cannot disagree about N.** The playoff seats the clubs below "the automatic places" and the swap slices "the top N"; if those two read a different N (the count is clamped to both table lengths, and to half a middle division) the bracket and the slice overlap and a club is promoted twice. One function, two callers — which is why it takes the whole `competitions` array: middle-ness is a property of the country's chain, not of the pair.
  - **Seated at the TOP link only, and that is now a decision rather than a consequence.** While `tier1Pairs` was the only way to walk the world, "the country's two divisions" and "the boundary a playoff decides" were the same thing; with `promotionLinks` handing back every boundary of an N-deep pyramid, `promotionPlayoffFields` says top-link-only explicitly. England and Germany both hold real playoffs further down, so this is a feature worth having — but it needs its own page, news and changelog, and a deeper pyramid must not switch it on by itself. `computeCountrySwaps` needs no matching guard: outcomes are keyed by the LOWER division's compId, so a lower link finds none and takes the plain top-N slice.
  - **Played at the offseason transition in `simThrough`, NOT inside `simOffseason` — and it must be before `reviewSeason`.** Where a country holds a playoff, "were we promoted" is not answerable from the table, so the board would judge the wrong clubs and a job offer would name the wrong division. Playing it there also means it runs on **end-of-season squads**, before progression ages anyone and before retirement takes a veteran out of his own club's final.
  - **`simOffseason` replays it if handed a league that never came through there**, which is the ~60 test and audit call sites that drive it directly. Both paths agree because every tie's stream is `hashInts(lid, season, d2CompId, round, tie, PROMOTION_PLAYOFF_STREAM)` — derived from content, not from a stream position — and both read end-of-season squads. Without that fallback every audit would silently measure a world with straight promotion while the game shipped playoffs. It sits above step 0 for that reason, which also meant hoisting `tablesByCompId` to the top of the function (nothing between there and step 3.5 moves a club between competitions, so it is the same map).
  - **Zero shared-`rng` draws**, so no league scoreline moves and the `touchStats` hash is untouched. What *does* change is which club goes up, so **dynasty baselines shift from the first offseason on** — expected, and the reason the ladder/solvency audit was re-run rather than assumed.
  - **Composites are z-normalized within the tier-2 competition, and here that needs no pooling** — unlike every cup, all four entrants come from that one division, so measuring them against it *is* measuring them against each other. Season form is applied (the playoff is the last act of the season it decides). Suspensions are deliberately not carried in, the same call the cups make: a ban is served in league matchdays and the offseason has no matchday clock to serve one against. Injuries are honoured, since `leagueMatchData` filters them for every caller.
  - **The better league finish is worth exactly ONE thing: the tie against the lowest-placed entrant.** Everything else that looks like a reward isn't. The tie's `home` is the *lower* seed, so the better finisher takes the **second** leg at home (the real English convention) — but each club hosts once, so `HOME_ATTACK_BONUS` cancels over the two legs, and `playExtraTime`/`playShootout` are handed raw composites and never apply it, so extra time and the shootout are venue-neutral too. Reversing the leg order would change no result. And the **final is played at a neutral venue** (below), so it carries no home advantage either. Measured over 44 finals the seed win split is **22.7 / 20.5 / 25.0 / 31.8%** — flat, which is both what the arithmetic predicts and how the real English playoff behaves.
