  - **`SimMatchOptions.neutral` is a new engine flag, and the promotion playoff final is its only caller (2026-08-30, user call).** England's final is at Wembley and neither finalist is at home; before this the engine had no way to say so, since `HOME_ATTACK_BONUS` was applied unconditionally at both sites in `simMatchDetailed` (the initial build and `rebuildTeam`, which re-applies it after every sub and red card — miss the second and the bonus reappears the moment someone is substituted). `resolveCupTie` takes it through as a trailing `neutral = false` param. **Default off, and it changes a composite value rather than a draw**, so every cup and league match is bit-identical and the `touchStats` hash is unmoved (verified). The better finisher is still listed as `home` because the box score and the event feed need two named sides; it just buys him nothing.
  - **Box scores are never stored** (`boxScore: null` on every tie). The world plays 36 of these ties a season and history is kept forever; scorelines are ~9 KB a season against a season-history entry's ~900 KB. Storing them would be the save-size mistake this file documents twice already. The consequence is that the page shows no scorers, which is the accepted cost.
  - **`LeagueStore.promotionPlayoffs` is TRANSIENT, not history.** Filled at the season's end, copied onto `SeasonHistoryEntry.promotionPlayoffs` by the offseason and then emptied — so it is non-empty only while the user sits in the offseason looking at the result. `/promotion-playoffs`, the News Feed and the Dashboard all read both places, the same way the Cup page reads `cup` and `cupHistory`. Migrated to `[]`; the history field is optional and **never backfilled**, because a season played before this really was decided on the table and inventing a bracket for it would be inventing results.
  - News is **derived** (`core/promotionNews.ts`), like the trophies and honours and for the same reason — the result is already on the season entry, so an event row would be a second copy that could drift. Tiered to the user's **own country** (`d1CompId === userCompId || d2CompId === userCompId`), which is right whichever division he is in: a top-flight manager wants to know who is joining his league, a second-division one who took the place he was chasing. Ten other countries deciding their second division is not world news.
  - **Gated on `weakLeaguesAudit.ts` (20 seasons x 4 seeds), run on BOTH sides — and the baseline is NOT green, which is the whole reason it was run.** The shipped version **passes outright: 4 of 4 seeds SOLVENT, `RESULT: all checks passed`**, ladder OK on every seed, every mean gate passing, block spreads BIG4->Serbia **+7.74** and France->Serbia **+4.54** (main: +7.06 / +4.32). `origin/main` at d0007c0 **fails the same gate** — seed 4, Serbia, **-£0.6M at season 19**.
    - **Do not read that as "playoffs improve solvency", and the intermediate measurement is why.** The same feature with a *home-advantage* final (the version before the neutral one) read **1 of 4 in deficit** — seed 3, Serbia, -£2.7M at season 21. Three builds, three different answers, all with the ladder healthy: main 1/4, home-final 1/4, neutral-final 0/4. The playoff changes *which* club is promoted, which shifts every downstream draw from the first offseason on, so each build lands the poorest league's knife-edge somewhere different. The deficits are a per-club wage ratchet in Serbia/Scotland being re-rolled, not something any of these builds fixes or causes. Expect the next change here to land somewhere else again, and re-measure both sides rather than quoting this line.
    - **The entry above this one claiming the shipped tree is "SOLVENT on 4 of 4 seeds" was measured before this and no longer holds — `origin/main` at d0007c0 exits non-zero on this gate.** That is exactly the trap the free-agency-queue entry warns about two bullets up ("measure main first rather than assume a green baseline"), and it caught this work twice over: measured against the stale 4/4 the home-final version read as a regression it had not caused, and this version reads as a fix it has not made. Re-measure both sides before touching `COUNTRY_BUDGET_SCALE` on the strength of either number.
    - Per-seed rung gaps wobble in both directions inside the converged bottom block on every build (main Turkey->Greece -0.15, shipped -0.18; main Portugal->Belgium +1.30, shipped -0.31), which is the documented coin-flip behaviour of one-point rungs and not a signal either way.
  - **Solvency measured worse with Germany on the German default, and it SHIPPED anyway on a user call — read this before touching `COUNTRY_PLAYOFF_FORMAT` or `COUNTRY_BUDGET_SCALE`.** `weakLeaguesAudit`, 20 seasons, deficits per seed: `origin/main` **1 of 4** (seed 4, Serbia, -£0.6M), English-with-home-final **1 of 4**, English-with-neutral-final **0 of 4**, per-country-with-Germany-German **3 of 4** (-£5.1M Turkey, -£3.6M Serbia, -£6.7M Serbia). **The ladder is healthy on every seed of every one of those builds**, and the block spreads on the shipped build are the widest measured (BIG4->Serbia +7.47, France->Serbia +5.16, against main's +7.06/+4.32). So this is a weak-league finance reading, never a strength one.
    - **A same-seed control was run and it did NOT clear the gap, which is worth knowing before someone repeats it.** Seed 5 reads DEFICIT on the shipped build (-£3.5M Serbia) *and* on the English-only build (-£3.1M Scotland), so seed 5 is simply a hard seed and cannot be used to blame the German default — but it also leaves seeds 1-4 at 0/4 vs 3/4 with the same code differing only in Germany's format.
    - **What is actually unresolved:** whether that gap is real. Against — there is no mechanism (Germany is a rich big-four country, the deficits land in Turkey and Serbia, and the only channel is the whole-world rng re-roll that has moved this same knife-edge on every build in this file's history). For — it is the cleanest A/B available, since every other part of the change is absent-means-default. Settling it needs seeds 6-8 on **both** builds, which was offered and declined; the feature shipped with the real-world defaults because the ladder holds and weak-league deficits pre-date all of it. **Do not read the 3/4 as evidence the German format is expensive, and do not read a later clean run as evidence it is free — measure both sides on fresh seeds.**
  - Probe: `scripts/promotionPlayoffProbe.ts` (bracket seeding, promoted-count conservation, winner-goes-up, the headless replay matching, and the seed-win distribution).
- **`enforceDivisionCeilings`** (`core/ai/divisionCeiling.ts`) — the load-bearing anti-drift mechanic. Every offseason, any AI-controlled tier-2 player ≥ `DIVISION_2_REFUSAL_OVR_THRESHOLD` (70) is *deterministically* reassigned to a needy tier-1 club **in his own country** (zero fee, releases the receiver's weakest if capped). Runs twice (after promotion/relegation, and after the summer market). **Its workload dropped sharply on 2026-08-08** (61→18 sweep moves in the `offseason.test.ts` fixture) because the market no longer sells good players into tier 2 — worth knowing because the sweep's collateral (releasing a capped receiver's weakest man) was the *only* source of free agents in the very first offseason, so that test now plays a second season to reach steady state. This holds "D2 strongest ≤ D1 average" flat across 100-season dynasties — probabilistic approaches did not (relegation doesn't route through any market decision). `wouldRefuseExtension` (`core/ai/breakoutRefusal.ts`, now a flat ovr≥threshold check) is kept for **user-facing** purposes only (Roster "Wants a move to Division 1" badge/Extend-block; surfacing rival stars as buyable). The user's own club is never swept.
- Market/loan **prevention guards**: a tier-2 club never buys/bids-on/is-loaned an over-threshold player (guards placed *after* jitter draws, per RNG-order), so the game stops manufacturing illegal D2 residents and the sweep only cleans up structural cases (relegated squads, homegrown breakouts).

**Continental Cup** (`core/cup/`) — **Swiss league phase + knockout** (all new cups)
- **32-club field** (20 → 24 with Belgium/Turkey, → 28, → 32 as four more countries joined), qualified by league position (not OVR): strong (big-four) leagues send top 4, every handicapped league sends top 2 (`CUP_STRONG/WEAK_LEAGUE_SLOTS`), so 4×4 + 8×2. **The Continental Shield is `countries × 2` = 24** and grows with every country, which is the second reason the country count must stay even. **The field size is derived, not read** — `CUP_LEAGUE_PHASE_SIZE` only documents and anchors tests; the draw and the split both work off the actual field length. A valid field is a **multiple of 4** — even, and split into `CUP_LEAGUE_PHASE_POTS` pots that are themselves even (32 → two pots of 16 ✓), which is why weak leagues all send 2 rather than some sending 1. **Skips season 1** (no prior table → `cup===null`). Cross-cut by a **shared normalization baseline** (`simThrough`'s `cupMatchData` pools the whole field) so weak clubs read as genuinely weaker. `LeagueStore.cup`/`cupHistory`, `/cup`.
- **Qualification is an allocation pass over every competition at once, not a slice of each table (`core/cup/qualification.ts`, 2026-08-28).** There are three routes in, taken in this order: **title holder** (the Cup keeps its own winner and takes the Shield's — real since 2015), **domestic cup winner** (takes one of his country's Shield places), then **league position**. `allocateContinentalPlaces` walks `CONTINENTAL_ORDER` and places each club **exactly once, in the best competition he reaches**; `qualifyCupTeams`/`buildCupState` read their format's slice, `qualificationByTid` is what the Standings shading uses. **Why it had to stop being a table slice:** disjointness used to be *arithmetic* — the Shield started at an offset derived by summing the slots above it, so the two fields provably butted up against each other. A club entering from 9th makes the qualifying places non-contiguous and no offset describes them, so disjointness is now *structural* (the `placed` set) and `cupOffsetForCompetition` survives only as a description of the usual case, for the legend and the Manual. **Slot COUNTS are untouched, and that is load-bearing:** a route never creates a place, it displaces its own league's lowest league-position qualifier, who cascades into the next competition. Field sizes therefore cannot move — and they must not, because `isValidCupFieldSize` only accepts multiples of 4 ≥ 12, so a route that grew a field would fail the *draw*, not the qualification. **The draw's same-country constraint moved from compId to country (`drawGroupOf`)**: a second-division domestic cup winner's compId is his own division, so keying on compId would let him be drawn against his country's top flight. Same equality relation for an all-top-flight field, so **existing draws are byte-identical** — the pre-change tests pass unmodified. Two gotchas: **(a)** a tier-2 entrant is seeded at `LOWER_DIVISION_SEED_BASE + rank`, below every top-flight club, because 5th in the second division isn't 5th in the first; **(b)** a special-route club who *would have qualified on merit* is labelled `"table"`, not by his route (`onMerit`, computed before any claim) — otherwise a first-placed champion's Standings row reads "as holders", which looks like a bug. Pure, **zero rng draws of any kind**, no persisted field and no migration (routes are read off `league.domesticCups` / `league.cup` / `league.shield` in `simOffseason`, which still hold the just-decided season's cups at that point). Measured with `scripts/qualificationProbe.ts`: over 5 seasons the cup route fires for 3–6 of the 8 domestic cup winners a season (the rest had qualified anyway and the place passes back down the table), holders need the route 1–2 times a season, and second-division clubs reach the Shield in 4 of 5 seasons.
- **Cup places are earned on a rolling country coefficient, not fixed by strength class (`core/cup/coefficients.ts`, 2026-08-28).** Each season the Cup's places are handed out in coefficient order: a five-season (`COEFFICIENT_WINDOW`) record of how each country's clubs did in Europe, pooling **both** competitions as UEFA does, scored as league-phase match points + per-tie wins + per-round bonuses + a title bonus, **divided by clubs entered** (so sending four is worth nothing by itself). **Two properties carry the whole design.** **(1) ZERO-SUM:** the ladder is the multiset of slot counts the world *already had*, re-sorted onto countries by rank, so the world total is arithmetically identical whatever the coefficients say. This is a **correctness requirement, not tidiness** — `isValidCupFieldSize` only accepts certain field sizes (in practice multiples of 4), so a reallocation that changed the total would fail inside the **draw**, seasons later and nowhere near its cause. It also means a custom world keeps the shape its author chose, just handed out on merit. **(2) DERIVED, NOT STORED:** computed from the archived cups a save already keeps (scorelines survive `archiveCup`; only box scores are dropped), and a club's **country never changes** (promotion moves him between his own country's divisions), so no history lookup, **no new persisted field and no `migrate.ts` change** — an existing save gets a real coefficient off its own history the moment it loads. Passed through as a per-season `SlotOverrides` on `QualificationContext`, deliberately **not** written to `Competition.continentalSlots`: that field is *user-authored* for custom leagues and a season's reallocation would destroy it. Two guards, each earned rather than assumed: **`COEFFICIENT_MIN_SEASONS` (3)** — reallocating from the first available season put Belgium on 4 places and **Germany on 2 in season 3** off a *single* season, then reverted it; a world's first cups are the noisiest data it will ever have. And the `COEFFICIENT_MIN_CUP_SLOTS` floor (a league with no places can never earn a coefficient, so zero is a **trapdoor, not a demotion**) **stands down when the world's own ladder already puts a league below it**, because that means the league was authored out of the Cup on purpose and forcing it back in would overrule its author. **Measured, `scripts/coefficientAudit.ts`, 20 seasons × 2 seeds — note every figure in the rest of this bullet was taken on the 8-country/24-club world and is kept as the record of why the design is what it is, not as a current reading:** total held at exactly 24 every season on both, zero AI deficits (poorest club per country £8.6M–£66M), and **no ratchet** — places churn around the 4th/5th cut line (7 changes on seed 1, 2 on seed 2; Italy and Germany each dipped to 2 places and came back). **It is not cosmetic: on seed 1 France finished on 4 places and England on 2**, England's coefficient falling 11.83 → 8.10 while France's rose 6.17 → 8.42. The strong/weak blocks stayed ordered on both seeds (seed 1 England 56.8 / Spain 58.9 / Italy 58.0 / Germany 58.6 > France 54.0 > Portugal 52.2 / Belgium 52.2 > Turkey 50.9). **The failure mode to watch is the feedback loop, not the numbers moving:** places won → prize money → strength → more places. It is damped by the five-season window, and `changes-per-dynasty` plus `at floor` in that script are what detect it; a ladder that *never* moves is equally a failure, since the feature would be inert. **The formal gate (`scripts/weakLeaguesAudit.ts`, 20 seasons x 4 seeds) passes on the shipped code**: ladder OK on every seed, all four SOLVENT, mean rung gaps BIG4→France +2.70, France→Portugal +2.77, Portugal→Belgium +0.89, Belgium→Turkey +0.69, BIG4→Turkey +7.06, France→Turkey +4.36. Worth knowing for next time: it was ALSO run against the pre-`COEFFICIENT_MIN_SEASONS` version, which reallocates more often, and that scored France→Turkey **4.17 against a 4.0 threshold** — passing, but close enough that it was re-measured rather than trusted. Adding the floor moved it to 4.36 with every seed ≥ 4.02, i.e. **reallocating less often widens the block spreads**, which is the direction to expect and the reason the margin is not a live worry. Re-measure both scripts before touching any COEFFICIENT_* constant. **It is also a per-save setting (`LeagueStore.rollingCoefficients`, New League screen, 2026-08-29), off = the shipped strength-class allocation forever.** The switch lives at the single choke point every consumer goes through: `coefficientSlots` takes `enabled` as a **required** parameter, so the offseason, the Standings projection (`seasonQualification`) and the News Feed cannot diverge and a new caller cannot silently opt a save back in. `seasonContinentalNews` takes it for the same reason, and the Cup page hides its coefficient table (a table describing a mechanic the save doesn't run). Non-optional field, migrated to `true`, which is what every existing save was already doing.
- **League phase** (`core/cup/leaguePhase.ts`) — one combined table, each club plays `CUP_LEAGUE_PHASE_GAMES` (6) games. The draw (`drawLeaguePhase`) splits the seeded field into `CUP_LEAGUE_PHASE_POTS` (2) strength pots and builds the schedule **directly as 6 edge-disjoint perfect matchings** (guarantees once-per-round; a build-then-color graph may not be 1-factorable), giving each club 3 opponents from each pot, no same-league pairings, an Eulerian home/away split. Runs on its own seeded rng per round (`LEAGUE_PHASE_STREAM` offset) so league scorelines stay bit-identical. Table tiebreak: pts→GD→GF→wins→seed (no head-to-head, Swiss-unreliable).
- **Split** (`splitLeaguePhase`): **sized off the field by `cupKnockoutPlan`, not by two fixed constants (2026-08-29).** `CUP_LP_ADVANCE_FRACTION` (0.5) of the field goes through, capped at `CUP_LP_MAX_ADVANCE_BRACKETS` (1.5) brackets' worth; the bracket is the biggest power of two inside that, capped at `CUP_KO_MAX_SIZE`; `directQF = 2·koSize − advancing` and `playoffTeams = 2·(advancing − koSize)`. The old constants (4 direct, 8 playoff = 12 through) were applied to *every* field whatever its size — right for the shipped 32 and 24, and badly wrong below that: a 16-club field advanced **12 of 16**, six league-phase rounds to eliminate four clubs. `seedKnockoutFromLeaguePhase` seeds a bracket of `cup.teams.length`, and `koRoundsOf` reads the depth off that array rather than a constant (an old save carries 8 slots and still answers 3, hence **no migration**).
  - **The 1.5-bracket cap is what makes this shipped-world-neutral, and that is the whole design.** The Cup's field is **32** and the Shield's is **24**, so *no single fraction leaves both alone* (half of 32 is 16, half of 24 is 12). Capped, both land on **4 / 8 / 8 exactly as before** — pinned by a test — so no scoreline and, critically, **no prize money moves**. 1.5 is not arbitrary either: it is the shape the Cup already played and the shape UEFA plays (36 → 24 = 1.5 × 16). It is also the better format at the top end, since uncapped a 32-club field advances 16, i.e. **zero direct qualifiers** and a league phase whose winner earns nothing but a seeding.
  - **A format change moves prize money, and that is the thing to check FIRST here.** An earlier cut of this shipped no cap and, on the *pre-#292 8-country world*, turned the then-16-club Shield into a no-playoff competition — which deleted its four `SHIELD_PRIZE_WIN_PLAYOFF` payouts, **£6M a season out of a world whose weakest clubs sit within a rounding error of zero**. Measured (`weakLeaguesAudit`, 20 seasons × 4 seeds, same script both builds): **baseline 0 of 4 seeds in deficit, treatment 2 of 4** (Turkey −£1.2M s20, −£1.5M s18, both about the size of one lost prize). The documented shape exactly — the finance column failed while every ladder check stayed green. Restoring the money cleared both, which *also proved the mechanism was the money rather than stream shift*, the thing the audit alone cannot separate. **The capped design makes the whole question moot by construction rather than by compensation**, which is why it needs no audit: prove the plan is unchanged for 24 and 32 and there is nothing left to measure. Keep it that way — if these constants are ever retuned such that a *shipped* field's split moves, that is a prize-money change and it needs the 4-seed audit before it merges.
  - **Bracket depth is now a property of the STATE, which is a new fixture trap.** `koRoundsOf` reads `cup.teams.length`, so a hand-built `CupState` that declares four slots and then asserts quarter-final names gets "Semi-finals" and, at round 2, `Round of 1` — exactly the shape of the existing `isSwissCup`/`leaguePhase: null` trap one line up in `liveCandidates.test.ts`. Real cups are always sized by `cupKnockoutPlan`; synthetic ones must match the depth they assert.
  - **Both ends of the split are reachable and both are legal**, which is the part callers get wrong: below 24 a field can advance exactly a bracket's worth and play **no playoff at all** (`playoff` is left `null`, never an empty round, or `playoffDue` fires on it forever). `knockoutSeeded` covers it because the direct side is then full.
  - **The ceiling on the bracket is the CALENDAR, not taste.** `CUP_KO_LEG_MATCHDAYS` allots two matchdays each to the QF and SF plus one to the final; a 16-slot bracket needs a round of legs the league season has no room for. So a field big enough to want one takes a stingier cut instead (36 → 16 of 36, not 24). A *shallower* bracket takes the **last** rounds of that table, so a small cup still finishes on matchday 37 — the fixture the run-in and the stop-before-the-user's-final check are both built around.
  - **A format change moves prize money, and that is what nearly broke this.** Losing the Shield's playoff deleted its four `SHIELD_PRIZE_WIN_PLAYOFF` payouts — **£6M a season out of a world whose weakest clubs sit within a rounding error of zero**. Measured (`weakLeaguesAudit`, 20 seasons × 4 seeds, the same script run on both builds): **baseline 0 of 4 seeds in deficit, treatment 2 of 4** (Turkey −£1.2M s20 and −£1.5M s18, i.e. both about the size of one lost prize). The documented failure shape exactly — the finance column failed while every ladder check stayed green. Fixed by `CupPrizes.knockoutEntry` (`SHIELD_PRIZE_KNOCKOUT_ENTRY` = `SHIELD_PRIZE_WIN_PLAYOFF / 2`, credited in `playLeaguePhaseRound` to each club that takes a bracket slot directly): **8 × the new prize is arithmetically the 4 × the old one it replaces**, so the Shield's pot is identical, and it is **0 for the Cup** so the Cup stays byte-identical. Re-measured, **all 4 seeds solvent**, which also *proves the mechanism was the money rather than stream shift* — the thing the audit alone cannot separate (see the domestic-cup prize entry, where it could not be). Pooled 4-seed rung means, baseline → final: BIG4→France +2.71 → +3.55, France→Portugal +2.78 → +2.63, Portugal→Belgium +0.90 → +0.39, Belgium→Turkey +0.69 → +1.03, BIG4→Turkey +7.06 → **+7.59**, France→Turkey +4.36 → **+4.04 against a 4.0 gate** — passing, but the tightest number here, so re-measure it rather than trust it before touching these constants again. **Pool the seeds by hand**: a single-seed invocation applies the script's *mean* gate to one draw and seed 1 duly reports Portugal→Belgium "BROKEN" at −1.07, which is the documented coin-flip pair, not a verdict.
- **Knockout** is format-aware (`isSwissCup`/`koRoundsOf`/`koRoundMatchdays`/`koLegMatchdays`/`koPrizeByRound`/`koFinalRound`): Swiss = QF→SF→Final (3 rounds). **QF/SF are two-legged, home-and-away, decided on aggregate** (`CupState.twoLegged`, true for all newly-built cups; final and playoff always single-leg). Level after regulation (90' single-leg, or aggregate after both legs) → extra time → shootout; LP games can draw. **The two legs are played on separate matchdays** (`CUP_KO_LEG_MATCHDAYS` = `[[29,31],[33,35],[37]]`: QF legs 29/31, SF 33/35, Final 37). Flow (`playKnockoutLeg`, dispatched by `dueCupLeg` → `{round, leg, twoLeg}`): the first-leg matchday plays all first legs (`playFirstLeg`, plain 90') and holds them in `CupState.koLegs` (no prizes, no bracket advance); the second-leg matchday resolves each held tie on aggregate (`resolveTwoLeggedTie` plays leg 2 with the host swapped, sums scorelines, ET/shootout if level), appends the finished `CupTie`, clears `koLegs`. `mergeLines` folds both legs' box scores into one so cup stats/UI see a single tie line; `CupTie.legs` carries the two 90' scorelines for display. Safe to spread across matchdays because cup matches don't carry fatigue/injuries between each other (own per-matchday composites); the home/away swap + doubled sample is what reduces cup-vs-domestic variance (single-leg knockouts between good teams are near coin-flips — measured, stronger club advance rate ~64%→70% at a strong-favorite edge). Each leg runs on its own seeded stream (`hashInts(lid,season,round,30,leg)`), so the league match stream is untouched. **`simThrough` halts before the user's final** (Dashboard warns; the single-leg final's matchday is unchanged at 37). Prizes: participation (LP) + per-LP-result + playoff-win + per-KO-round (prize-only, credited when the tie finalizes) — see **Continental prize money** below for the shape and why it matters. Cup stats live on each tie's `BoxScore` (LP matches carry their own `boxScore`), separate from `SeasonStats`; Player Profile Cup tab. The user's Schedule page lists their cup fixtures (LP/playoff/each KO leg) alongside league games.
- **Legacy cups** (pre-Swiss saves, `leaguePhase===null`): old 16-team straight bracket + `CupPlayIn` kept alive only so an in-progress old cup finishes; `migrate.ts` backfills `leaguePhase`/`playoff` to null and `twoLegged` to false (so any cup already in progress finishes single-leg; the next built cup is two-legged). New cups (built each offseason via `buildCupState`) are always Swiss + two-legged. Matchday layout — two-legged Swiss: LP `[3,7,11,15,19,23]`, playoff `27`, KO legs `[[29,31],[33,35],[37]]`; single-leg Swiss (old in-progress saves): KO `[31,34,37]`; legacy bracket: `[8,16,26,34]`.

- **Continental prize money: qualifying is the biggest single payment, not the trophy (2026-08-31, user call).** The prize tiers shipped "title-ish" — £2M for reaching the league phase against £30M for lifting the Cup — which made a six-game campaign against the best clubs in the world worth **a tenth of finishing 5th in your own division** (`PRIZE_TOP_5`, £20M), and made the whole competition a lottery ticket rather than something a club builds a season around. Real continental football is the other way up: UEFA's starting fee is roughly what *reaching the final* pays. So participation went £2M → **£10M**, the league phase now pays **per result** (`CUP_PRIZE_LP_WIN` £1.5M / `CUP_PRIZE_LP_DRAW` £500k, the draw paid to both clubs), and the knockout tiers came down to meet them (final £30M → £14M). The Shield took the same reshape at its documented ~40%.
  - **The champion's total is almost unchanged, and that is the point** — ~£47M from the playoff round against the old ~£48M. The money moved to the *front* of the competition rather than into it, so this is a redistribution among the same clubs far more than an injection. What moved is the ratio that matters: a league-phase exit went £2M → ~£14M against `PRIZE_TOP_5`'s £20M. Quote that ratio, not the trophy, when re-tuning.
  - **The pot is dominated by the league phase, which is the easy thing to get wrong.** 32 clubs collect participation once but play **96 matches** between them, so a per-result fee that looks small beside the trophy is a bigger line than the trophy is. An earlier draft of this change sized the pot in a comment and was out by ~40% for exactly that reason. `scripts/continentalPrizeProbe.ts` prints the pot by stage, per-club earnings against `PRIZE_TOP_5`, and earnings per country as a share of base income — size a retune on that, not on arithmetic.
  - **Deliberately NOT scaled by `financeScale`** (user call), which is what the game already did and what UEFA does: a Belgian club earns exactly what an English one does. That flatness *is* the feature — it is why Europe is transformative for a small-league club — and it is simultaneously the risk, since a rich weak-league club climbing the ladder is this file's most-repeated tripwire. Hence the audit below is the gate for any change here.
  - **Measured `scripts/weakLeaguesAudit.ts`, 20 seasons x 4 seeds, against the same script run on the merge base — and the first thing that run establishes is that MAIN ITSELF FAILS IT.** Baseline: **3 of 4 seeds end in deficit** (Turkey -£5.1M s21, Serbia -£3.6M s20, Serbia -£6.7M s20), ladder OK on all four. **The "SOLVENT on 4 of 4" record higher up this file is stale** — something between that measurement and #309 regressed it, and anyone tuning here who assumes a green baseline will misread their own change as the cause. Measure the merge base first, every time.
  - **Verdict: the ladder is clean, solvency is a wash, and the pot is 3.3x.** Ladder OK on every seed on both sides; mean **BIG4→Serbia 7.47 → 7.89** (the headline block spread *widens*) and France→Serbia 5.16 → 4.37, both inside their gates — so the feared weaker-but-richer inversion, the whole risk of paying flat, **did not happen**. Solvency: deficits go **3 of 4 seeds → 4 of 4**, but every one of them is *smaller* — **worst case -£6.7M → -£3.4M and the total shortfall -£15.4M → -£10.1M, down a third**. So it neither fixes nor meaningfully worsens a pre-existing failure; it spreads a smaller total more thinly. **The one rung to watch is France→Netherlands, mean +2.09 → +0.17** (per seed +0.17/-0.89/+0.08/+1.31): it passes, but it is a 3-point "resolvable" rung that has nearly collapsed on the mean, and it is the first thing that will fail if these constants go up again.
  - **Why the poorest leagues still run deficits even though their clubs are the ones this pays**: only 4 of a 16-club league reach Europe, and the other 12 compete in a wage market the 4 have just been enriched in. That is the real-life dynamic (European money creates *domestic* inequality) and it means European prize money is not a lever for weak-league solvency, whatever its size. Don't reach for it as one.
  - **Measured pot (`continentalPrizeProbe.ts`, seed 7): Cup £527M + Shield £167M = ~£694M a season, against ~£211M before — 3.3x**, over 56 of 420 clubs. Per qualifier that is **14-16% of a big-four club's base income and 25-31% of Serbia's, Scotland's or Greece's**, monotonically increasing as the league gets poorer, which is exactly what paying flat means. Cup per-club: min (league-phase exit) £10.5M, median £14.5M, champion £45.5-47M. **Note this is well above the "roughly 2x" the change was scoped at** — the scoping arithmetic under-counted the 96 league-phase matches, which is the same trap this entry's third bullet warns about, made while writing this entry. Participation alone is £320M of the £527M, so trimming the per-result fees cannot get back to 2x; `CUP_PRIZE_PARTICIPATION` is the lever if it ever needs to.
  - **The obvious next idea is a coefficient-weighted "value pillar", and it was left out on purpose.** It is UEFA's biggest pillar and the game already computes country coefficients (`core/cup/coefficients.ts`), so it is tempting and cheap to build. It also closes a **feedback loop that file explicitly names as its failure mode to watch** — places won → money → strength → coefficient → places won. If it is ever added, gate it on `coefficientAudit.ts`'s churn metrics (`changes-per-dynasty`, `at floor`), not just on solvency.
  - **No persisted field and no migration** — these are read at sim time. The `touchStats` scoreline hash is untouched, for the documented reason: that baseline runs `simSeason` (`core/season.ts`), which contains **zero** cup references. Budgets do feed AI market decisions, so multi-season dynasties diverge from a pre-change baseline; single-season results do not.

**Continental Shield** (2026-08-19) — the second continental competition, the Europa League to the Cup's Champions League. **Structurally the Cup with different numbers**: everything that differs lives in `CUP_FORMATS` (`constants.ts`), keyed by `CupState.competition` (`"continental" | "shield"`) and read through `cupFormat(cup)`. State is `LeagueStore.shield`/`shieldHistory`, built and archived beside the Cup in `simOffseason`, advanced by the same `advanceCompetition` path in `simThrough`. Page is `/shield` — the *same* `Cup` component with a `competition` prop.
- **Field: strong leagues' 5th–6th, weak leagues' 3rd–4th = 16 clubs.** Where the Shield starts in a league is **derived**, by summing what every competition above it takes from *that same league* (`cupOffsetForCompetition` over `CONTINENTAL_ORDER`) — not a stored per-format offset, which would silently overlap or leave a gap the moment a league carried its own slot count (`Competition.continentalSlots`). It shipped as a `strongOffset`/`weakOffset` pair and this file described it that way for a while; the derivation replaced it. Since 2026-08-28 disjointness no longer rests on the offset at all — see the Cup's qualification bullet.
- **Both competitions share the Cup's matchdays** (LP 3/7/11/15/19/23, playoff 27, KO legs 29/31, 33/35, 37) — sound *only* because the fields are disjoint, and it's what the real midweek calendar does. No calendar work, and no fatigue interaction (cup matches already build their own per-matchday composites).
- **A 16-club field needed no change to `leaguePhase.ts`**: two pots of 8 against 3 games per pot ✓, and `splitLeaguePhase` lands exactly (4 direct QF / 8 playoff / 4 out).
- **Every cup rng stream is salted per competition** (`CupFormat.streamTag`, continental **30** = the literal it shipped with, shield 70; plus a separate `drawSeed`). Two cups in the same season on the same matchdays would otherwise draw *identical* streams round for round. Any third competition must take its own tag.
- **Each competition pools its own normalization baseline.** Pooling the two fields together would measure Shield clubs against Cup clubs, which is the comparison neither makes.
- **Prizes ≈ 40% of the Cup's** (`SHIELD_PRIZE_*`): champion ~£20M vs the Cup's ~£47M, front-loaded the same way (see **Continental prize money**). The relationship these were sized to hold — finishing 5th and winning the Shield must not out-earn finishing 4th and going out of the Cup's league phase — **was false as shipped and is only closer now**: the Shield champion took ~£21M against a Cup league-phase exit's £2M, because the exit paid almost nothing. Front-loading both narrows it to ~£20M vs ~£14M. Closing it outright would mean paying a Cup league-phase exit more than a Shield champion, which is its own call about how far apart the competitions should sit.
- **Held as its own `LeagueStore` field, not a list of cups** — `cup` has ~30 readers across sim and UI and a list turns every one into a lookup. Revisit only if a third competition arrives.
- **Deliberately NOT wired into the worldwide awards.** `worldAwards.ts` reads `ctx.cup` only, so Shield matches score zero there (exactly what already happens to every non-Cup player; their league seasons still count in full). The cup component is delicately balanced — `WORLD_AWARD_CUP_MULTIPLIER` is 1.0 for measured reasons and the team bonuses trade directly against winner's world-ovr rank — so adding an unmeasured second competition is the move those comments warn against. Needs a `scripts/worldAwardsAudit.ts` run before it goes in.
- It *is* wired into club history, the Frivolities trophy cabinet and both GOAT boards (`GOAT_SHIELD_TITLE_WEIGHT` 8 vs the Cup's 25; `GOAT_TEAM_SHIELD_TITLE_WEIGHT` 40 vs 150), a profile honour pill via `playerHonors.ts`'s `HonorCups.shieldHistory`, and `freeAgentCull` scrubs its box-score lines like the Cup's.
- **Merged with domestic cups (#244) on 2026-08-20; the two were built in parallel and touch the same dozen integration points.** They compose cleanly, and the two facts that make that true are worth keeping: the domestic tie stream is `0x0d0d` against the Shield's tag 70 (so no stream collides), and `DOMESTIC_CUP_MATCHDAYS` `[5,9,13,21,26,36]` is disjoint from the continental calendar (so a club can hold all three at once and never double-book). A club's trophy total now counts league + Cup + Shield + domestic + tier 2; **a treble is deliberately league + Cup + domestic and does NOT include the Shield** — the Shield is what you win instead of the Cup, so a "treble" containing it would be the wrong shape.
- Old saves backfill `shield: null` / `shieldHistory: []` and pick a Shield up at their next offseason (qualification needs a finished table), exactly as pre-cup saves did for the Cup. `scripts/shieldProbe.ts` verifies the whole thing end to end on a simmed world (field sizes, zero overlap, which places each drew from, distinct champions and scorelines).
- **Prize money is the Shield's ONLY channel into the world, and that was proved rather than argued.** Set every `SHIELD_PRIZE_*` to 0 and a 4-season dynasty comes out **bit-identical to `origin/main`** (same per-country mean OVR to 0.1, same rung gaps to 0.01, same min AI budget). So the Shield adds no rng-stream contamination, no fatigue, no availability effect — it plays its matches on salted streams and touches the league solely by crediting budgets. **Re-run that check after any change here**; it is cheap (two 2-minute runs) and it is the fastest way to catch a competition accidentally reaching into the league stream.
- **The ladder was audited against `origin/main` and shows no systematic effect** (`scripts/weakLeaguesAudit.ts`, 10 seasons × 4 seeds, the identical script run on both branches). Solvency holds on **8/8 runs**. The rung that looks alarming is Belgium→Turkey, and it is the documented coin-flip pair: shield `−2.04 / +0.51 / +0.12 / +2.04` against main `+0.51 / −1.07 / −0.66 / −0.10`, i.e. the **sign flips by seed on both branches** and the Shield's cross-seed mean (**+0.16**) is *better* than main's (**−0.33**). Block spreads likewise: BIG4→Turkey mean **5.10 vs 4.86**, BIG4→France **2.71 vs 2.10**, France→Turkey **2.39 vs 2.76** (all well clear of their gates). **Seed 1 alone reads as a gross inversion on the Shield branch and is noise** — the exact trap the audit's two-scale design (per-seed catches gross, mean-across-seeds catches systematic) exists to avoid, and a reminder that a 4-seed range is not a variance estimate. Caveat worth keeping: this is **10 seasons, not the documented 20**, so it is evidence of no *large* effect rather than a full dynasty clearance; `SEASONS=20 SEEDS=1,2,3,4` is the real gate if the prizes are ever raised.

**Domestic cups** (`core/domesticCup/`) — one open-draw knockout per country, both tiers, so a club can win the treble
- **One cup per country, contested by all 40 clubs** (tier 1 + tier 2), stored as `LeagueStore.domesticCups` (this season, one per country) and `domesticCupHistory` (archived at offseason rollover). Unlike the Continental Cup **nothing qualifies**, so cups run from **season 1** and `createLeagueState` builds them; the offseason archives the finished ones and builds next season's off the same `tablesByCompId` the Continental Cup uses. Reuses `CupTie` and `resolveCupTie` outright — a domestic tie and a European one are resolved by identical code.
- **Open draw, redrawn every round — so only the drawn rounds exist.** `DomesticCupState.rounds` grows one entry at a time: `advanceDomesticCup` records the played round and draws the next from `[...byes, ...winners]` (`drawPairings`, a Fisher-Yates shuffle, home = whoever comes out first). There is deliberately no bracket to render in advance, which is what makes it a domestic cup. Draw stream is `hashInts(season, compId, round, DRAW_STREAM)` — **no lid**, so a cup drawn at league creation (lid still 0) and one drawn in the offseason use the same rule; ties play on `hashInts(lid, season, compId, round, DOMESTIC_TIE_STREAM)`. Never the shared `rng`, so **league scorelines are untouched** (`touchStats` hash unchanged — that test runs `simSeason`, which has no cups at all).
- **The field isn't a power of two, and `prelimTieCount` is where that's handled.** 40 clubs → `totalRoundsFor` 6 and 8 preliminary ties: the **worst-ranked 16** play, the other 24 get byes, 32 remain. Ranking is `(tier played in LAST season, finishing position)`, so a relegated club outranks everyone already in tier 2 and a promoted one sits atop the tier-2 block — the only thing this ordering decides is who enters early. No tables at all (season 1, fresh import) → tid order, which is the tier-1 block then the tier-2 block. **An exact power of two must return 0 prelim ties** or a full opening round gets labelled "preliminary" (caught by a test).
- **Round identity is distance from the final, never the round index** — `domesticRoundName` and `domesticPrizeForRound` both key off `roundsFromFinal`, and `DOMESTIC_CUP_PRIZE_BY_ROUNDS_FROM_FINAL` is indexed `[0] = lifting it`. Same reason `cupRoundsFromFinal` exists for the Continental Cup: a cup with a preliminary round and one without must pay the same for a semi-final.
- **Composites pool the WHOLE country (both divisions), and this is load-bearing** — the identical lesson as `cupMatchData`. Composites z-normalize within a competition, so a tier-2 club measured against its own division reads as an average side and would meet a top-flight club as an equal. `simThrough` builds one `leagueMatchData` over all 40 clubs per cup matchday. Measured on a simmed season: **9 of 64 quarter-finalists were tier-2**, and all 8 champions were tier-1 — upsets happen without being cheap.
- **`DOMESTIC_CUP_MATCHDAYS` = [5, 9, 13, 21, 26, 36]**, every one clear of `CUP_LEAGUE_PHASE_MATCHDAYS`/`CUP_PLAYOFF_MATCHDAY`/`CUP_KO_LEG_MATCHDAYS` (a test asserts this), of deadline day, and of the finale. A smaller field takes the **last** n, so the final never moves. `simThrough` also **halts before the user's domestic final** (matchday 36), same as the Continental final on 37.
- **The cup paid NOTHING until 2026-08-31, and that was a measured decision — see the re-enablement entry below for what ships now, and read this one first for why the numbers are small.** It shipped with real prizes first (12M down to 0.25M per round, runner-up 3M, each ×`financeScaleFor` so a weak league could not out-earn a strong one). `scripts/weakLeaguesAudit.ts`, 4 seeds × 20 seasons, **against the same script run on the merge base**: the **ladder held** everywhere (per seed and on the 4-seed mean, so the financeScale scaling did its job) but **2 of 4 seeds went into deficit** — Belgium −£0.5M s20, Turkey −£1.4M s19 — where the baseline is solvent on all four. That is precisely the result that got the need-buy tightening rejected, and the documented failure shape: **the finance column fails first, not the ladder.** The audit cannot separate the two candidate mechanisms (a one-off prize converted into a permanent wage liability — both deficits land late — versus plain stream shift, since crediting a prize before the winter window moves every downstream AI market decision and the weak leagues already sit within ~£0.01M of zero at their thinnest). **At zero, `creditPrizes` is never reached, no budget is touched, and a dynasty is bit-identical to one with no domestic cups at all — verified, seeds 1 and 3 reproduce the baseline's 20-season numbers exactly, to every decimal.** That is what made shipping the competition provably safe, and it is no longer true: the prizes are live, so a dynasty is **not** bit-identical to one without domestic cups, and that bit-identity check is gone as a tool. `domesticCupIntegration.test.ts` pins the payout structure rather than the zero.
- **Archived like the Continental Cup** (`archiveDomesticCup` folds every round's box scores into `statLines` and drops them) — 39 ties per country per season × 8 countries is a lot of box scores to keep forever, and save size is what freezes the game. `cupStats.ts` was refactored so both cups share `aggregateBoxScores`/`lineFromBoxScores` rather than growing a second copy. `freeAgentCull` scrubs both shapes; `migrate.ts` backfills `[]` and deliberately **does not** build a cup mid-season (half its matchdays are already gone) — an existing save gets one at its next offseason.
- **The Ballon d'Or pays for winning it — as a TEAM bonus only (`WORLD_AWARD_DOMESTIC_CUP_BONUS` 0.25, pro-rated over `WORLD_AWARD_DOMESTIC_CUP_FULL_INVOLVEMENT` 4 ties).** Added because the award already paid `WORLD_AWARD_LEAGUE_TITLE_BONUS`, so a double-winner was credited for one trophy and not the other; 0.25 ≈ 3 striker goals against a title's ~10, the intended hierarchy (World Cup > Continental > league > domestic cup). **No domestic cup goals/assists/ratings enter the award, and that is the load-bearing part**: those ratings z-normalize within one country, so they carry exactly the weak-league bias `leagueStrengthOffsets` exists to remove, and no correction is available for them (the Continental Cup escapes this only because `cupMatchData` pools its whole field). A uniform squad bonus has no such bias — it can move the award *between* clubs but never lift a player above a better team-mate, the same property that made raising the other team bonuses safe. `BallonDOrEntry.domesticCup` is **optional** so every persisted pre-feature entry still typechecks and reads as 0; `WorldAwardContext.domesticCups` likewise, so `migrate.ts`'s backfill scores old seasons exactly as before rather than re-ranking them on a trophy nobody played for. Second-tier winners count (unlike a tier-2 league title, which `championTidByCompId` excludes). **Measured** (`worldAwardsAudit.ts`, 3 seeds × 12 seasons, against the same script with the term removed — world awards feed nothing in the sim, so both runs play identical dynasties and only the ranking can move): winner's median world-ovr rank **69 → 68**, ST share **69% → 69%**, league-title rate **72% → 72%**, Continental-win rate 28% → 25%, mean score split league 12.45 / cup 0.95 / title 0.55 / intl 0.55 / **dcup 0.04**. It is a **tie-breaker-scale** term by design and flipped one or two winners in 36 seasons; the guarded metrics held. The audit now reports a `dcup` column and a "won domestic cup" rate — note that rate reads 0 on any build without the term, since it keys off the optional field, so it is not a before/after comparison on its own.
- **Domestic cup prize money, re-enabled 2026-08-31 at ~1/5 of the rejected numbers, PLUS a glamour-tie payment that is the actual feature.** The table above records why these shipped at zero. Re-enabled because a real domestic cup *is* small money — the FA Cup's whole fund is ~£16M and winning it pays ~£3.9M against a league title's ~£60M merit payment — so the old £12M-a-round table was ~3.6x too generous *as football*, quite apart from bankrupting two seeds. A winner's run is now **~£3.3M** and a country's pot ~£11.7M before scaling.
  - **Scaled by country but FLAT ACROSS TIERS, which needed its own `domesticCupScaleFor`** rather than `financeScaleFor`. The country half is realistic (domestic broadcasters) and is the direction that protects the ladder. Dropping the tier half is the point: the real cups pay a fourth-tier club exactly what they pay a champion for the same round, this cup fields **every** division of a country, and taxing a lower-division run by `tierScale` (`DIVISION_2_BUDGET_SCALE ** (tier-1)`, so 0.36 at tier 3) deletes the only part worth modelling. Opposite call from the continental competitions, which pay flat because UEFA does.
  - **The prize table alone did NOT serve the feature's purpose, and only measurement showed it.** With prizes and no glamour payment, `domesticCupPrizeProbe.ts` read the median tier-2 club that earned anything at **0.3% of its season**, while the one or two who won their cup outright took **20-27%** — and that money is not the prize table at all, it is the **Continental Shield place** a cup win earns (`allocateContinentalPlaces`), collected the following season and made ~4x more valuable by the same day's continental reshape. So the competition was a **lottery**: one transformed club a year and ~100 others getting nothing. Do not read "a tier-2 club took 27%" as the prize money working.
  - **`DOMESTIC_CUP_GLAMOUR_TIE_BONUS` (£300k) is the fix**, paid to the **smaller club only** in any cross-tier tie, **win or lose**, and **multiplied by how many divisions separate them** (added flat, made per-gap on 2026-09-01 when the world gained a third division — a tier-3 club drawing the top flight is the competition's most romantic tie and must not price the same as a tier-2 club drawing the division immediately above). It models the one real mechanism by which lower-division clubs profit from a cup: gate receipts from drawing a giant, which are taken on the day and do not care about the result. Only the smaller side is paid because a cup gate is a rounding error to a top-flight club, so paying both would double the money entering the world to buy no gameplay — targeting it is what lets it be big enough to matter. Measured effect on the **three-division** world (`domesticCupPrizeProbe.ts`, seed 7, seasons 3-5): lower-division clubs earning anything **300-315**, median **0.9% of a season**, best cases a Scottish tier-2 club at **24.1%**, a French tier-3 club at **12.8%** and a German tier-3 club at **11.9%** — and a tier-2 club won its country's cup outright in every season sampled, taking the Shield place. **A gradient instead of a lottery, which is the half of the purpose that survives.**
  - **The pyramid does NOT pay downward at £300k, and losing that was the price of clearing the ladder gate — do not re-quote the £600k figure.** At **£600k** the money per club that earned anything ran **tier 1 £0.54M, tier 2 £0.60M, tier 3 £0.66M**: the third division genuinely out-earned the top flight, which was this feature's headline result. At the shipped **£300k** it reverses to **tier 1 £0.48-0.51M, tier 2 £0.38-0.41M, tier 3 £0.34-0.39M**, top flight highest in all three seasons sampled. The mechanism is a straight tug-of-war and is worth understanding before anyone retunes: the **prize table** rewards going deep, which top-flight clubs do, while the **glamour bonus** rewards being small, and halving the latter handed the balance back to the former. So the shipped state buys *breadth* (300-plus lower-division clubs earning something rather than one lottery winner) but not *tilt*. Recovering the tilt means either a larger glamour bonus — which is what broke `Turkey→Greece` — or a **smaller prize table at the same glamour**, which is the untested configuration and needs its own 4-seed audit.
  - **The structural caveat, which no tuning fixes:** this game's lower divisions earn **60%** and **36%** of their top flight (`tierScale`) where real football's third tier earns ~1%, so a realistic cup cheque can never mean to these clubs what it means to a real one. £300k is ~0.6% of an English tier-2 club's £52.8M season and ~0.9% of a tier-3 club's £31.7M. Going much further prices the cup above the league places beside it; the honest ceiling here is "funds a signing".
  - **Cost on the 626-club world: domestic £0 → ~£180M a season, 0.74% of world base income** — the denominator that matters, since against *prize* money alone it reads 21%, which overstates it badly. World base income is **£24.3bn** (`scripts/worldBaseIncomeProbe.ts`, which sums `BASE_SEASON_BUDGET` over every club scaled by country and tier); the continental pot beside it is ~£694M, i.e. **2.85%**. Measured on top of the continental reshape by `scripts/domesticCupPrizeProbe.ts`, which reports the **combined** total, since two probes each reporting half cannot answer what the audit reacts to.
  - **The first audit FAILED at £600k, and halving it is what fixed it — which is also the only evidence that magnitude is the lever (2026-09-02).** `weakLeaguesAudit.ts`, 20 seasons x 4 seeds, both sides re-measured on the 626-club main. At **£600k**: `Turkey→Greece INVERTED on the mean (-1.71 < -1)`, and **not as a re-roll** — the rung is negative on *all four* seeds (-2.80 / -1.50 / -1.56 / -0.96) where the baseline is mixed and positive on the mean (+0.25). That is the documented weaker-but-richer tripwire, tripped by this feature's own money. At **£300k** the same rung reads **-0.26** (+0.89 / -2.47 / -0.91 / +1.47), signs mixed again like the baseline. **Halving the money roughly halved the distortion — a dose-response, and the thing that actually identifies magnitude as the lever** rather than the assertion it was before this.
  - **Shipped verdict at £300k: every gate passes on the pooled four-seed mean, and every seed reads `ladder OK` on its own.** Converged rungs (mean, baseline → treatment): Portugal→Belgium +1.14 → **-0.52**, Belgium→Turkey +0.88 → **+0.95**, Turkey→Greece +0.25 → **-0.26**, Greece→Scotland -0.58 → **+0.89**, Scotland→Serbia +1.43 → **-0.36** — every one inside the ±1 tolerance, and moving in both directions rather than uniformly down, which is what the converged block's documented coin-flip looks like. Resolvable rungs stay positive on all four seeds: BIG4→France +3.95 → **+3.96**, France→Netherlands +1.76 → **+1.70**, Netherlands→Portugal +1.44 → **+3.44**. Block spreads narrow slightly and stay far clear of their gates: BIG4→Serbia +10.27 → **+9.81**, France→Serbia +6.31 → **+5.85**. **Pool the per-seed values by hand** — a one-seed invocation applies the mean gate to a single draw and seed 4 duly reports Belgium→Turkey "BROKEN" at -2.03, which is the documented misuse, not a verdict.
  - **Solvency is NOT a wash, and the honest reading is that it is slightly worse.** Both sides run **4 of 4 seeds in deficit** — main has been red on this gate since #308, so do not assume a green baseline — with comparable worst cases (baseline -£3.3 / -£9.7 / -£3.4 / -£7.9M, treatment -£12.4 / -£5.7 / -£0.7 / -£8.1M). What moves is *frequency*: **any club in deficit at 10 of 168 sampled points on main against 19 of 168 here**, on a similar worst-at-once count (1-3 clubs of 625 either way). This is the pre-existing weak-league wage ratchet being fed a little harder, not a new failure — and it did **not** respond to halving the money (v1 and v2 are indistinguishable on solvency where the ladder halved cleanly), which is exactly what marks it as a re-roll rather than dose-responsive.
  - **Two mechanisms were proposed for the £600k failure and NEITHER survived measurement — record this before proposing a third.** (a) *"The bracket is capped at 32 clubs, so a smaller country splits the same late-round pot among fewer clubs"*: **refuted.** Scale-adjusted domestic money per club is nearly flat across the world (£0.54-0.78M), and Greece vs Turkey is £0.63M vs £0.61M, a **3%** gap where the theory predicted ~33%. (Scotland's £0.78M *is* a real size effect — 32 clubs exactly, so no preliminary round and every tie pays — but Scotland is not the rung that broke.) (b) *"The late rounds concentrate on top-flight clubs, which is what the gate reads"*: **unproven.** Money per top-flight club does read Greece £0.54M against Turkey £0.40M, the right direction, but across all twelve it spans £0.39-0.55M **without tracking top-flight size** — the Netherlands has 18 clubs and the highest figure, Scotland has 12 and one of the lowest — and it is a single season of a single seed, so most of that spread is draw noise. `domesticCupPrizeProbe.ts` prints both tables.
  - **So magnitude is the only evidence-backed lever and the mechanism is still unknown.** The honest experiment nobody has run is glamour-bonus-only against prize-table-only, two audits; until someone does, treat £300k as *calibrated* rather than *understood*, and re-measure both sides before raising it.
- **Treble** = tier-1 title + Continental Cup + domestic cup in one season, derived in `clubHistory.ts` (`ClubSeasonRecord.treble`, `ClubHistory.trebles`). Domestic titles also feed the Frivolities trophy cabinet, both GOAT boards (`GOAT_DOMESTIC_CUP_TITLE_WEIGHT` 6 / `GOAT_TEAM_DOMESTIC_CUP_TITLE_WEIGHT` 40 — deliberately the cheapest trophy: six games can win it), and **`playerHonors.ts` now credits cup wins at all**, which it never did — `computePlayerHonors`/`computeArchivedHonors` take an optional `HonorCups` and return `continentalCups`/`domesticCups` alongside `leagueTitles`, so the Continental Cup gained a profile pill in the same change.
- UI: `/domestic-cup` (country + season dropdowns, rounds as columns), a Domestic Cup stats tab on Player Profile, Schedule rows (`kind: "domestic"`), a Dashboard final warning, and the sim ticker — which needed `MatchdayProgress` to gain a `domesticTies: DomesticTieResult[]` argument (through the worker protocol), because a `CupTie` carries only a round *index* and the ticker had "Continental Cup" hardcoded.

**Super cups** (`core/superCup/`, "champions cups" to the player, 2026-08-31) — one match per country plus one worldwide, played in the **preseason**
- **The whole competition is one `resolveCupTie` call per tie.** A domestic super cup is its country's league champion against its domestic cup winner; the continental one is the Continental Cup winner against the Shield winner. Single match, **neutral venue** (`resolveCupTie` already took the flag for the promotion playoff's final), extra time then penalties. `LeagueStore.superCups` holds this preseason's; `SeasonHistoryEntry.superCups` archives them at the next rollover. Both new, both migrated to `[]`/absent, and **never backfilled** — a season that opened before the competition existed played none, so absent is the truth rather than a gap.
- **Seeded at the offseason roll, played at the start of the next season, and that split is the point.** `buildSuperCups` runs beside `buildCupState` because that is the last moment all four winners are readable at once (the cups carrying them are archived a few lines later); `playSuperCups` runs in the new season, so the match is contested by the squads that will actually start it — which is what a real Super Cup does. Consequence worth knowing: **the summer window is open either side of it**, so a user who signs someone first fields him. Eager and lazy play are the same *match* (the seed is `hashInts(lid, season, compId, SUPER_CUP_STREAM)`, not a function of when it is played) but not necessarily the same *personnel*, so nothing downstream may assume a super cup was played on any particular squad.
- **The preseason gate is DERIVED (`superCupsPending`), not a third `phase` value** — nothing to migrate, and nothing to read in the ~dozen places that switch on `phase`, for a state that lasts one click. **And it is not actually a gate:** `simThrough` plays anything still pending on its way into the season rather than refusing to run. That is deliberate and it is the bug this nearly shipped with — a hard block stalls every caller that never sees the UI (autopilot's `jumpSeasons`, the headless audits, the test harnesses) with no way to clear itself. Same eager-or-lazy arrangement `playPromotionPlayoffs` uses, for the same reason. **A spectator save is the clearest case**: `SpectatorDashboard` renders none of this (there is no club to prepare) and sims straight through `simAction`, so a real gate would have deadlocked those saves at the start of every season with no button anywhere to clear it.
- **The double rule: league champion vs cup winner, and the LEAGUE RUNNER-UP steps in when one club won both** (user call). What the Community Shield does, it always produces a match, and the opponent is a genuinely strong side. The other real convention — the beaten cup finalist — was passed over because a **tier-2 club can reach a domestic cup final here**, which would put a second-division side in the showpiece far more often than reads right. The continental one needs no such rule: the two fields are disjoint by construction (see `cup/qualification`), so the same club can never hold both.
- **A two-club normalization pool would be degenerate, and this is the `cupMatchData` lesson again.** Composites z-normalize within their pool, so measuring the two contestants against each other alone makes every match read as even however far apart the sides are. A domestic super cup therefore pools its **country's clubs across every one of its divisions** — it has to, since the domestic cup is open to all of them and its winner can be a second- or third-tier club — and the continental one pools **every tier-1 club in the world**, the only baseline that means anything between two elite clubs from different countries. The pool is keyed off `country`, never an enumerated pair of tiers, which is why the third division added on 2026-09-01 needed no change here.
- **No prize money, and that is what makes it provably safe to ship.** Same call the domestic cups shipped under and for the same measured reason (real payouts there put 2 of 4 audit seeds into deficit while every strength check stayed green). With no credit, no box score kept, no injury or card carried out of the match and a dedicated rng stream, a season played with super cups is **bit-identical** to one played without — asserted directly by `superCup.test.ts` ("does not move a single league result"), which also checks the match really was played so it cannot pass by doing nothing. **That test is the gate for any future change here**: a prize credit would shift every downstream AI market decision and nothing else in the suite would notice.
- **Filed under the season it OPENS, which is the one place it differs from every other record on a `SeasonHistoryEntry`** — everything else there describes the football that closed the season. Same for the News Feed, where a super cup is the one trophy sorted at `PRESEASON_ORDER` rather than season-end, beside the summer window rather than below the champions.
- `ClubHistory.superCupTitles` reads the super-cup records directly rather than a `ClubSeasonRecord`, because a super cup belongs to no season's *table*; **a season can legitimately appear twice** there (a club that is both league champion and Continental Cup holder contests both), so it is a list rather than a set and deduplicating would undercount the cabinet. Deliberately **not** wired into the Ballon d'Or, the GOAT boards or the treble — the treble is league + Continental Cup + domestic cup and a fourth trophy would be the wrong shape, and the world-awards balance is delicate enough that adding an unmeasured term is the move those comments warn against. `COUNTRY_CUP_ADJECTIVE` gained the four countries added on 2026-08-28 (Dutch/Greek/Scottish/Serbian), which also fixes the domestic cups reading "Netherlands Cup".
- UI: `/champions-cups` (season dropdown, a card per tie), a Dashboard preseason panel offering the match ahead of the sim buttons, and a trophy row on Club History shown only once a club has one.

**International football** (`core/international/`) — national teams, played entirely inside the offseason
- **Four-year cycle. Watch-only unless the user manages one of the nations** (see "National team management" above, which is layered on top of everything in this section rather than changing any of it) (`INTL_CYCLE_YEARS`=4, `qualifyingLeg`/`isTournamentSeason`). Keyed off `season % 4`: seasons 1/2/3 of a cycle each play one qualifying leg, season 4 (`% 4 == 0`) plays the `INTL_FIELD_SIZE` (**32**) team `World Cup` (8 groups of 4 → `INTL_KO_SIZE` (16) bracket, R16→QF→SF→Final reusing the Continental Cup's `seedOrder`/`resolveCupTie`). **Qualifying is ONE campaign drawn once (at the cycle's first qualifying offseason, `qualifyingLeg==0`) and played a leg at a time across the three offseasons** — the campaign persists on `LeagueStore.international.qualifying`, accumulating results, and its 32 qualifiers are computed (`computeQualified`) only when the third leg completes; the tournament offseason then draws the World Cup from them. Each leg is seeded independently off the campaign's start season + leg index (`playQualifyingRound`), so leg-by-leg play is deterministic. Squad pids are filtered to living players each leg (a 3-season campaign outlives some players named at draw — `leagueMatchData` would deref a retired pid otherwise). Every nation but the user's auto-picks (`selectSquad`: best 23, ≤3 GKs, fit players before injured; `chooseBestFormation`). `LeagueStore.international` (`{qualifying, tournament, history, qualifyingHistory, powerRankings, stage, stageInjuries}`).
- **The field is 32, and the binding constraint is nationality depth, not club count (2026-08-23, user call).** Raised from 16 once the world reached 16 competitions. Measured on a fresh 320-club world, stable across 3 seeds: 78 nationalities present, **44** clear `INTL_MIN_POOL`, and squad rating runs ~76 at #1, ~70 at #16, **~64 at #32**, then falls off a cliff to ~56 by #40 — so 32 is near the largest field this world fills with nations worth watching, and 40 would be padded. **The accepted cost is that qualifying means much less:** at 16 places, 28 of the 44 could miss out; at 32, only **12** can, and all of them are European or African. A confederation with no more nations than places plays *no qualifying matches at all* (`initQualifying` skips it), so South America (4 eligible), Asia (1) and North America (1) now qualify their whole membership — Asia and NA already did at 16. **Do not try to widen the field by lowering `INTL_MIN_POOL`**: dropping it to 15 adds ~5 nations (Mexico 16, Gambia 16, Kenya 15, Albania 15) that all rate below 56. The only real fix for the qualifying stakes is more league countries feeding deeper nationality pools.
- **`seedBracket` splits the draw into halves, and that was a real bug fix, not tidying (2026-08-23).** It used to emit each partner pair's two ties *adjacently* (`W(a) v R(b)`, `W(b) v R(a)`, then the next pair), which puts them together in the **next** round — so a group's two qualifiers met in the semi-final of a four-group tournament, and would have met in the quarter-final of an eight-group one, contradicting the function's own docstring. Mirror ties now go into a second accumulator appended after the first, which is also how a real bracket is laid out. **It changes results for existing four-group tournaments too** (the World Cup's old shape and the confederation cups' largest) — harmless because international matches take no draw from the shared `rng`, so no club result moves. Pinned by a test that asserts group-mates land in opposite halves at 2/4/8 groups.
- **The knockout is ONE repeating `"knockout"` stage, not one stage per round.** `IntlStage`'s `"qf" | "sf" | "final"` collapsed into it when the bracket gained a round, because bracket depth is a function of the field (4 rounds at 32, 3 at 16, and a small confederation cup's can be a single final) and hard-coding it means a new stage every time the field moves. `roundsRemaining` (`tournament.ts`, shared with the confederation cups for the same reason) reads the depth off the fixtures, and that is what names the round in the Dashboard button/headline and in Schedule's round picker — **never the stage name and never `KO_ROUND_NAMES` indexed directly**, or an old save's quarter-finals get labelled "Round of 16". `migrate.ts`'s `migrateIntlStage` maps the three old values onto it; an in-progress bracket therefore finishes at whatever size it was drawn.

- **Played in stages the user clicks through, *before* the offseason advances (`core/international/staging.ts`).** The campaign is *drawn* the instant the club season ends (in `simThrough`, at the offseason transition — `initInternationalCampaign` names squads + draws groups/bracket, fixtures unplayed) and then *played* one stage at a time (`playIntlStage`): a qualifying offseason plays this cycle's one qualifying leg; a tournament offseason is group stage → QF → SF → Final. (A qualifying campaign is drawn only in the cycle's *first* qualifying offseason; the next two resume the in-progress campaign for their leg — see the four-year-cycle bullet above.) `InternationalState.stage` (`"qualifying"|"groups"|"qf"|"sf"|"final"|"done"|null`) tracks progress; the Dashboard's "Advance" button is replaced by the stage buttons (`isIntlStagePending`) until it reaches `"done"`. `simThroughInternational` plays every remaining stage at once (the "sim through" button, and the headless-test path). **The gate is presentational, not structural** — `simOffseason` *opens* by calling `simThroughInternational`, so it always completes a campaign the user left unplayed. That's what the Dashboard's **"Skip qualifying"/"Skip the World Cup"** button relies on: it just routes to `/set-scouting` (the normal advance), plays nothing itself, and lands identical results because the stages own their seeds (equivalence test in `international.test.ts`). **Each stage owns its seed** (`GROUP_STREAM`, `KNOCKOUT_STREAM + round`), so click-through == one-pass == old bulk result — `runQualifying`/`runTournament` are thin composers over the very same stage functions (equivalence test in `international.test.ts`). Init is player-free and shared-rng-free; every match runs on its own `hashInts` stream (`QUALIFYING/GROUP/KNOCKOUT/DRAW_STREAM` tags), **never the shared `rng`**, so club results stay bit-identical (`touchStats` hash + `simThrough` gate unchanged).
- **Now runs entirely *before* `simOffseason`, so retirement falls *after* the tournament** — a player about to retire gets one last campaign, and (as before) one injured in May still misses it, because squads read end-of-season ratings/injuries. Caps/goals/assists are credited per stage (additive `CareerDelta`); the one-off `tournaments`/`titles` counters and the `history` summary land at the Final. `simOffseason`'s old step 1.7 is gone.
- **A nation = a synthetic club.** `nationMatchData` builds `LeagueTeam`s keyed by `nid` (index into the campaign's `squads`, stands in for `tid`) and pools the whole field into **one** `leagueMatchData` normalization baseline — mandatory, exactly like `cupMatchData`, or z-norm flattens every nation to parity. `simMatchDetailed` runs unmodified.
- **Eligibility + fieldability.** A nation enters if pool ≥ `INTL_MIN_POOL` (18) with ≥ `INTL_MIN_KEEPERS` (1) GK **and** has a confederation (`confederationOf`). The GK floor is a *correctness* requirement: `selectXI` fills a GK slot with an outfielder when none exists, corrupting the keeping composite. **44 of the 78 nationalities present clear the bar on a fresh 320-club world** — measured, identical on 3 seeds (`scripts/intlFieldProbe.ts`).
- **Confederations + slot allocation** (`confederations.ts`) — `CONFEDERATION_OF` maps every name-pool nation to one of 6 confederations (test-guarded: `international.test.ts` asserts no nation is missing). `allocateSlots` distributes the 16 places by largest-remainder **weighted by each confederation's count of *contender* nations** (the global strongest 16), floor 1, capped at nation count. Weighting by raw nation *count* was measurably wrong — Africa's 8 nations matched Europe's 24 for places while Europe held every strong side, filling the field with makeweights.
- **Qualifying is a three-leg round-robin** (`INTL_QUAL_LEGS`=3, `roundRobin(nids, legs)`), **one leg per qualifying offseason** — each fixture is tagged with its `leg` (0/1/2) at draw time (`IntlGroupMatch.leg`, optional for legacy saves → treated as 0), and `playQualifyingRound` plays the next unplayed leg via `playGroups(..., onlyLeg)`. A single round-robin left strong nations missing out on variance; three legs across three offseasons make a long, low-variance campaign that reads like real World Cup qualifying. Tournament groups stay single-leg (variance is the point). Serpentine group draw for qualifying (`serpentineGroups`), pot draw for the tournament (`potDraw`). `fillPlaces` walks group finishing positions (winners, then best runners-up via per-game-rate `rankAcrossGroups` since group sizes differ) so a confederation always fills its allocation.
- **Career record + injury carry-over; no rating/finance impact.** `Player.intl` (`IntlCareer`: caps/goals/assists/tournaments/titles, in `international/career.ts` to avoid an import cycle with `players/types.ts`) accumulated at sim time (`CareerDelta`), surfaced on Player Profile. **Nothing feeds progression or valuation** — cannot disturb the rating equilibrium. **Injuries do carry into the new club season** (`stageInjuries`, `collectInjured`, `carryIntlInjuries`): a knock in an international match sidelines the player for the opening weeks of the club season. Collected *post-hoc* from box-score injury events (never stamped mid-campaign → international match XIs/results are unchanged), held on `InternationalState.stageInjuries`, applied in `simOffseason` (step 3.05) **after** progression heals the club-season knocks, rolled on a dedicated seeded stream (`INTL_INJURY_STREAM`, never the shared `rng`). The rolled duration is reduced by `INTL_INJURY_OFFSEASON_RECOVERY` (2) — the summer break serves part of the recovery, so a short knock heals before kickoff and only a more serious injury carries, at a shortened spell. Note this is the *one* intended club-side effect: it changes club availability the following season, so multi-season league results after an international campaign are no longer bit-identical to a pre-feature baseline (single-season `touchStats` hash is untouched — `simSeason` never crosses an offseason). **Fatigue and value/hype bumps remain deferred** (would touch the equilibrium; need their own audit). Migrated: new arrays/`stageInjuries` backfill to empty, `Player.intl` absent = never capped.
- **"Light" archival + the National Teams section.** Only the *current* qualifying campaign and tournament are held in full; past editions collapse to compact self-contained summaries — **results, not box scores.** `IntlTournamentSummary` now carries final group tables (`IntlGroupTable`, nation-keyed via `groupTableSummary`) + every knockout scoreline (`IntlKnockoutResult`); `IntlQualifyingSummary` carries the qualifying group tables + qualifiers; both drop per-match box scores and squad lists (career totals on `Player.intl` are the lasting per-player record). `InternationalState` gains `qualifyingHistory`, `powerRankings` (an `IntlPowerSnapshot` — every eligible nation ranked by squad rating, taken each time a campaign is drawn in `initInternationalCampaign`), plus the existing tournament `history`. Archival happens at sim time: qualifying summary appended when qualifying finishes, tournament summary at the Final. Per-nation records (titles/finals/best-finish) are **derived**, not stored (`nationHistory.ts` `nationRecords`/`finishOf`). Old saves backfill the new arrays to empty and fill going forward (migrate.ts). UI: the **`/national-teams/*`** section (sidebar group, ordered *after* Team; `ui/pages/nationalTeams/`) — World Cup, Qualifying, **Rosters**, Schedule, Power Rankings, Stat Leaders (national teams + players filterable by country, off `Player.intl`), History. Navigation is sidebar-only (the pages carry no tab strip of their own). The old single `/international` page is gone (redirects to `/national-teams/world-cup`).
- **Rosters tab + per-campaign player record.** `Rosters.tsx` renders the live campaign's `NationSquad[]` — nation list sorted by squad rating, selected squad sorted by `sortByPosThenOvr` with the projected XI (`selectXI` off the squad's own formation) shaded. Squad pids missing from `league.players` are dropped, matching what `playQualifyingRound` feeds the sim (a 3-offseason campaign outlives some named players). **`IntlCareer.seasons`** (`IntlSeasonLine[]`, `career.ts`) breaks caps/goals/assists out per offseason played, `kind: "qualifying" | "tournament"` — surfaced as the Player Profile's third stats tab. Written by `applyCareerDelta`, which therefore takes `season`/`kind`; `season` is **threaded in from the caller** (`playIntlStage`/`simThroughInternational` gained a param) rather than read off the campaign, because a qualifying campaign's own `season` is the cycle's *start*, not the offseason being played. The squads-only call at the Final passes an empty delta and must not stamp empty rows — `mergeSeasonLine` only runs for players with a delta entry. Old saves backfill `seasons: []` (irreconstructable — archived campaigns hold no box scores); career totals stay the authoritative record. "Which campaign is current" is one shared helper (`liveCampaign` in `nationalTeams/shared.tsx`), used by both Rosters and Schedule: pending stage first, else **the more recent of tournament/qualifying by `season`** — "tournament if there is one" was stale, since a newly drawn cycle's qualifying outranks the previous World Cup. **Render volume is the qualifying schedule's gotcha:** a campaign is ~280 fixtures (39 nations, 7 groups, `INTL_QUAL_LEGS` legs), so Schedule filters to one round at a time by default (`currentLegOf` mirrors `playQualifyingRound`'s next-leg rule; "All rounds" opts back in), and `NationName` passes `tip={false}` to `Flag` — the tooltip only repeats the adjacent name and costs an absolutely-positioned shadowed span per flag (1674 → 186 flag nodes on that page).

**Confederation cups** (`core/international/confederationCup.ts`, `format.ts`) — the Euro / Copa América / AFCON, played inside the offseason alongside a World Cup qualifying leg
- **Cadence is additive, not a restructure.** They run in the offseason of the cycle's *middle* qualifying season (`isConfederationCupSeason`, `CONFEDERATION_CUP_QUALIFYING_LEG` = 1, i.e. `season % 4 == 2`), which is the real Euro/Copa offset from a World Cup. That offseason plays its qualifying leg **and then** the championships. Replacing the leg instead was rejected: it would drop `INTL_QUAL_LEGS` 3 → 2 and reopen exactly the qualifying variance those three legs exist to damp.
- **No confederation cup qualifying.** A field is the confederation's strongest nations at draw time (`buildSquads` is already sorted). A second qualifying campaign per confederation would multiply the ~280-fixture campaign qualifying already runs.
- **The format scales with the field, and it has to** (`format.ts` `TOURNAMENT_FORMATS`/`formatFor`): 16 → 4 groups of 4, 12 → 4 of 3, 8 → 2 of 4, 6 → 2 of 3, ≤5 → **one round-robin whose top two contest a final** (which is how Copa América was really played until 1975, so it reads as a format rather than a fudge). Every shape ends in a power-of-two knockout. `seedBracket` therefore takes its group count from `groups.length` and a `qualifyPerGroup` param instead of `INTL_GROUPS`/`INTL_QUALIFY_PER_GROUP`; a single-group tournament returns `[winner, runnerUp]` since there is no partner group to cross with. World Cup output is byte-identical (defaults preserved).
- **Measured world support is the reason three of six ship dark.** Every player is generated from one of the eight European leagues' nationality tables, so non-European nations exist only as imports. On a fresh world (seed 1): Europe **25** eligible nations, Africa **12**, South America **5**, North America **1**, Asia **1**, Oceania **0** (`INTL_MIN_POOL` 18 + ≥1 GK). So the Euro, AFCON and a small Copa are held; the Asian Cup, Gold Cup and OFC Nations Cup are defined, gated on `CONTINENTAL_MIN_NATIONS` (4), and light up on their own if a world ever supports them. **Don't "fix" this by lowering `INTL_MIN_POOL`** — that changes who enters World Cup qualifying, hence the confederation allocation, hence existing saves' results; and a pool under ~14 can't field 11 fit players, which `selectXI` does not guard.
